- **`prevent-secret-writes` now sees writes behind every command runner core understands, and bare `shred`, `unlink`, `ln` and `touch`.** `writer_targets` peeled a private five-word wrapper list, so `env cp x .env`, `env -i cp x .env`, `nice -n 5 cp x .env`, `timeout 5 rm .env`, `sudo -u root cp x .env` and `stdbuf -oL tee .env` were allowed while bash wrote or deleted `.env`. It now runs core's shared `peel_command_runners` (the peel `prevent-secret-leaks` and `enforce-worktree` use), plus a detector-only skip for `command -p`, `exec -a NAME` and `time -p`. `shred`/`unlink` join `rm`'s every-operand arm, `ln` joins `cp`'s destination arm, and `touch` blocks each operand it would create (`-r`/`-d`/`-t` values excepted). Still allowed: the same commands on a non-secret target, `ln -s .env link`, `touch -r .env other`, safe templates, and `env -S '…'` (tracked in #1135). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#816)

- **The plan-store reader can no longer be steered out of `<config_dir>/plans` by a concurrent swap, a symlink, or a hardlink.** `persist-plan-approval` and `lint-plan-shape` read the plan named by `planFilePath` only when it sits under the store. The reader used to canonicalize and nesting-check that path and then open it, so a directory inside the store swapped for a symlink between the two was followed at open time (a review reproduced 834 outside reads in 200k attempts). On Unix it now canonicalizes only the store root and walks the path below it with `openat`, opening every directory and the final name `O_NOFOLLOW`, so any symlink below the root, including a directory symlink that stays inside the store, reads as no plan. The opened file must also be a regular file with exactly one link, which rejects a hardlink in the store to a file outside it, and the final open is non-blocking so a FIFO cannot stall the hook. Other platforms keep a best-effort check that compares the opened file's length and timestamps with the path's, and have no link-count check. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#763)

### Changed

- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip`'s GraphQL field mapping is now pinned in raw response JSON.** The one-request lookup from cadence-hooks#1024 reads bot logins without REST's `[bot]` suffix and the reviewed commit from `commit.oid`; the existing fixtures reached that shape only through a REST-to-GraphQL conversion in the test double. A table test now feeds the GraphQL shape directly: a bot author's self-approval still nudges, another bot's or a human's approval on head stays silent, and an approval on a stale `commit.oid` nudges. Test-only; no verdict changes. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#985)
- **The polish gate asks for the security family when a record says the arm ran but not who ran it.** A marker with `security=ran` and no `--arm-model` attestation, on a branch whose diff touches code, now draws a nudge naming the re-record command (`--arm security=ran --arm-model security=opus`). It was silent through the attestation rollout; the polish skill has emitted `--arm-model` since 2026-08-26 and markers expire after 30 days, so an unattested record is now a hand record or an older tool. It ranks below every other polish nudge, and a docs-only branch stays silent. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#785)

### Fixed

- **Every guard now sees the command a script-running utility runs.** `env -S`/`--split-string`, a here-string fed to a shell (`bash <<< '…'`), `script -c` (and BSD's `script FILE CMD`), `flock FILE CMD` and `flock FILE -c CMD`, `watch CMD`, `tmux` (`new-session`/`new-window`/`split-window`/`run-shell`/`if-shell`/`display-popup`/`pipe-pane`/`respawn-*`, the global `-c`, and the text `send-keys` types into a pane), and `su`/`runuser -c` (plus `runuser -u USER CMD`) are surfaced through core's shared child-script path, with backslash removal, in both quoted and escaped spellings. Before, `env -S 'cat .env'` and `watch 'git push --force origin main'` reached no guard, and `su -c` reached only `prevent-secret-leaks`' own scan. `ssh HOST CMD` is deliberately not surfaced: its command runs on another machine. A substitution inside a wrapper's argument (`watch "$(watch "$(…)")"`, and the existing `bash -c "$(…)"`) is now expanded once rather than again by the wrapper's script at every nesting level, which had doubled the work per level. The script's copy is identified by source — the parent tags each substitution it expands at its own position before the script is read, so a textually identical substitution the child runs after its own `cd` (`bash -c 'cd x && echo $(git push …)' "$(git push …)"`) is always expanded — and a script that is only such a substitution is emitted as one segment rather than split and re-read (`child_scripts` leaves it out for walkers). enforce-worktree, `warn-plan-ready-flip` and the polish gate also stop re-expanding the same command, so 200 KB nested-wrapper inputs stay under 0.5 s in every guard. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1144)
- **`gh pr new` is read as `gh pr create`, and every other gh alias as its verb.** `gh_canonical_verb` maps gh's cobra aliases (`new` for `create` under `pr`/`issue`/`repo`/`gist`/`release`, `remove` for `secret`/`variable delete`, `co` for `pr checkout`, `ls` for `list`) wherever core reads a gh verb, so the polish gate treats `gh pr new` as a ship and `guard-gh-write` blocks `gh pr new -R evil/x`, `gh repo new evil/y` and `gh secret remove X -R evil/x`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#996)
- **`nudge-polish-before-pr` and `log-polish-nudge` no longer spawn git once per distinct ship.** Inside a checkout, a 200 KB flood of distinct targets (`gh pr ready 1 -R xN/yN; …`, or `(cd dN && gh pr merge -R own/repo); …`) took about 3 s against the hook deadline. Both hooks now go through `markers::resolve_located_ships`, which resolves each distinct (directory, target) pair once, reads checkout state once per directory and `git remote -v` / `git worktree list` once per checkout, and judges at most `MAX_JUDGED_SHIPS` (32) distinct pairs. Past the cap the rest are not judged and one cannot-check advisory stands in for them, so a capped command always nudges and never goes silent. Verdicts under the cap are unchanged; the ledger still records the command-order first anchor. Both floods now finish in about 0.25 s in release. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1152)

- **`warn-plan-ready-flip` is linear again: any 200 KB Bash command took 2.8 s, past the hook deadline.** `windowed_flips` re-scanned every earlier token for a `GH_REPO=` retarget at each index before checking for `gh pr`, and copied the whole tail for every hit; a flood of flips aimed at another repo also spawned git once per flip, 3.2 s in a checkout with a plan. The retarget position is now found once, the first window keeps its full tail and later ones end where the next begins, identical flips are judged once, and past 32 distinct flips the guard nudges without judging the rest. Every flood measured stays under 0.15 s release. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1150)

- **A comment on every continued line no longer makes the continuation join quadratic.** `take_logical_line` found and joined the whole remaining backslash-newline run before asking where the current logical line ended, so a 200 KB run of `echo a #;\` lines (each its own logical line, since bash does not continue a comment) cost O(rest of input) per line: 2.0 s in `guard-gh-dangerous` in release, past the hook deadline that fails open. The run is now discovered and joined lazily, only as far as the comment search asks, and the same flood takes 0.05 s; every logical line still ends exactly where the rescanning reference ends it. The bare `a\` flood from the issue already ran in 0.03 s on main. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1144)
- **The shell tokenizer keeps an unquoted `$(…)` or backtick substitution in the word it sits in.** `rm $(realpath .)/.env` and `echo x > $(mktemp -d)/.env` were split at the substitution's inner space, so guards judged `.)/.env` and `$(mktemp` instead of the word bash builds. A balanced span on one line (depth-bounded and linear; unbalanced, multi-line, or comment-bearing spans keep the old reading) is now one piece of its word, and redirect targets read it whole too. Three consumers that read words inside the old split keep that coverage: `trash-guard` also reads a substitution-bearing word's inner words, so `rm -rf $(echo /vault/x)` (and the double-quoted spelling it never saw) stays blocked; `guard-gh-write` no longer drops a `-R $(…)` value as whitespace-bearing prose and falls back to the cwd's repo (the quoted `-R "$(…)"` had the same hole); and `guard-push-remote` judges a substitution-bearing destination on its text up to the first blank, so the URL in `$(echo <owned-url> | sed …)` is not taken as the target. Every substitution outside quotes (read from the raw text, so `""$(…)` counts and `"$(…)"` does not) is still judged as the words of its source, which is the old split's reading: `prevent-secret-leaks` re-reads each one in place wherever it sits (the command word, behind `sudo -u root`/`timeout 5`/`nice -n 5`, after `if`/`!`/`do`, or as an `eval` or `sh -c` script), so `$(echo cat .env)` and its wrapped forms keep blocking and a metadata-safe head such as `echo $(…)` keeps its exemption, and `prevent-secret-writes` reads a redirect such a substitution prints, so `bash -c "$(echo echo x \> .env)"` now blocks where it never did. `x $(echo cat .env)`, whose output is only `x`'s arguments, blocks as it did before, an accepted over-block. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1106)
- **The shell tokenizer splits words only on bash's blanks: space, tab and newline.** Vertical tab, form feed, carriage return, NBSP and other Unicode spaces are word characters to bash, so `jq --rawfile a<VT>b .env.local` is one word there and was two here; guards reasoning about argument positions could read an argv bash never builds. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1055)
- **Joining backslash-newline continuations no longer rescans the line per join.** `take_logical_line` rescanned the whole growing line for comments after every join, so 8,000 continuations took 1.6 s in release, past the hook deadline that fails open. The shared heredoc reader's join (galloping to the first comment-bearing prefix) now carries this, pinned by a 40,000-continuation timing test and an equivalence test against the old rescanning join. The Unicode-whitespace half is the tokenizer fix above. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1084)
- **The segmenter keeps an fd duplication whole.** `split_segments` cut `2>&1`, `>&2`, `<&-` and `>& file` at the `&`, handing guards `… 2>` and a lone `1` as a command; `>&`/`<&` is one operator to bash, so the `&` now stays in its segment in every mode (`&>` stays opt-in). `gh pr merge 2>&1 12` is now seen naming a PR. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#848)
- **`prevent-secret-writes` catches `>& .env` and `>&.env`.** Bash writes both streams to a file when the word after `>&` is not a descriptor number or `-`; the redirect parsers read every `>&` as a duplication and recorded no target. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#849)
- **Redirect targets end where bash ends them: at an unquoted `)`, not before a glued `}`.** `clobber_redirect_targets` trimmed a trailing `)`/`}` unconditionally, so `> note}` was judged as `note`; a glued `}` is part of the name bash writes, while an unquoted `)` is a metacharacter that ends it (`{ (echo hi > .env)}` still yields `.env`). An escaped `\)` or `\;` no longer ends the name either. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1092)
- **Every heredoc reader in `core::shell` shares one quote model, so a fake heredoc no longer drops real commands.** `heredoc_introducers` tracked quoting with two private toggles, so the escaped quote in `echo "a\"b <<EOF"` closed the string, the `<<EOF` behind it registered as a heredoc bash never opens, and `strip_heredoc_bodies` dropped every line up to a matching `EOF` from every segmenting guard while bash ran them. It now reads a command line with the shared quote scanner, skips a `"$( … )"` span whole as the splitter does, and recognises comments by the splitter's rule; a `<<` inside `$(( … ))` or `${ … }` is no longer read as a heredoc, and one inside a multi-line quoted string is no longer read as one either. Both heredoc readers now end a body by one terminator model measured against bash 5.2: the line must equal the delimiter exactly (after tab-stripping for `<<-`), so `  EOF`, `EOF ` and `EOF\r` are body text where the top-level reader used to `trim()` them into terminators and read the rest as code. A body line `EOF\r` followed by the payload now reads as body data, as bash reads it; that is the one input class this change allows that was blocked before. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#813)
- **A heredoc delimiter is parsed once, the way bash parses it.** The top-level reader stopped a quoted delimiter at its first matching quote, so `<<"E\"F"` ended at `E\` there but at `E"F` in the substitution scanner and in bash; the body was never found, its `"` opened a phantom quote, and the command after the substitution reached no guard. Both readers now share one parser, which also decodes `$'…'` (`<<$'E'` ends at `E`), reads `$"…"`, keeps a backslash inside `"…"` that escapes nothing (`<<"E\xF"`), joins quoted parts (`<<E'F'G`), removes a backslash-newline inside the operator or the word, and accepts the empty delimiter `<<''`. A command line carrying a heredoc inside a `"$( … )"` or `` "`…`" `` span is no longer continuation-joined inside that span: joining rewrote `x\` ⏎ `EOF` into `xEOF` inside a quoted-delimiter body bash never joins (and treated a `\` before a CR as a continuation, which bash does not), so the body ran past bash's terminator and swallowed the command after it — on `main` for `'EOF'`/`"EOF"`, and in every quoted spelling once this parser read them all. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1116)
- **A heredoc body starts after the first unquoted newline.** The top-level heredoc reader started the body on the line after the introducer, so a quote left open there (`echo $(cat <<EOF;echo 'q` ⏎ `it's` ⏎ `EOF` ⏎ `) ; cat .env`) put every later line in the wrong quote state and hid the read after the substitution. The command line now runs, as in bash, to the first newline that is not quoted, escaped or inside a `"$( … )"` span, and the body starts there — the rule the substitution scanner already followed, now pinned in both by one test. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1117)
- **The substitution scanner reads a `case` statement, so a pattern's `)` no longer ends the substitution.** `$(case a in a) cat .env;; esac)` in a heredoc body ended at `a)`, so the read fell outside the span and reached no guard while bash ran it. The scanner now models `case … esac` the way bash parses it: the keyword opens one only where a command can start (the body's start, after a separator, or after a leading reserved word such as `then` or `!`), `in` starts the patterns, a pattern's `)` (after an optional leading `(`, with extglob groups counted) opens a clause without closing a level, `;;`/`;&`/`;;&` return to the patterns, and `esac` closes it. A statement still open at end of input widens like the scanner's other lexed constructs, and the quote-blind second reading models `case` too, so a 200 KB `$(case a in a) ` flood surfaces one widened body instead of two per nesting level (1.4 s in `enforce-worktree` before, 0.3 s now). `guard-gh-write` also strips a segment's `case` openers with a cursor instead of shifting the token vector once per opener. Failing closed on every `case` instead was tried and rejected: a failed scan latches every later `"$( … )"` span in the splitter to a character-by-character reading, and a commit body with an odd `"` after it then hid a later read. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1094)
- **A line continuation that destroys a heredoc terminator no longer ends the body.** In an unquoted-delimiter heredoc bash joins `x\` ⏎ `EOF` into `xEOF` before comparing, so the body runs on; `enforce-worktree` and core both ended it at `EOF` and read the real code after bash's terminator as body, allowing `git -C <primary> commit` behind a later heredoc opener. `enforce-worktree` now treats that shape as suspect, and core's terminator model joins continuations first (tabs stripped from the joined line's start only, a quoted delimiter joining nothing — except inside a `` `…` `` span, whose text bash reads with backslash-newlines already removed, so `` `cat <<\EOF` `` ⏎ `EO\` ⏎ `F` ends at the joined `EOF`). Joining continuations for any segmenting guard is also no longer quadratic: a 200 KB run of backslash-newlines took 4 s in `git-safety` and 23 s in `enforce-worktree` (past the hook deadline, which fails open) and now takes milliseconds. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1122)
- **The polish gate judges each ship in the directory its own segment runs in, as well as where it judged it before.** `(cd ../other && gh pr create)`, `{ cd ../other && gh pr create; }`, and `cd a && gh pr create ; cd b && gh pr create` were judged in the cwd or in the last `cd`'s directory, so a ship from an unpolished sibling worktree could pass silently. The gate and `log-polish-nudge` now share one walk (`markers::located_ship_segments`) that judges every ship twice and keeps the sharpest verdict: once in the whole-command directory (the previous reading, so the gate never nudges less than it did), and once in the directory its own segment runs in. In the second reading, a `cd` moves only the ships after it, a `( … )` subshell or `$( … )` substitution keeps its `cd` to itself, a brace group shares its parent's directory unless it is piped or backgrounded, a `cd` in a pipeline stage or a backgrounded segment moves nothing, and quoted or escaped parentheses open and close nothing. The ledger's anchor kind is the first ship in command order. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#997)
- **`record-polish` and `nudge-polish-before-pr` are tested together through the built binary.** New `tests/polish_gate_e2e.rs` records and then gates in one fixture: an attached-HEAD record passes silently, a detached-HEAD record exits 1 and the gate nudges, a record for one branch does not cover another, a `--repo-root`/`--branch` record covers a ship from a subshell, and an unattested security record draws the attestation nudge. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#826)

## [0.110.0] - 2026-09-29

### Changed

- **The registration audit treats eight hooks as wired, and its checked-in fixture follows the monorepo.** `guard-held-close`, `warn-chezmoi-apply`, `warn-entry-posture`, `warn-stacked-base-delete`, `warn-stale-pr-body`, `warn-instruction-narrative`, `warn-plugin-root-cruft` and `warn-inline-body` leave `PENDING_WIRING_HOOKS` now that cameronsjo/cadence#1526 wires them (the #618, #610 and #902 rows stay). `warn-stacked-base-delete` joins `KNOWN_DUPLICATE_REGISTRATIONS`: it is wired as two single-rule `if:` entries (`Bash(*git push*)`, `Bash(*gh pr merge*)`) because `if:` alternation is unsupported. `tests/fixtures/registration-audit/` is refreshed from cadence `origin/main` (06e35d85). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#612, #514, #620, #473, #766, #922, #282, #611)

### Fixed

- **A `-c` script is read as the word the shell hands the wrapper, escapes removed.** Once the tokenizer kept an escaped blank in its word, `bash -c cat\ .env`, `bash -c git\ push\ --force\ origin\ main` and `su -c cat\ .env` reached every guard as one unsplit script and were allowed, while bash runs `cat .env`. `shell_c_argument_tokens` and prevent-secret-leaks' nested-script scan now unescape the script, as `eval` and `trap` already did; over-unescaping a single-quoted script can only add a block.
- **`guard-push-remote` treats a Windows drive path after `cd` as absolute, like `/x`.** A literal absolute `cd` ends the directory doubt an earlier `cd "$HOME"` raised, but the test was `starts_with('/')`, so `cd "$HOME" && cd C:\repo && git push` stayed "could not be verified" and nudged on Windows. It now uses core's `looks_absolute`, the same test `resolve_cd_target` already applies. This was the one Windows CI failure after #1132 merged.
- **`enforce-worktree` reads a directory value the way bash does, in every spelling.** `--git-dir=<p>`, `--git-dir <p>`, `--work-tree` in both forms, `-C <p>`, `env -C <p>`, `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` (prefixed or exported) and `cd <p>` now all go through one rule. Since #1074 the `=` forms had removed backslashes while the others kept them. On Windows that turned a native `D:\repo\.git` into `D:repo.git`, a path naming no repo. On Linux the raw forms judged `/p\#repo` where bash hands git `/p#repo`. Either way, a commit from a worktree into the primary checkout was allowed. Now a native Windows path (`C:\…`, `C:/…`, `\\host\…`) stays as written, and any other value gets bash's backslash removal. A value the guard cannot read the same way bash does is treated as unreadable and blocks from a worktree. That covers a drive-like prefix in another shape, an escape in front of `$`, a backtick, `~` or a glob character, and a literal-backslash spelling that exists on disk. The shared tokenizer also keeps an escaped blank in its word, as bash does, so `git -C /p\ repo commit` is read as a commit into `/p repo` and no longer as a `repo` subcommand with no target. That also closes the recorded miss for `/c/Program\ Files/Git/cmd/git.exe commit`. `cd\ /other && git push` is now read as bash runs it: one command word that never moves the shell, not a refused directory verb. Separately, the quote-blind fallback reading turns backslashes in a native Windows path into `/` instead of deleting them, which had added a phantom relative target. Three test fixtures also compared against `crates/guardrails/../../target` paths the guard folds, and so failed only outside `.claude/worktrees/`; their scratch root is now built without `..`.
- **Every reader of gh's `-R`/`--repo` shares one parser that follows gh's own grammar, and reads URL-shaped values.** Five scanners with four grammars (first-wins, last-wins, unanimity, prefix-only) are replaced by `core::shell::gh_repo_flags` (every pflag spelling — `-R v`, `-Rv`, `-R=v`, `--repo v`, `--repo=v` — in any position before `--`) and `parse_gh_repo_value` (go-gh's split: a URL only when it starts with `git@` or a supported scheme, otherwise `[HOST/]OWNER/REPO`). `guard-gh-write` now judges `-R github.com/cameronsjo/x` and `-R https://github.com/cameronsjo/x` as `cameronsjo/x` on github.com instead of false-blocking them, and judges `-R https://evil.example/cameronsjo/x` on `evil.example`. A value gh would refuse (`-R cameronsjo`, `-R a/b/c/d`) or that cannot be split exactly as gh does (a percent-escape in a URL) now blocks `guard-gh-write` as unresolvable instead of being compared as an `owner/…` string; `warn-issue-tracker` nudges on it instead. An inline `GH_REPO=` is judged as a target too. Repeated `-R` flags resolve last-wins, as gh's flag parser does, when each is certainly a flag; `warn-issue-tracker` took the first and `loop_analysis` could pick a decoy (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#937)
- **`guard-gh-write` sees writes that put `-R` before the verb, run gh through `env -S`, or set `GH_HOST` on a wrapping shell.** Write detection now also reads the parsed argv, finding the command group and verb the way cobra does, so `gh -R evil/x issue create`, `gh --repo=evil/x pr create`, `gh pr -R evil/x create` and `gh.exe issue create` are writes. `env -S '<cmd>'`/`--split-string` is unwrapped like `eval`, and a `GH_HOST=h` in front of `bash -c`/`sh -c`/`timeout … bash -c` that runs gh is recorded as a host the inner write may reach. The prefix-assigned `eval` and `env GH_HOS${X}T=` shapes named in the issue already blocked on main (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1077)
- **`guard-gh-write` no longer lets a `-R`-shaped value of another flag stand in for the real target.** `gh issue create --body -Rcameronsjo/x`, `-t -Rcameronsjo/x`, and `-R cameronsjo/x -R ''` from an unowned checkout were judged as the owned decoy while gh wrote to the checkout's own repo. A reading that follows a flag which may take a value (anything but a short list of known booleans such as `--draft` and `--squash`) is now uncertain, and the guard judges both it and the fallback target. A `--` ends the flags only where no earlier flag can take it as its value, since gh's flag parser hands `--` to a value-taking flag: `gh issue create -b -- -R stranger/y` targets `stranger/y` and now blocks. Quoted prose (`--body "see -R other/repo"`) was already one token and still is (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1069)
- **`warn-issue-tracker` reads `-R` from the parsed argv, so a `-R` inside a quoted title or body no longer wins over the real flag.** It split the raw text on whitespace and took the first `-R`; it now uses the shared parser, which also makes it see `gh -R o/r issue create`, URL-shaped values, and repeated flags that disagree (an unreadable-target nudge) (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#737)
- **The gh repo-value parser no longer reads an scp-looking value gh treats as a path.** `gh_repo_value_parts` read `github.com:x@evil.example/o/r` as scp-style with host `github.com`; gh splits it on `/`, and connects to `evil.example`. It now splits every value the way go-gh does, so the merge anchor, `markers::resolve_ship_target` and `verify-pr-autoclose` compare the host gh actually reaches (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1037)
- **`guard-gh-write` and `guard-gh-dangerous` read the brace-expanded argv.** `gh {pr,} create -R evil/x`, `{gh,} pr create -R evil/x`, `gh repo {delete,} o/r --yes` and `{gh,} repo delete o/r` were allowed because both guards matched the raw text; they now also match the tokens bash runs. A gh segment whose brace expansion is too large to model blocks in both guards (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1115)
- **A `tool_input` with one wrong-typed field no longer hides the whole operation from every guard.** One mistyped field used to degrade the entire `tool_input` to `None`, so `{"command":"git reset --hard","file_path":5}` read as "no command" and every guard allowed it. Each field is now parsed on its own: a mistyped field is dropped and the rest are kept, so that command is judged and blocked on its merits. When the dropped field is the one a guard judges (`command`/`cmd` on a Bash call; the path, content or edit fields elsewhere; or a non-object `tool_input` on Bash/Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit), a security-critical guard now blocks with a message naming the field, never its value, and writes an `unreadable_input` row to `failopen.jsonl`. This is not ADR-0001's fail-open case: the guard has no bug; the input is unreadable. Non-critical hooks still run on the salvaged input, and `mcp__*` tools, whose shapes belong to third-party servers, are never blocked this way. An argv-array `command` of strings (the historical Codex `shell` shape) is joined into the one shell string that reproduces it, each word single-quoted unless it is plain, and then judged; an array holding anything but strings still blocks. A mistyped top-level field (`"cwd": 5`, `"session_id": {}`) is dropped the same way instead of failing the whole parse and exiting 0 unjudged; an unreadable `tool_name` counts as unreadable input. `replace_all` accepts `"true"`/`"false"` strings, and any other value reads as `true`, the worst case for the content simulation, rather than blocking the edit. The `schema_drift` row is unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1087)
- **`verify-pr-autoclose` picks its flow from parsed `gh pr` segments, and a bare `gh pr merge` can verify.** A substring test used to pick the flow, so `gh pr -R owner/repo merge 17` and `gh -R owner/repo pr merge 17` never reached the merge flow, while `echo 'gh pr merge'` did. The current-branch lookup passed `-R <slug>` with no selector. gh refuses that combination ("argument required when using the --repo flag", `pkg/cmd/pr/view/view.go`), so a bare `gh pr merge` never verified anything. That path now runs `gh pr view --json number,url` in the session cwd and uses the number only when the URL names origin's repo. After `--delete-branch` the checkout has already left the merged branch, so the path stays silent there. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1076)
- **The `gh` calls in `verify-pr-autoclose` and `warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` are bounded per call.** Both used to wait on `gh` indefinitely, and on a slow GitHub day the external hooks.json timeout killed them with no message. `warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` now uses `BoundedGhRunner` (2 s per call inside the shared hook budget). **Behaviour change:** a `gh` answer that takes 2–5 s now gets no answer and the nudge stays silent, where it used to nudge late. `verify-pr-autoclose` cannot share that budget, because it deliberately sleeps for GitHub's auto-close. Its calls get 8 s each and stop 25 s into the flow, under its 30 s hooks.json timeout. Its auto-close sleeps are bounded by the same deadline: a sleep that would leave no room for one more call is skipped and the flow stays silent, and `GH_AUTOCLOSE_WAIT_SECONDS` is capped at 15. A call that times out writes the usual `deadline` row to `failopen.jsonl` and a stderr line naming `gh`, instead of disappearing silently. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#986)
- **The panic hook's stderr line is bounded and sanitized, and now names the source location.** It used to print the whole panic payload verbatim. A formatted panic can quote runtime data, and Claude Code can keep hook stderr in its debug logs. Control and invisible-format characters now become spaces, the message is cut at 400 characters, and the `file:line` location is appended after the cut. The `failopen.jsonl` row is unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#990)
- **A bounded subprocess that is given up on takes its grandchildren with it (Unix).** `run_bounded_capped` now starts each child as the leader of its own process group. On a timeout, an output overflow, or a pipe that never drains, it SIGKILLs the whole group, so the orphan that held the pipe no longer outlives the hook. A clean run signals nothing, so a helper a tool detaches on purpose keeps running. The reader thread also retries an interrupted read and stops collecting once the caller has given up. Every production `GitSpawn` match was already exhaustive; the enum's doc now makes "no `_` arm" the rule, so a new variant fails to compile at each call site. Windows still kills only the child. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#939)
- **`enforce-worktree` sees a commit behind `nice`, `timeout`, `sudo`, `stdbuf` and `xargs`.** From a primary checkout, `nice -n 5 git commit`, `timeout 5 git commit`, `sudo -u root git commit` and `stdbuf -o0 git commit` were allowed: only the transparent prefixes and `env` were peeled, so the command word stayed the runner. Each runner's options are now walked with core's runner grammar (the peel `peel_command_runners` uses), a `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` word after `sudo` is read, and a runner option that grammar does not model (`sudo -D <dir>`, `timeout --weird`) makes the commit unreadable, which blocks from a linked worktree and judges the session cwd from a primary. An in-chain `dismiss-enforce-worktree` is still recognized only as the segment's own command, so a dismiss behind `sudo` or `nice -n …` licenses nothing (a bare `nice` is a transparent prefix, so a dismiss behind it still licenses). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1111)
- **`enforce-worktree` closes five misses left after #1110.** (1) A heredoc a shell runs is walked as a child script: `cat <<'EOF' | sh` (any pipe stage, `|&`, a runner in front), `bash <<'EOF'`, `sh -s <<EOF`, `source /dev/stdin <<'EOF'`, `. /dev/stdin`, `source <(cat <<'EOF' …)`, `eval "$(cat <<'EOF' … )"` and `bash -c "$(cat <<'EOF' … )"` all committed in a primary from a `cd` in the body. A group or subshell piped on (`{ cat <<EOF; } | sh`), a pipe target an expansion decides (`| $SHELL`), a pipe left open at the end of the line, and a heredoc inside such a body count too. Such a command is no longer plain, and its body's `cd`s and commits join the union reading. (2) A `..` through a symlink into a directory the same command creates (`mkdir link/../nd && git -C link/../nd commit`, also `env -C`, `GIT_WORK_TREE=` and a `cd`) is unreadable: the longest existing prefix is resolved physically and the rest folded, so a missing target no longer skips the check. (3) A `~` in a command that may rebind HOME (`HOME=<p>; git -C ~ commit`, `export HOME=<p>; cd ~`, or a name built by an expansion handed to `export`, `printf -v`, `test` and the like) is unreadable, like `$HOME`; an ordinary `~` keeps its process-home reading. (4) An exported `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` (`export GIT_DIR=<p>/.git; git commit`, `GIT_DIR=…; export GIT_DIR`, `declare -x`, `set -a`) reaches every commit without its own, and a value the walk cannot see (`read GIT_DIR`, `export ${v}_DIR=…`) is unreadable. (5) A backslash-newline that joins two body lines of an unquoted heredoc into its delimiter (`EO\` then `F`) ends the body there, as bash does, so the lines after it are read as code. A heredoc written to a file and run later is still not followed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1113)
- **The secret guards close three #1097 follow-ups: `.e*` sweeps, abbreviated pattern suppliers, and `-e` over-blocks.** A glob component opening with `.` plus at most one literal (`.e*`, `.n*`, `.p*`) now counts as a dotfile sweep, so `cat .e*`, `source .e*`, `tar czf x.tgz .e*` (reads) and `rm .e*` (writes) block while `.git*` and `.eslintrc*` stay allowed. `prevent-secret-leaks` reads any `--name=` abbreviation of a pattern-supplying option as a supplier (`grep --reg=x .env*`, `sed --exp=p .env*`, `awk --sou=`), adds awk's `--exec`/`-E`, and reads ag's optional-value `-A`/`-B`/`-C`/`--after`/`--before`/`--context` as taking none, so the file operand never lands in the exempt pattern slot. A pattern given through `-e`/`--regexp` (and `sed --expression`, `awk --source`) before the first positional now gets the positional pattern's glob exemption, so `grep -e 'secret*' x.txt` allows; its literal secret names and peeled redirections still block. The exemption, positional or option-supplied, now holds only when every glob character in the word was quoted or escaped: bash expands an unquoted `grep .env* x` into `grep .env .env.local x`, so an unquoted glob there is judged like any operand (a new `MarkedToken::unquoted_glob` mark in core's tokenizer carries the quoting). The file a pattern is loaded from is judged as a known filename in every spelling, so `grep --file=.env x`, `--fil=.env`, `-fprod.env`, `sed --file=` and `awk --exec=`/`-E` block like `grep -f .env x` (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1114)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` judges the files `curl` uploads.** `-F name=@FILE`/`name=<FILE` (with `;type=` stripped), `--form`, `-d@FILE` and the other `--data*`/`--json` spellings attached or not, `--data-urlencode`/`--url-query`/`--variable name@FILE`, `-H @FILE`, `-T FILE`/`--upload-file`, short clusters (`-sF`, `-sd@`), 3+-character long abbreviations, and curl 8.3's `--expand-` prefix are each read as a read of that file. `--form-string` and `--data-raw` values, and a header value such as `-Hd@x`, stay literal (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1098)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` stops blocking `kubectl --kubeconfig ~/.kube/config`.** Per the operand-position floor ruled on this issue, only that one option of that one verb, under the byte-exact head, is exempt: `--kubeconfig[=]PATH` when `PATH` is a non-secret file directly in a `.kube` directory and the command carries no `config` word (`kubectl config view --raw` prints the file). A substitution, redirection, glob, or whitespace in `PATH` voids the exemption, and so does any word of the command carrying a backslash, quote character, backtick, or `$` other than a leading `$HOME/` (`conf\ig view --raw` is `config view --raw` to bash). Every `--kubeconfig` value the exemption does not cover is judged as a known filename, since kubectl reads the last one: `--kubeconfig=~/.kube/config --kubeconfig=.env` blocks. `export KUBECONFIG=~/.kube/config` still blocks: the ruling forbids an assignment right-hand-side exemption (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#771)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` stops blocking `gcloud storage ls gs://…/.netrc`.** Under the same operand-position floor, `gcloud storage ls|du` operands carrying a `gs://` scheme are exempt: an object listing reads no local file. `gcloud storage cat`, a local-path operand, global flags before `storage`, and a substitution or redirection in the operand still block (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#782)
- **A padded chain of shell assignments no longer runs a Bash guard past its deadline.** `core::shell` searched every earlier `VAR=value` on each `$` and cloned the whole list for every substitution, so 200 KB of `Dn=$(x y);` before `rm $D1/.env` took 9.9 s in `prevent-secret-writes` and the hook failed open. `D=ab; D=$D$D; …` doubled the value on every segment and hung outright. Assignments are now indexed by name (newest wins), and a subshell borrows its parent's scope instead of copying it. A value over 4 KiB is stored as its first and last 2 KiB. Once 1 MiB of substitution has been spent, a value over 64 bytes goes in as its first and last 32 bytes. No bound ever drops a name or leaves `$NAME` literal, so a padded prefix cannot hide the `D=.env; cat $D` behind it. The 200 KB chain now blocks in about 0.07 s, and 400 KB in 0.13 s. `guard-gh-write` also blocks a command nested more than 64 groups deep: at about 900 levels of `{ (` the shell parser used to overflow the stack and abort the hook. Deep `{ (` nesting was also quadratic: `strip_group_wrappers` walked the whole rest of the segment at every leading `{` to check for a brace expansion, so 50 KB of nesting took 6–8 s in `prevent-secret-writes`, `git-safety` and `guard-gh-dangerous`, 2 s in `guard-sops-decrypt`, and 15–30 s in several nudges. Every one of them failed open. The check now walks only the first word, and it skips the walk entirely when `{` stands alone (followed by whitespace or an operator, as in `{(echo hi)}`). At most 64 glued `{` words are walked. Each shape now blocks in about 0.01 s. The debug-build wall-clock bound of `enforce_worktree`'s `adversarial_inputs_for_the_1111_and_1113_readings_stay_fast` is now 12 s: it hit 4.36 s against its 4 s bound under a loaded parallel suite with no algorithmic change. The release bound stays at 4 s. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1114)
- **Bash guards judge the words bash builds, not only the raw text.** `git-safety`'s `contains("git")` fast path and both gh guards' `contains("gh")` fast path skipped `$'\x67it'`, `g''it`, `g\it` and `$'\x67h'`. They now skip only commands that contain no quoting, escape, expansion or brace syntax. `guard-gh-dangerous` and `guard-gh-write` also match their raw-text patterns against the decoded words, so `gh $'repo' delete`, `'gh' repo delete`, and `gh issue $'create' -R x/y` block, while quoted prose still does not. `git-safety` now treats a `+` refspec as a force: `git push origin +main` and `+HEAD:main` block, and `+feature` nudges. Redirect targets decode ANSI-C escapes, so `echo hi > $'.en\x76'` blocks. The secret guards also see through a glued group closer (`{ (cat .env)}`, `{ (cp x .env)}`) and an unquoted backslash (`cat .e\nv`). `git-safety` now judges a subshell or group glued to its words, such as `(git push --force origin main)` or `{(git reset --hard HEAD~3)}`, which it used to read as the tokens `(git` and `main)`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1103)
- **An `env` assignment operand that is not a valid shell name no longer hides the command behind it.** `env` takes every leading operand containing `=` as an assignment, so `env A${Y}B=1 bash -c 'gh pr create -R evil/x'` runs the inner gh (measured with a canary). The runner peel stopped at `A${Y}B=1`, so no guard saw the `-c` script. The peel now follows `env`'s rule, after flags and `--` as well. Separately, `guard-gh-write` tracks an exported `GH_REPO` the way it tracks `GH_HOST`: `export`, `declare -x`, `set -a`, an `eval`/`trap` string, a non-literal declared name, and a `GH_REPO=` on a wrapping shell. Any of these retargets a later write that has no `-R`, and an inherited `GH_REPO` is read too. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1129)
- **`guard-push-remote` judges a push in the repository it runs in.** The guard found the push directory with `parse_work_dir`, a flat scan for `cd`. `eval 'cd /other'; git push origin main`, a `trap` action, `git -C /other push`, `pushd`, `builtin cd`, `\cd`, a subshell or `bash -c` `cd`, and a `GIT_DIR=`/`--git-dir` redirect all pushed from an unowned repository while the guard checked the owned checkout. It now reads `core::push`'s non-flat walk through the new `push_locations`. A push in another known directory is checked there. A push whose repository can't be known (an `eval`, a `trap` action, a `GIT_DIR=`/`--git-dir` redirect, a prefix the walk can't peel) is blocked. A push after a `cd` or `git -C` target the walk can't read (`cd "$VAR"`, `cd -`, `cd $(…)`, `popd`, `git -C "$D"`, `env -C "$D"`) is a nudge, and a later literal absolute `cd` clears that doubt. `env -C DIR`/`--chdir` is followed like `cd`. `cd`/`git -C` to `"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"` and `eval "$(ssh-agent …)"` (options only, redirections allowed) stay in the checked repository and allow. Neither allow applies when the substitution is single-quoted or backslashed, or when the command may redefine `git`/`ssh-agent`: a function, an alias, a `PATH` assignment or export, `hash`, `enable`, `source`/`.`, or `BASH_ENV=`/`ENV=`. `PushInvocation` gains `repository_unresolved` and `directory_unverified`, which split `unresolved` into its which-repository and unread-directory causes. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1095)
- **The remote-URL ownership parser refuses URLs that git or curl read differently.** `host_and_repo_from_url` read `https://evil.com#@github.com/o/r` as host `github.com` via the last `@`, and `https://github.com/o/x/../../other/y` as owner `o`. It now returns no owner for a scheme URL with `#`, `?`, `[`, a backslash, whitespace, a percent-escape outside the userinfo, or more than one `@` in the authority, and for a `.`/`..` path segment in either URL form. Callers treat that as not owned. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1066)
- **The secret guards judge the files `curl` and `wget` read and write through their options.** `prevent-secret-writes` now sees `curl -o`/`--output` (under `--output-dir` too), `-D`, `-c`, `--trace*`, `--stderr`, `--etag-save`, `--libcurl`, and `-O`/`--remote-name[-all]`, which save under the URL's last path component; and `wget -O`/`--output-document`, `-o`, `-a`, `-e output_document=…`, and a plain `wget URL`, which saves under the URL's name (under `-P`). `prevent-secret-leaks` now sees `curl -K`/`--config` and `-b FILE` (a `name=value` cookie string stays allowed), and `wget --post-file`, `--body-file`, `-i`/`--input-file`, `--config` and their `-e` wgetrc spellings, attached or spaced, abbreviated or in a short cluster. The curl grammar from #1098 moved into `secret_patterns` so both guards read one argv the same way; `curl --opt=value` stays allowed because curl refuses that spelling. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1125)
- **The secret guards judge files a `sed` or `awk` program opens, and attached `--opt=FILE` values of flagged options.** `sed`'s `r`/`R` (reads), `w`/`W` and `s///w` (writes) and `e COMMAND` (a command, scanned in its own right) are read from the positional program and every `-e`/`--expression`, with the file name running to the end of its line as sed reads it; awk's `getline < "f"`, `print > "f"`, `system("…")` and pipes are read from string literals, and a program that opens a name built from an expression makes every literal a candidate. `kubectl --from-file=`/`--from-env-file=`/`--client-key=`, `grep --include=` and ripgrep's `--glob=`/`--iglob=` are judged like their spaced spellings; a generic `--opt=FILE` rule is not applied, since #771 ruled `node --env-file=.env` allowed. `cat .e\nv` and `cat ~/.kube/conf\ig` already block after #1118. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1130)
- **`core::shell` models declaration builtins, `+=`, default-word expansions and arrays when it resolves variables.** Operands of `local`/`declare`/`typeset`/`readonly`/`export` (past their options) and every leading `A=1 B=2` word are assignments; `D+=v` concatenates; `${D:-word}`/`${D-word}` and `${D:=word}` give the value when `D` is assigned and `word` otherwise (`:=` also assigns it for later segments), `${D:+word}` gives `word`. The walk cannot prove a tracked assignment still holds (`D=`, `unset D`, `false && D=x`, a prefix-only `D=x cmd`), so a segment whose expansion turned on `D` being set is also emitted the other way — the default word, or nothing for `+` — and `D=x; cat ${D:-.env}` blocks; `NAME=(a b)` arrays resolve `${NAME[i]}` for a literal index, `${NAME[@]}`/`${NAME[*]}` and an index the walk cannot evaluate to every element. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1124)

## [0.109.0] - 2026-09-29

### Added

- **`guardrails warn-stale-pr-body` nudges at the ready flip when the PR body was never edited after the branch gained commits.** On `gh pr ready`/`gh pr merge` it reads the PR's `createdAt`, `lastEditedAt`, and newest commits in one GraphQL call and warns when the body is untouched while at least one commit's committer date is after creation, so the entry-posture placeholder does not become the squash-merge record. It resolves the PR exactly as `warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` does (that guard's resolution is now shared), queries only `github.com` or `origin`'s host, and fails open on any error. Built but not yet wired. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#766)
- **`guardrails warn-stacked-base-delete` warns before deleting a branch that open PRs base on.** `git push <remote> --delete <branch>` (also `-d` and `:<branch>`) and `gh pr merge --delete-branch` look up open PRs with that `baseRefName` and name them, since GitHub closes such PRs rather than retargeting them. A fork-head merge is skipped, a URL remote on an untrusted host is never queried, and every error is silent. Built but not yet wired. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#473)
- **`guardrails warn-entry-posture` checks the entry posture on a session's first write in a linked worktree.** It nudges once per session per worktree when the branch has no upstream (`git push -u`) or, once pushed, no open PR (`gh pr create --draft`). The marker is claimed before probing, so a healthy worktree pays for the check once. Built but not yet wired. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#620)
- **`cadence guard-held-close` blocks `gh issue close` against the HELD-issue ledger.** The ledger is `backlog-report.sh`'s format (`owner/repo#N`, whitespace-separated), read from `CADENCE_DRAIN_HELD` when set or else from `--ledger <file>`; with neither, nothing is held. Matching errs toward blocking: every issue-shaped operand is a candidate, a bare number is scoped to the `-R`/`GH_REPO` repo or the checkout's remotes and matched against the whole ledger when neither can be read, and a non-literal selector searches the whole command. Built but not yet wired. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#612)
- **`guardrails warn-chezmoi-apply` warns when `chezmoi apply` would discard local drift.** Per the #514 ruling it runs `chezmoi status` through a bounded call and nudges on `MM`/`MD` rows, narrowed to the apply's targets and `--include`/`--exclude`; an unscoped apply gets a scoping clause (`--include=files`). A clean tree, a dry run, a relocated source or config (never run before approval), and an absent `chezmoi` are all silent. Built but not yet wired. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#514)
- **Metrics ledgers are created owner-only (`0600`), and `doctor` warns when one grows past 64 MiB.** Every `<metrics_dir>/*.jsonl` writer, `plan-links.jsonl` included, now opens through one `metrics::open_ledger` helper. A ledger an older binary left group- or world-readable has its group and other bits cleared on its next append (owner bits are kept), and a mode that is already tighter is left alone. On Unix the ledger is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, so a symlinked ledger is skipped: nothing is written through it and no chmod reaches its target. On Windows the open is unchanged. For size, `doctor` raises one warning per ledger at or above 64 MiB and gives a copy-pasteable `mv <ledger> <ledger>.archived`; the next write starts a fresh file, and nothing is deleted or rewritten automatically. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#425)
- **`crates/metrics/prices.json` is now generated from the published pricing table, with a weekly drift check.** `scripts/generate-prices.py --refresh` parses the `## Model pricing` table of the markdown pricing page and rewrites the file in canonical form (models sorted by id); `--check` validates it offline and runs on every PR that touches the table or the generator, alongside the generator's unit tests, which use a saved copy of the page and never fetch. The new `prices.yml` workflow runs `--refresh` weekly and on dispatch and opens a PR with the Forge Bellows app token when a rate moves, so the PR runs CI. It runs only on `main`, checks out without persisted credentials, and skips while a refresh PR is open; only a same-repo PR authored by the app counts, so a fork PR with a matching branch name cannot suppress it. The generator refuses a rate that is non-finite, zero, or above 1000, a committed rate that moves more than 10x, a new model's rate below 0.001, and (without `--allow-removals`) a model the page dropped, which the weekly job keeps rather than deletes. It also refuses a non-`text/markdown` response, a redirect off `https` on an allowlisted host, and a body over 2 MiB. `lastVerified` moves only when a rate changes. The first regeneration adds nine rows the hand table lacked, among them `claude-opus-5-5` ($4/$20, cache read 0.05x), `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-mythos-5-1` and `claude-mythos-5`, so sessions on those models are priced instead of recorded at $0. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#866)
- **A `tool_input` or `tool_response` object that fails its typed shape now writes a `schema_drift` row to `failopen.jsonl`.** The field still degrades to `None` exactly as before; the row only makes a drift-shaped mismatch visible. A non-object value (a `Read` string, a `Glob` array, `null`) is expected per-tool variance and stays silent, and `mcp__*` tools are skipped because their shapes belong to third-party servers. The row names the key only (`tool_response: object did not match its typed shape`), never a value, and the exit code is unchanged. It fires on both the check and the logger dispatch paths, once per process: a `group` writes the rows once, attributed to its first member. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#364)
- **`metrics log-subagent` records cost on `SubagentStop`, so read-only agents are no longer invisible to the cost ledger.** The Stop row in `subagents.jsonl` now carries `model`, `tokens`, `byModel`, `unpricedModels` and `costUsd`, scanned from the agent's own transcript (`<parent transcript minus .jsonl>/subagents/agent-<agentId>.jsonl`) and priced from the same table as `commits.jsonl` and `sessions.jsonl`. Reviewers and explorers never commit, so before this their spend reached no ledger at all. When the agent transcript is missing, unreadable, not a regular file (a FIFO returns at once rather than blocking), over 256 MiB, or has no usage yet, the five fields are `null` and the lifecycle row is still written. An `agent_id` outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` is not used to build a path. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#283)
- **`doctor --prune` takes `--keep-newest N` and `--older-than <age>`, so a machine that is never quiet can still prune.** Without them, `--prune --apply` refuses while any session is registered, and on a busy machine the live set turns over before it empties. With either bound, each dir is decided on its own: it is removed only when it carries an `.orphaned_at` stamp, falls outside the N newest (unstamped dirs rank newest and are always kept), is at least `<age>` old (`90m`, `12h`, `7d`, `2w`), and was orphaned more than ten minutes before every live session started, since a session that started later loaded the pin the manifest named then. Session records now carry `start_verified`, set only when a fresh `startup` registered them and dropped by any later `resume`, `clear`, `compact` or `fork` registration: those run in a process that may have loaded its plugins earlier, and so does a record a heartbeat recreated or one from an older binary. While any such session is live, or a live start is dated in the future, or a registry directory exists but cannot be listed, the bounds keep every dir. The bounds see only registered sessions: one idle at the prompt past the staleness window has had its record swept by peers, and one whose cwd is outside any git repo never registers, so neither holds back a delete. Close those before a bounded `--apply` too. `CADENCE_DOCTOR_PRUNE_FORCE=1` drops only the live-session check; the bounds still apply. Every `--prune` run also drops a candidate that resolves onto a pinned install, or outside the plugin cache root, once symlinks are resolved. As a tripwire, not a guarantee, `--apply` exits 2 and names the path if any install the manifest named is gone afterwards: it reports damage already done, and it reads only the invoking profile's manifest. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#904)
- **`persist-plan-approval` reminds a same-session approval to announce the driver.** attune asks for a bold driver line right after approval ("**Plan approved — Sonnet-drivable**" or "**Plan approved — needs an Opus driver: <trigger>**"), and nothing prompted it. The persist nudge now carries that reminder, pointing the session at the plan's `## Orchestrator` block, between the persist sentence and the model check. It is static text, and an approve-and-clear pickup does not get it, since the approving session there is the parent. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#608)
- **`cadence warn-instruction-narrative` — a nudge when an always-loaded instruction file gains narrative.** A PreToolUse Write/Edit/MultiEdit check on `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md` and `AGENTS.md` that judges only the lines the call adds (for a Write, lines absent from the file on disk). It fires on three or more past-event markers (an ISO date, `measured`, `incident`, `turned out`, …), or on an added paragraph past 8 sentences or 1200 characters that carries at least one marker, with fenced code and table rows stripped and each list item its own paragraph. A pointer phrase (`commit history`, `see`, `memory`, …) clears both, and length alone never fires. The limits were tuned against the estate's own instruction files, where dense rule-plus-dated-receipt bullets are the house style: the issue's 3 sentences / 400 characters / 2 markers fired on 42% and 24% of their units, and the shipped limits fire on 7% and 6%. An RFC-2119 keyword does not clear it. The message names the commit message, a gotcha doc and project memory as the homes for the story. Built unwired: the `cadence` plugin's hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up monorepo PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#922)
- **`cadence warn-live-memory-write` — a nudge on a direct write to live auto-memory outside a dream adoption window.** A PreToolUse Write/Edit/MultiEdit check: a target under `<config dir>/projects/<slug>/memory/` nudges unless `<config dir>/cadence/dreams/<slug>/.dream-lock` exists and is under 6 hours old, the window dream itself uses to tell a live run from a crashed one. A stale lock nudges and says so. It reads one `stat` and never opens the lock or the memory file. Warn only, so tend's approved retires and ordinary memory writes stay legal. Built unwired: the hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up monorepo PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#618)
- **`cadence warn-plugin-root-cruft` — a nudge on a write that creates plugin-root `docs/` or `scripts/` content.** A PreToolUse Write/Edit/MultiEdit check built to the 2026-08-09 rulings in `docs/plans/2026-07-28-guard-design-decisions.md`. A segment pre-filter with no I/O requires `plugins/<name>/{docs,scripts}/<file>`, with `docs`/`scripts` directly under the plugin directory, so skill-nested `skills/<skill>/scripts/` never matches. Only a hit reads `<root>/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` through the capped untrusted reader, and the target counts only when a `plugins[].source` string declares `./plugins/<name>`. The plugin cache is out of scope. Nudge tier, per the plan's shared tier policy. Built unwired: the hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up monorepo PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#282)
- **`guardrails warn-inline-body` — a nudge when `gh pr create` or `gh issue create` posts a long body inline.** A PreToolUse Bash check: for every create segment it takes the last body flag, the one `gh` uses, and nudges when that flag is an inline `--body`/`-b` of more than 200 characters, which covers a `"$(cat <<EOF …)"` heredoc. The message steers to `--body-file`, since the file is the redaction review moment. `--body-file` and short inline bodies stay silent. The issue's flux half is not built: the flux board and its `flux-card.sh` helper were retired on 2026-08-07. Built unwired: the `cadence-guardrails` hooks.json entry lands in a follow-up monorepo PR (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#611)

### Changed

- **Every imperative the SessionStart injectors emit now names who acts.** An audit of `session start`, `session backstop-warn` and `metrics warn-stale` against the injected-context contract found five actorless imperatives: the worktree-posture line, the uncommitted-plan line, the missing-rules line, the loose-ends warning and the stale-telemetry nudge. Each now says whether Claude or the user acts (for example `Claude: tell the user, and offer /outro …`; the opt-out `CADENCE_NO_OUTRO_BACKSTOP` is named as the user's). The peer-count line and the checkout-staleness line carry no imperative and are unchanged. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#358)
- **Fixture `git` commits no longer sign, so a broken commit signer cannot redden the suite.** A workspace `.cargo/config.toml` sets `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` (the environment form of `git -c`) to `commit.gpgsign=false` and `tag.gpgsign=false` on every process cargo spawns, which reaches every hand-rolled fixture helper. An ambient `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` still applies alongside it. The installed binary is unaffected. `fixture_git_resolves_commit_and_tag_signing_off` pins it. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1075)
- **`rules validate-frontmatter` now enforces the mechanical Description Format Rules on skills, introduced-only.** A `description` written as a block scalar (`>-`, `|`), with an indented continuation line (including a multi-line quoted string), or longer than the spec cap of 1024 characters is blocked, but only when the edit introduces the violation: a skill that already violates stays editable, though an over-long description may not grow past its on-disk length. Quoting is resolved before comments, so `"Parses # headings"` and `'#12'` measure correctly. A missing trigger clause is a **nudge**, never a block, because it reaches third-party and synced skills that cannot be gated; it accepts `Use when|whenever|after|before` (also `Use this skill …`, `Use it …`, `Use proactively …`, `Invoke when`) in `description` or `when_to_use` (block scalars included), and ignores negations such as "Do not use when". The house 250-character target is not enforced: 20 of 103 shipped skills exceed it. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#613)
- **`rules validate-frontmatter` rejects an introduced `@skills/` force-load reference in skill and command files.** The syntax bypasses conditional activation. A reference already in the file stays editable, and mentions inside inline code spans, fenced or indented code blocks, or glued to a preceding word are allowed. A CommonMark parser (`pulldown-cmark`) is used only to locate code blocks and inline code, which are blanked out; tokens are then scanned in the raw body, which starts at the earlier of the first exact `---` line and where Claude Code's own lazy frontmatter match ends (so a mid-line or trailing-space `---` closes it too), so raw HTML, comments, link definitions, destinations and titles are still caught, while `&#64;skills`, `\@skills` and `@skills/*x*` are not references. Fences in list items, multi-line code spans and indented code follow the spec so the rule can be documented. Trailing `.,;:)` is stripped from a token before comparing. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#614)
- **`rules validate-frontmatter` blocks a nested `provenance:` in a living plan's frontmatter, introduced-only.** For a direct child of `docs/plans/` that opens with a frontmatter block, it also blocks a second frontmatter block above the title and a fenced `provenance:` block that sits before the first heading (a plan body may document the rule). Only violations the edit introduces block, so a plan with a legacy violation stays editable. A plan with no frontmatter is left alone. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#607)
- **`rules validate-frontmatter` nudges when a plugin agent declares `hooks`, `mcpServers`, or `permissionMode`.** The platform accepts and silently ignores all three on plugin-distributed agents (a directory named `agents/` whose parent holds `.claude-plugin/`). It is a nudge, not a block, and `.claude/agents/` is not checked. `skills:`/`mcpServers:` on teammate-dispatched definitions are not checked, since the file does not show how it is dispatched. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#615)
- **Binary-level integration tests can no longer write into the real `~/.claude`.** Every helper that spawns the binary now goes through `tests/support`'s `cadence_hooks()`, which pins `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` to a fresh per-binary dir under cargo's target tmp dir and drops an ambient `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`; `tests/try_hook.rs` and `tests/persist_plan_driver_tier.rs` are handled in cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1065. Test-only, no shipped behavior changes (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1062).
- **`persist-plan-approval` picks the plan's destination from the session root, not from an incidental `cwd`.** The repo holding `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (or the payload `cwd` when it is unset) is the target. The approve-and-clear arm no longer skips a non-repo `cwd` and retries, which used to make whichever repo a later tool call ran in the target. A `cwd` in another repo no longer changes the target. When no repo destination can be proven, the plan goes to a user-scoped `<config_dir>/cadence/plans` and the nudge says why. That copy's `plan-links.jsonl` row records its absolute path, and a re-fire nudge names it. `try session persist-plan-approval` still writes nothing: it runs the hook with `CADENCE_NO_PERSIST_PLAN=1` and without `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1021)
- **The plans directory is configurable per repo, and the nudge no longer orders a commit.** `CADENCE_PLANS_DIR` in a repo's `.claude/settings*.json` `env` block names a relative plans dir, default `docs/plans`. It must resolve inside the checkout, and a value with a `\`, a `..`, or a `.git` component is refused. An empty value opts the repo out. The follow-up sentence now defers to the repo's own plan and commit conventions (its `CLAUDE.md`) instead of always saying to commit (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#560)
- **A session rooted in a multi-repo wing no longer writes the plan into a member repo.** A root outside any git repo gets a destination only from a `CADENCE_PLANS_DIR` in the root's own settings. Without one, the plan stays out of every repo (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#798)
- **A repo you don't own no longer receives the plan.** Every fetch and push URL of the target repo must pass `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS` / `CADENCE_ALLOWED_REPOS`, the allowlist `guard-push-remote` uses, or the plan goes to the user-scoped dir. A repo with no remotes counts as owned. An unset allowlist, an unparseable URL, or a failed `git remote -v` counts as not owned. A non-owned repo's `CADENCE_PLANS_DIR` is never read (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1014)
- **A plan approved while working in a linked worktree lands in that worktree.** When the `cwd` is a linked worktree of the session root's repo, the plan is written there and `branch:` carries the worktree's branch, so it can be committed with the work (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#739)
- **In a worktree-first repo's primary checkout, the plan no longer carries `branch: "main"` or an instruction to commit it there.** When `enforce-worktree` would block commits in the target primary, the plan is written without a `branch:` value, and the nudge says to move it into the feature worktree with the first feature commit and to add the feature branch (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1045)
- **A build with `panic = "abort"` now fails to compile instead of silently disabling every panic guard.** The fail-open `catch_unwind` guards in dispatch, the logger wrapper, and the per-check guards only work when panics unwind, and tests always build with unwind, so no test could catch the switch. `cadence-hooks-core` now carries a `#[cfg(panic = "abort")] compile_error!`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#762)
- **A registration-audit failure from the sibling cadence checkout now says where that checkout stands against `origin/main`.** A sibling leg that is behind reads red on an untouched checkout, and the only hint was to go run `git status -sb`. The failure now carries the measured position: "N commit(s) BEHIND origin/main … most likely checkout skew", ahead with unmerged wiring, diverged, or level. The leg still fails; it no longer needs a second command to diagnose. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#629)
- **`persist-plan-approval` no longer writes `body_sha256:` into a persisted plan's frontmatter.** The hash was stamped only on writes the hook saw, so a PR merge, a `gh api` contents write or an edit from an unwired checkout left it certifying a body that no longer existed, and nothing could verify it. The field is retired: nothing writes it, and no reader may start checking it. Plans already on disk keep the key, and the persist still reads it as a legacy re-fire check, but only where the `plan-links.jsonl` ledger has no row for that path or has one for the same hash. A plan's own `body_sha256:` line is still dropped when the hook renders it. A copy of an approved plan is now recognized in three ways: its body recomputes to the approval (for a plan with its own frontmatter, the approval past that frontmatter), or the ledger records that the hook wrote that path for that body and the file's own `session_id:` names that row's writer, whichever session approves it again (the writer check keeps a row from another checkout sharing the metrics dir, or a reused path, from vouching for a different plan), or this session's ledger row names an earlier copy. What still writes a `-2` copy: a different plan already at the stem, and a same-stem file whose body has drifted and that the local ledger does not record for this body under the file's own writer, such as a plan edited or ticked after its row aged out of the ledger's 1 MiB tail, or one persisted on another machine. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#870)
- **The persist-time model-check nudge now asks first, on any tier difference.** It used to open with "dispatch workers at {tier} or hand the session down" and compare models only "if you are about to execute inline", which a session read as leave to skip the ask. It now says to compare the plan's driver against the running model before the first implementation step, that equal is the only pass, and that any difference, a stronger running model included, means pausing to ask the operator. Dispatching workers at the driver tier or handing the session down is named as an option to offer in that ask, not a way around it. This matches the skill wording in cameronsjo/cadence#1212. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#823)
- **`nudge-plan-tick` names `git commit --amend` as the way to fold the tick into the commit that just landed.** The nudge fires after a successful commit, so the tick always rode a follow-up commit. It now says that when that commit is unpushed, `git commit --amend` lets the tick join it. The commit-time arm the issue also asks for, which would warn before the commit while the plan is dirty but unstaged, is not built: it needs a choice of mechanism and a hooks.json wiring change in the plugin repo. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#691)

### Fixed

- **The shared tokenizer now brace-expands words the way bash does, so `{cat,.env}` and `{sops,-d,secrets.yaml}` no longer hide their command.** Bash expands `{a,b}` and `{x..y}` before running anything, but every walk read `{cat,.env}` as one word, so `prevent-secret-leaks`, `guard-sops-decrypt` and `git-safety` (`{git,reset,--hard}`) all allowed. `tokenize` now expands each unquoted, unescaped brace word in bash's order (quoted or escaped braces, `${…}`, groups with no comma or sequence, and assignment words stay literal), and `strip_group_wrappers` no longer trims the `{` of a brace word as if it opened a `{ …; }` group. Expansion is bounded: 4096 words per word and 64 groups (`${` does not count), plus a budget of 1 MiB / 16k words per tokenizer call and 8 MiB / 256k words per hook thread, charged before anything is built, so a flood of `{1..4096}` words re-tokenized per segment cannot run a guard past its timeout. Past a bound the word is left whole, and `prevent-secret-leaks` and `guard-sops-decrypt` refuse rather than judge a command they cannot see; heredoc bodies are data and never count as an overflow. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1096)
- **`guard-sops-decrypt` now sees a decrypt behind a runner or a flagged prefix.** It skipped only unflagged transparent prefixes, so `nice -n 5 sops -d …`, `timeout 5 …`, `stdbuf -o0 …`, `env -i …`, `sudo …`, `setsid …`, `time -p …`, `command -p …` and `exec -a x …` piped to `grep` were all allowed. The guard now resolves the command word through the shared `peel_command_runners` (which learns `setsid` and its `-c`/`-f`/`-w` flags), and any `sops` word with decrypt-to-stdout arguments at a later position of the segment counts as a decrypt, so a prefix or runner the peel cannot model (`sudo -C 5`, `ionice -c3`) is refused rather than dropped. The consumer allowlist is unchanged: a decrypt behind a prefix still piped to `curl --config -` stays allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1090)
- **A guardrails unit test no longer empties `PATH` for the whole test process, which made fixture `git` calls fail intermittently.** `chezmoi_unavailable_is_silent_not_no_drift` set `PATH` to a nonexistent directory under the env lock, but fixture setup (`git_in`, `init_repo`) does not take that lock, so a concurrent `git init`/`git config` in `warn_branch_base` and other modules could not find `git` and panicked. The test now probes a tool name that resolves nowhere and leaves the process env alone. `git_discovery_env_defers_to_git` had the same shape with `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`; the disk-read env check is now injectable (`read_from_disk_with_env`) so that test sets nothing. The shared `git_in` fixture helper also clears repo-discovery `GIT_*` variables and reports git's stderr, exit status and an env snapshot on failure, so the next such flake is diagnosable. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1112)

- **The substitution scanner now places a `$( )` terminator where bash does across comments, backticks and `${…}` expansions.** A `)` inside a `#` comment or an unquoted backtick span ended the substitution early, and a `(` inside a comment, a backtick span or `${x:-(}` opened a level bash never opened. Inside an expanding heredoc either one cost the whole span, so `$(echo hi # )⏎cat .env)`, `` $(echo `echo )` ; cat .env) `` and `$(echo ${x:-(} ; cat .env)` all reached no guard while bash ran the read. The scanner now skips comments (at bash's word boundaries), backtick spans and `${…}` expansions as opaque, and any run-off-the-end after that lexing, or with more than one level open, reports the new `ScanStop::LexUnresolved` scanner limit so the span widens instead of being dropped. Heredoc bodies inside a substitution are now data to the scanner, including bash 5.2's `EOF)` terminator, so `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'⏎It's done⏎EOF⏎)" ; cat .env` no longer has its apostrophe swallow the read. A span carried out of an expanding heredoc has its own heredocs resolved, under one work budget shared by the whole recursion (8x the input plus 64 KiB; once spent, spans are kept whole, never dropped), so a nested-heredoc flood stays linear instead of pushing the hook group past its fail-open deadline. The comment pass also recognises a `#` directly inside `$( )` (never inside `$(( ))` or `${…}`). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#831)
- **A backtick inside a double-quoted run inside `$( )` no longer moves the terminator earlier than bash.** The scanner read `` "`echo ")"`" `` as plain quoted data, so the backtick's own `"` closed the run and the next `)` ended the substitution, cutting `cat .env` out of a heredoc span. The backtick span is now opaque in the unquoted and double-quoted states, as bash reads it. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#836)
- **A command substitution inside a double-quoted run no longer leaks its own quoting into the segment splitter or the comment pass.** `split_segments_with_ops` read `"$(echo '")')"` as quoted data, let the inner `"` close its run, opened a phantom `'…'` on the apostrophe, and swallowed the `;` and the command after it, so `echo $(echo "$(echo '")')" ; cat .env)` reached no guard while bash ran the read. The splitter and `comment_spans` now copy or skip a `$(…)` or `` `…` `` inside `"…"` whole, bounded by the substitution scanner's depth cap. `\$` and `` \` `` stay literal, and unquoted substitutions keep splitting inside as before. Once one such scan cannot be bounded, the rest of the pass stops scanning, so an opener flood stays linear rather than running into the hook timeout. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#830)
- **Command-substitution scanning is now pinned stack-safe under pathological opener floods.** The premise checked out as already bounded — the quote-blind fallback is a flat counter that never recurses, and re-scans go through `expand_segments`' wrapper-depth cap — so this adds the missing proof: 4096-deep `"$(`, `$(`, backtick and heredoc floods through `command_segments` on a 512 KiB stack return and keep the trailing payload visible. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#821)
- **`enforce-worktree` reads a `git -C` value the way git's `chdir` does, and treats `env -C` as a directory change.** git resolves `-C` physically, so from a linked worktree with `link -> <primary>/sub`, `git -C link/../sub2 commit` and `git -C link/.. commit` committed in the primary while the guard's lexical reading said the worktree. A directory value (`-C`, `--work-tree`, `--git-dir`, `GIT_DIR=`, `GIT_WORK_TREE=`) whose `..` resolves somewhere other than its lexical folding is now unreadable, which blocks from a worktree. `env -C <dir>` / `--chdir` ran the commit in `<dir>` while the guard judged the session cwd; `env`'s own options are now parsed (`-i`, `-u`, `-C`, `-P`, `-0`, `-v`, `-`, `--` and the long forms), the `-C` directory is collected like a `cd`'s, `env -i` and `env -u GIT_DIR` drop the `GIT_DIR=`/`GIT_WORK_TREE=` values set before them, and a commit behind an `env` option the guard does not model (`-S`) is unreadable. This also closes `env -i git commit` from a primary checkout, which the guard never saw. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1100)
- **`enforce-worktree` treats a commit in a `trap` action as unreadable.** The action runs when the signal fires, in whatever directory the shell has reached, so `trap 'cd <p> && git commit' EXIT; cd ..` from a worktree beside `<p>` resolved the trap's `cd` from the worktree and was allowed while bash committed in `<p>`. A trap action that commits is now judged from every directory the command names and blocks from a linked worktree, as core's push walk does. An `eval`'d `cd` already made the command's cd unreadable and keeps doing so. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1091)
- **`enforce-worktree` no longer reads a commit message's escaped quotes and `cd` prose as code.** An escaped quote anywhere in a command with a heredoc made it non-plain, so `cd <worktree> && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF' … \"x\" … EOF)"` blocked from a primary, and from a worktree a body with an escaped quote and prose such as `then cd .. and retry` read as an unreadable `cd`. Escapes inside a heredoc body whose delimiter is plainly spelled (`EOF`, `'EOF'`, `"EOF"`) no longer count, and the line-by-line reads skip such a body. A delimiter with an escape or an inner quote in it (`<<"E\"F"`) is still unreadable, and that repro still blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1101)
- **`enforce-worktree` no longer allows a commit whose `git -C`, `--work-tree`, `--git-dir`, `GIT_DIR=` or `GIT_WORK_TREE=` value it cannot read.** `git -C "$VAR" commit` was judged at `<cwd>/$VAR`, which names no repo, so it was allowed wherever `$VAR` pointed. A leading `$HOME`/`${HOME}` now expands under the same allowlist a `cd` target uses; any other expansion, a glob, a brace or a `~user` form is unreadable, which blocks from a linked worktree and judges the session cwd from a primary. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1056)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer blocks `touch "$D/.env"` when `D` came from an unquoted `$(mktemp -d …)`.** The segmenter recorded an assignment's value from its first whitespace-split token, so `D=$(mktemp -d /x.XXXXXX)` recorded `$(mktemp`, and every later `$D` expanded into a broken substitution that read as a secret operand of a command named `.env`. The metadata-only exemption the denial message advertises (`ls`, `stat`, `wc`, `rm`, `touch`) could not fire. An unquoted `NAME=$(…)` value is now read whole when it is plain: no quotes, backslashes, or backticks anywhere, and no parentheses in the substitution but its own. Any other value keeps the old fragment, so its expansion is unchanged. Where `$D` stands outside double quotes, the value is spliced in quoted so it stays one word, which keeps `rm $D/.env` and `echo x > $D/.env` visible to `prevent-secret-writes`, and an apostrophe inside `"…"` no longer stops later expansions. `cat "$D/.env"` still blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#970)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` sees a secret glued to a redirection, and a jq option value glued to its flag.** The Bash arm tokenizes on whitespace, so `cat<.env`, `jq -R .x<.env`, `jq .env<.env`, and `cat .env>/tmp/x` reached the classifier as one word that named no secret, and were allowed while the spaced spellings blocked. A whitespace-free word is now also read as the shell splits it: the text before its first `<` or `>` is judged where the word sat, and the text after each `<` or `<>` is judged as a file opened for reading, including in the `git` subcommands that keep their exemption (`git column<.env`). Here-strings, heredoc delimiters, fd duplications, and output targets are not read as input files, and a word carrying whitespace or a substitution is still treated as prose. For `jq` alone, an attached option value (`-f.env`, `-rf.env`, `--from-file=.env`) is judged as a file too. jq 1.7 rejects those spellings as unknown options, so this blocks nothing that works today and covers a jq that accepts them; other commands keep the attached-path allowance `node --env-file=.env app.js` relies on (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#771). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1054)
- **Both secret guards judge a glob by what it could expand to.** `cat .env*`, `head .en?`, `cat .[e]nv`, `cat .e{n,}v`, `rm .env*`, and `echo x > .env*` were allowed, because the classifier compared the literal token, and `.env*` is not a secret's name.
