A token carrying `*`, `?`, `[…]`, an extglob group, or a brace group is now compared, as a pattern, with a pattern for every deny-set family, and blocks when two things hold: the two could share a name (whether or not the file exists), and the glob's literal text carries that family's distinctive stem (`.env`, `id_rsa`, `credentials`, `key`, `service-account`, `npmrc`, …). So `.env*`, `*credentials*`, `id_*`, `*.key`, `*env`, `.[e]nv`, and `~/.ssh/*` block, while sweeps by extension or by an unrelated name do not: `cat *.json`, `prettier --write "**/*.json"`, `jq . package*.json`, `cat *.pem`, `.*rc`, `.git*`. Three shapes block without a stem: any glob directly inside `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.kube`, or `.docker`; an extglob group; and a dotfile sweep such as `.*` or `.??*`. Bash's rule that a wildcard never matches a leading `.` is honoured, and a bracket is judged in its own case against either case of the name, so `.[!E]nv` and `id[![:alpha:]]rsa` block. Brace groups are expanded first, up to 64 words, and a token over 4 KiB carrying glob or brace syntax blocks unread, which keeps adversarial input under a tenth of a second. The pattern operand of `grep`, `rg`, `ag`, `sed`, `awk`, `jq`, and `yq` is a regex or filter, so it is exempt from the glob judgment (`grep -r '.*TODO' .`, `yq '.*' x.yaml`); a literal secret name there still blocks, and so do the file operands. Globs whose every match is a template (`.env*.example`) stay allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1052)
- **The secret guards' Bash arms now refuse the key-material files the Read and Write tools already refuse.** The shell classifier held only the `.env` family and the exact credential filenames, so `cat /home/u/prod.key`, `cat deploy-key.pem`, `rm cert.p12`, and `echo x > service-account-x.json` were allowed while the tools blocked the same paths. One shared predicate now serves both surfaces: the `-key.pem`/`_key.pem`/`.private.pem` suffixes and `service-account*.json` block everywhere, and the `.key`/`.p12`/`.pfx`/`.keystore`/`.jks` extensions block wherever the word is known to name a file (a pure reader's operand, a redirection or writer target, or any path with a `/`). A bare `obj.key` on another command's line stays allowed, because jq, yq and JavaScript property paths are spelled the same way. Past the 16 KiB structured-scan cap, where no command grammar vouches for a file, a key-material name anywhere in the command blocks. Its glob half is the cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1052 entry. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#814)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer lets a quote-split or substitution-built secret name skip its operand scan.** A raw-text prefilter ran before the Bash arm tokenized anything and passed only commands whose text contained a deny-set name, so `cat .en''v`, `cat .ssh/id_''rsa`, and `cat "$(echo .e)nv"` were never classified and were allowed. The prefilter is gone, as it went from `prevent-secret-writes` in #655: every command now reaches the segment scan, and a block still requires an operand the token classifier calls a secret. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#819)
- **`session status` lists the sessions in every worktree of the repo, not only the checkout you run it from.** Each worktree keeps its own `.claude/sessions`, so in a branch-mode repo, where the feature work happens in worktrees, the old listing showed only the sessions not doing the work and gave no hint it had looked nowhere else. It now reads the current checkout's registry first, then each worktree `git worktree list` names, prints a heading for every registry holding a record, and ends with a footer counting the worktrees checked and how many have a registry. When the worktree list times out or cannot be read, the footer says so and that only this checkout was checked. Every printed path has control and invisible-format characters replaced with spaces, since a worktree directory name can carry a terminal escape or a newline. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#845)
- **A dotenv read inside a `trap` action is now scanned.** `trap` hands its first operand back to the parser as a script, and `EXIT` fires when the Bash tool's shell ends, so `trap 'cat .env' EXIT` printed the file while `prevent-secret-leaks` allowed it. The shared wrapper walk now surfaces an installing `trap`'s action as a child script, so every guard that expands wrappers sees it; a push walk treats a trap's push as unresolved, since it runs in whatever directory the shell has reached when the signal fires. The action gets the shell's backslash removal, a `-`-leading action after `--` counts, and `$'…'` escapes (`\n`, `\xHH`, octal and the rest of bash's table) are now decoded by the tokenizer, so `trap $'echo a\ncat .env' EXIT` and `bash -c $'…'` are seen too. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1059)
- **Guards now see the command inside `eval`.** `eval 'git push origin main'`, `eval "cat .env"` and `GIT_DIR=/x eval '…'` were opaque to every command-walking guard. The shared wrapper walk now joins `eval`'s operands with spaces after backslash removal, as the shell does, and walks the result like a `bash -c` script. A chain of `eval`s unwraps in place (up to 16) instead of spending the three-level wrapper budget. An operand that is not statically known (`eval "$CMD"`) stays unresolved, and the push walk's refusal on `eval` stays, because an `eval`'d `cd` moves the parent shell. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#886)
- **A path-spelled or `--`-terminated transparent prefix no longer hides the command behind it.** `/usr/bin/nohup sops -d secrets.yaml`, `nohup -- sops -d …` and `/usr/bin/env GIT_DIR=/x git …` left the prefix as the command word, so `guard-sops-decrypt` and every other guard that peels prefixes never saw the verb. The shared prefix test now resolves the word through `command_word`, which basenames it, and treats a `--` straight after a prefix as that prefix's end of options. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#888)
- **A trailing `}` that belongs to a word is no longer trimmed off it.** `strip_group_wrappers` trimmed every trailing `}`, so `git push origin secret}`, `cd /other}`, `rm {a,b}` and `echo ${HOME}` lost a byte the shell keeps. It now trims a `}` only where the shell reads it as a group closer: alone, or after whitespace, `;`, `&`, `|` or `)`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#889)
- **`doctor` no longer warns that `CADENCE_ALLOWED_OWNERS` is unset when the guards would allow.** The push and gh-write guards read the allowlist from their process environment, but doctor looked only in the user settings.json, so an allowlist exported from a shell rc file drew a false "every push and gh write is blocked" warning. Doctor now checks the process environment with the guards' own resolver (`env_allow_entries`), and still treats a string value in the settings.json `env` block as authoritative, since Claude Code applies it over the inherited environment: an explicit empty value there still warns, because the guards would block. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#987)
- **`enforce-worktree` now expands `$HOME` in a `cd` target, but only for commands it can show leave HOME alone.** Before, `cd "$HOME/…/worktree" && git commit` was treated as unresolvable and judged against the starting cwd. From another repo's primary checkout that was a false block, and from a worktree it let a commit into a primary through. A leading `$HOME`/`${HOME}` now resolves against the same process home `~` uses, and only where bash would expand it (`'$HOME/…'` stays literal). It resolves only when every segment of the command starts with a word that cannot rebind HOME (`cd`, `git`, `ls`, `echo`, `pwd`, `true`) and the command has no unquoted grouping or braces and no command, arithmetic or parameter substitution. Everything else keeps the previous fail-closed fallback, including brace-built names like `export {HO,}ME=…`, function bodies, `test -v 'a[HOME=…]'`, and child shells such as `env -i bash -c` or `sudo bash -c`. `~` keeps its existing resolution. `~user` and quoted `"~/…"` no longer fail open to a mangled path. From a primary checkout, a cd the guard still cannot resolve keeps blocking, and the block message now names it and suggests a literal path or `git -C <path>`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1018)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` no longer blocks a jq filter that reads a JSON key named `env`.** `jq '.env.foo' x.json` was hard-blocked as a dotenv operand, but jq's first positional argument is a program, not a file. The guard now walks jq's option grammar to that one argument and exempts only its index; input files, `--rawfile`/`--slurpfile` values and any `-f`/`--from-file` still block. The exemption applies only when the filter is `.`-led with no shell metacharacters before or in it, and the whole command is a plain pipeline: every segment's head is one of `jq cat head tail wc grep ls pwd true`, with no expansion, substitution, grouping, backslash, comment, redirection, heredoc or non-ASCII character. Anything else keeps the full scan, which is how same-command rebinding of `jq` stays out (the same-command form of cadence-hooks#843). A `jq` planted on PATH by an earlier call, or an alias or function from the user's own profile, remains an ambient residual. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#947)
- **`nudge-polish-before-pr` no longer says "No polish recorded" when the command runs outside a repo.** A session rooted outside any git checkout (`~/.claude`, say) that ships with `-R`/`--head` has no checkout to look the marker up in, so the gate now gives the cannot-check advisory instead of claiming polish never ran. The `log-polish-nudge` row records `markerTarget: cannot_check` for the same case. No cwd at all, and a detached HEAD, keep the no-marker nudge. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#453)
- **The polish ship anchor reads `gh pr create`'s draft flag the way gh does.** It compared whole tokens to `-d`/`--draft`, so a title of `-d` (`-t -d`) or a redirect target (`> -d`) made a real create read as a draft and the nudge stayed silent, while the real drafts `-fd` and `-dH feat/x` drew a spurious nudge. The anchor now walks `create`'s flag grammar: it skips other flags' values and redirect targets, reads shorthand clusters, and honors `--draft=false` and `-d=false`. A flag the grammar does not know stops the walk, and only a draft seen before it counts, so an unreadable command nudges. `warn-changelog-entry` and `log-polish-nudge` share the anchor. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#998)
- **An SSH-alias remote now stands for github.com only.** A remote whose host has no dot (`git@github-work:own/repo.git`) matched any repo value with the same `owner/repo`, so `-R gitlab.example.com/own/repo`, or `GH_HOST=ghe.corp.example` with a bare `-R own/repo`, looked the marker up on the local branch as if both named the same repository. Such a remote now matches only a value that names no host or names `github.com`; any other forge gives the cannot-check advisory, and a retargeted `gh pr merge` does not anchor. An alias for a GitHub Enterprise host used with that host spelled out also gets the advisory. No SSH config is read. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#999)
- **The `doctor --prune --apply` gate no longer reads a working session as stale under a short `CADENCE_SESSION_STALE_MINUTES`.** A working session with no override refreshes its record every ten minutes, a third of the default window, whatever the reader's setting. A doctor run with the variable set to 5 read a peer between beats as stale and pruned under it. The gate now reads sessions on the longer of the variable and the default. It also refuses, naming the directory, when a session registry exists but cannot be listed; the registry readers returned nothing on that error, so the gate read it as no sessions and pruned. The gaps 0.106.0 named remain, and closing them is an operator call: a session idle at the prompt for 30 minutes has its record swept by peers, and a session outside any git repo never registers. Close sessions before an unbounded `--prune --apply`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#902)
- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` now checks both halves of the "reviewed" contract it cites.** A PR also counts as unreviewed while any reviewer's latest decisive review is `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, grouped by reviewer id as `poll-prs.sh` does. A clean marker posted after a request for changes no longer reads as reviewed. The request does not clear on a push, so the warning names each one with its dismissal command filled in, and leads with addressing the changes and asking for a re-review; dismissing stays the operator's call (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#978)
- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` says when it found a marker it could not use.** A `cadence-review` marker on a review that does not parse is reported by the fields that failed (`sha=` for `head=`, a short SHA, a missing reviewer field, a missing count), and a marker posted only as an issue comment is reported with the `gh pr review` command that makes it count. Nothing from the comment body is echoed (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#838)
- **`warn-plan-ready-flip` stays quiet for a PR outside the cwd's branch.** A flip whose `-R`, `--repo`, `GH_REPO=`, or PR URL names a repo that is not a remote of the cwd's checkout, or whose branch selector names another branch, no longer warns about the cwd branch's plan. A PR number in the cwd's own repo still warns, as before (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#972)
- **The ready-flip guards see retargeted and prefixed flips.** `warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` and `warn-plan-ready-flip` share one matcher that reads the segment the way the ship anchor does, so `gh -R owner/repo pr ready 12`, `gh pr -R owner/repo merge 5` (the `pr`-level flag, now read by the ship anchor too), `GH_REPO=o/r gh pr merge 5`, `env … gh pr merge 5`, and a keyword-led `then gh pr ready 12` are checked. A path-qualified `gh` still matches, and `warn-plan-ready-flip` keeps its old token-window scan beside the shared matcher so `sudo`, `timeout`, `nice -n`, and `env -u` in front of `gh` still nudge (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#778)
- **`warn-gh-merge-preflight` and `warn-pr-issue-link` see prefixed and keyword-wrapped `gh`.** Both now use the shared `gh pr` matcher, so `GH_TOKEN=x gh pr merge 5`, `env`, `time`, `command`, `nohup`, `exec`, `for … do` / `if … then` segments, and a global or `pr`-level `-R` now nudge. Quoted prose stays silent, and `bash -c '…'` and a path-qualified `gh` still nudge. `sudo`, `timeout`, `xargs`, `stdbuf`, and `eval` remain unseen (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#545)
- **`verify-pr-autoclose` reads a new PR in the repo `gh pr create` printed, not in `origin`.** A PR opened on an upstream from a fork checkout was looked up by number in the fork, so the check reported another PR's refs, and that repo's issue states, as this PR's. The create check now takes the repo from the PR URL, stays silent when there is no URL, when the URL names another host, or when the body is not JSON, and words an unreadable ref as "could not be read" rather than "not found". The merge check had the same defect with side effects: `gh pr merge 16 -R other/repo` read PR 16 in `origin` and could close `origin`'s issues. It now resolves the merged PR with the shared flip matcher, `ship_target`, and `pr_selector`, and makes no `gh` call at all unless the repo is confirmed to be `origin`'s: a repo flag, `GH_REPO=`, `GH_HOST=`, or PR URL naming anything else, a branch or unreadable selector, or two merges in one command all stay silent. The whole command must be plain `gh pr …` calls, so a `cd`, `pushd`, `export`/`declare`/bare `GH_REPO=` assignment, prefix, subshell, group, wrapper, or any other command around the merge also stays silent, as do `--help`/`-h` and a PR whose `mergedAt` is more than 10 minutes old (a re-run must not close issues reopened since). The create check stays silent when stdout carries two different PR URLs (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#759)
- **Three test-only races are closed; no shipped behavior changes.** Two `persist_plan` nudge-composition tests called `persist_and_nudge` without the shared metrics lock, so their placeholder `plan-links.jsonl` rows landed in whichever `CADENCE_METRICS_DIR` a locked sibling had set, or in the live ledger; they now run under `with_scratch_metrics_dir` (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1062, cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#768).
- **`closed_stdout_covers_doctor` accepts both legitimate outcomes.** It passes on death by SIGPIPE or on `doctor`'s clean exit 2 for the missing manifest, and still fails on a panic, a `Broken pipe` complaint, any other exit code, or a failopen row. The file's closed-stdout pipe is now close-on-exec, so a sibling test's `fork` can no longer carry a live reader into its child and turn the expected SIGPIPE into exit 0 (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1061).
- **The synthetic-panic seam now guarantees a measurable span.** `a_panicking_check_fails_open_and_dispatch_survives_it` relied on the parent stalling stdin, which loses under load when the child starts after the stall; the debug-only `CADENCE_TEST_PANIC=1` trigger now sleeps 5 ms so `log_timing`'s strict threshold always writes its row (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#540).
- **`guard-gh-write` names a working fix for an account-scoped `gh api` write.** A `user/…` endpoint has no owner in its path, so the write still blocks, but the hint no longer points at a `repos/<owner>/<repo>` form that does not exist for it: `user/keys` names `gh ssh-key`, `user/gpg_keys` names `gh gpg-key`, and any other `user/…` path says to ask the user (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#756)
- **`guard-gh-write` calls an unexpanded `--repo` value unresolvable instead of unowned.** `--repo "$1"` still blocks, but the hint no longer proposes `-R <owner>/$1`, which grafted an owner the caller never chose onto a shell expansion. It now asks for a literal `-R owner/repo` under the `gh-write-target-unresolvable` rule (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#757)
- **`guard-gh-write` offers `-R` in its fork block only for an owned remote.** It used to suggest `-R <upstream>` for an upstream off the allowlist, which the guard then blocked as an unowned target. An unowned remote now gets the path that works: the user runs that write themselves (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1032)
- **`guard-gh-write` judges a write against a `GH_HOST` exported earlier in the same command.** `export GH_HOST=evil && gh pr create -R <owned>/x` was checked against the default host while gh sent it elsewhere. The write must now be owned on every host the command may have left in place, since whether an earlier `export` or `unset` ran is unknowable. An `export` inside `eval` is followed. Forms it cannot resolve map to an unknown host that blocks: `declare -x` and its siblings, and any nameref. So does a variable name the shell builds at expansion time, whether in `export GH_HOS${X}T=…`, behind `env`, or as the name operand of `read`, `printf -v`, `mapfile` or `getopts`. An expansion in the value, an assignment inside `${…}` or `$((…))`, and an `eval` nested past three levels block the same way. Where the command position is not understood (a subshell, a function body, a case arm), a declaring or name-writing builtin anywhere in the segment next to a `GH_HOST` mention or a non-literal `NAME=` fails closed. A `trap` action is read like an `eval` string. An `eval` or `trap` whose command word comes from an expansion fails closed, so `eval "$(direnv export bash)"` in front of a `-R` write now blocks. So do a `source`/`.` fed by a heredoc that names `GH_HOST`, an `env -S` prefix, and a `BASH_ENV=`/`ENV=` assignment. A plain mention (`rg GH_HOST`, a commit message, a gh `--title`) changes nothing. A bare `GH_HOST=x; gh …` still changes nothing unless the variable is already exported (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#548)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` now blocks `dd if=.env`, and `git` commands outside a short list of metadata-only subcommands.** `dd if=.env`, `git diff --no-index /dev/null .env` and `git stripspace <.env` print the file and were allowed: `if=.env` is one token whose basename matched no secret pattern, and every `git` command was exempt. `dd`, `gdd`, `dcfldd`, `dc3dd` and a `busybox`/`toybox dd` applet now have their `if=` operand checked. `git` keeps its exemption only for subcommands that stage, move or name files (`add`, `rm`, `status`, `commit`, `checkout`, …) and for `log`/`show`/`diff` with `--stat`, `--name-only` or `--name-status`. `log` and `whatchanged` keep it only when every option is on a list of metadata-only options (`--oneline`, `--format`, `-n`, `-S`, `--stat`, …). The exemption is removed by a patch flag (`-p`, `-u`, `-U`, `--binary`, …), `status -v`/`commit -v`, `-F`/`--file`, `-t`/`--template`, any global `-c` or `--config-env`, a `GIT_*` variable assignment anywhere in the command, an option that runs a command (`rebase -x`, `--exec`, `--upload-pack`, `--receive-pack`, `clone -u`), with git's unique-prefix spellings (`--fil`, `--patc`, `--exe`) counted as the full option, or an input redirection from a secret file. The command strings in those options, and in `GIT_*` values, are scanned as commands. Any other `git` command with a secret-file operand now blocks, including `git column`, `git config -f .env`, `git grep --untracked` and `git blame .env`. A `git diff`, `git show`, `log -p`, `stash show -p` or `status -v` in the same command as any secret-file name also blocks (`git add -N .env && git diff`), Content printers also include `grep`, `cat-file`, `format-patch`, `archive`, `blame`, `checkout-index --stdout` and `merge-file -p`. `git stash show -p` with untracked files switched on (by flag or `stash.showIncludeUntracked`), `git show stash@{0}^3`, and `git grep --untracked --no-exclude-standard` always block. Residual: `stash.showIncludeUntracked` set in an earlier command is not seen. Residual: the same pair split across two separate commands is not seen. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#850)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` now blocks a secret path built by a quoted command substitution.** `cat "$(echo .env)"` and `cat "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.env"` were allowed: the quoted substitution stays one token with whitespace in it, which the guard skips as prose. When the text outside the substitutions has no whitespace, the guard now replaces each substitution with what it can be shown to print (`echo`, `printf`, `true`, `ls`, `realpath`, `basename`, `dirname`, `find`, including nested and multiple substitutions) and classifies the result. Unquoted backtick runs (`` cat `echo .env` ``), quoted parens and escaped backticks inside a substitution, nesting past the depth cap, and more substitutions than the combination cap are all handled. Anything still unbalanced fails closed when its raw text names a secret, and an oversized word is judged by its literal tail. Heredoc bodies are ignored, a backtick or `$(` inside single quotes or a quoted heredoc is treated as text (only when the quoting is balanced), and prose around a substitution and a quoted multi-word `echo` are still skipped. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#815)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` now sees a `cd` behind a leading redirection, a wrapper's flag, or `sudo -D`.** `>/tmp/o cd /x && cat .envrc`, `command -p cd /x`, `time -p cd /x`, `sudo -D /x cat .envrc` and `sudo --chdir=/x cat .envrc` all change directory before the read, but the scan missed the change. The `.envrc` check then approved the clean loader in the starting directory while the shell read a different file. All five now block, as do an fd duplication or named fd in front of the `cd` (`2>&1 cd /x`, `{fd}>/tmp/o cd /x`), `sudo -i`/`--login`, a `sudo` behind another prefix (`nice -n 5 sudo -D /x`), and `su -`/`-l` or `runuser -l`. These are checked before a `-c` string is split off, and the `-c` string passed to `su`, `runuser`, or a shell behind an unparsed `sudo` flag is now scanned as a command, so `su - root -c 'cat .envrc'`, `su -lc '…'`, `sudo -D /x sh -c 'cat .envrc'` and `bash -o posix -c '…'` block too. Nested command strings are re-scanned within a fixed budget per command, and a command that exceeds it blocks if it names a secret file anywhere, so deep `su … -c` nesting cannot stall the hook past its timeout. A command over 16 KiB, or one whose scan runs past one second, skips the structured scan. It then blocks whenever it names a secret file anywhere, prose included, once quotes and backslashes are removed. `c\d /x` was already fixed. `command if cd …` is a bash syntax error that runs nothing. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#832)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` caps the size of the `.envrc` it reads to decide on a loader.** A `.envrc` symlinked to a FIFO hung the hook indefinitely, and one symlinked to `/dev/zero` grew it without bound, because both the Bash and Read arms used an unbounded read. Both now go through the shared capped reader, which rejects non-regular files and anything over 1 MiB. That reader now opens the file non-blocking (and without taking a controlling terminal) and checks the opened handle, so a path swapped for a FIFO after the first check is still refused. A rejected file stays blocked. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#818)
- **`prevent-secret-leaks` exempts only the exact `--file` argument of a `forgectl env` call.** The exemption matched the argument's text, so any other copy of the same string in the call was exempt too: `forgectl env keys --file .env .env` passed a second `.env` that forgectl was never checked against. The guard now exempts by argument position, the way the jq filter exemption does. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1053)
- **`docs/hooks.md` now catalogs every registered hook, and a test keeps it that way.** Twenty-two hooks had no row, five of them in `## session` (`warn-branch-intent`, `end`, `backstop-record`, `backstop-warn`, `persist-plan-approval`) and the rest across `cadence`, `guardrails`, `rules` and `metrics`. The README counted 15 `cadence` and 26 `guardrails` hooks against 16 and 28 registered, and the `heartbeat` row claimed a PostToolUse wiring it no longer has. `docs_hooks_catalog_matches_registry` and `readme_namespace_counts_match_registry` now compare both files with `HOOKS` on every `cargo test`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#789)
- **`nudge-upgrade-after-push` no longer fires on text that only mentions a push to main.** It matched the substring `git push` anywhere in the command, so a heredoc, an `echo`, or a commit message containing `git push origin main` produced "cadence-hooks was just pushed to main" from a feature branch. It now reads pushes through the same shell walk the push guards use, judges a refspec by the branch it writes on the remote (`feat:main` counts, `main:feat` does not), resolves a `HEAD`/`@` remote side to the current branch, counts `--all`/`--mirror` as a push to main, follows `git -C`, and stays silent on `--dry-run` and deletes. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#893)
- **`session plans` scans every plan file and says when it could not read one.** It reused the SessionStart scanner, which reads only the newest 50 files by mtime and drops listing and read errors, so an older blocked plan went missing and a failed read printed `no in-flight plans` at exit 0. It now scans every `.md` file in `docs/plans/`, prints what it found, and exits 2 with the scanned root and each failed entry on stderr. The SessionStart pointer keeps its cap and its silence, and says "At least N" when the cap cut candidates. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#968)
- **`migrate-config` writes `cadence.json` atomically and reports a partial run instead of discarding it, and the `redaction` section is documented.** The merged file is written to a fresh temp file and renamed into place, so a crash cannot leave it truncated and a symlink swapped in after the pre-write check is replaced rather than followed. The temp file takes the existing file's mode before any content is written. A legacy-file rename that fails after the write no longer throws away the report: the command prints what it wrote and renamed, names the file it could not rename, and exits 1. `docs/configuration.md` now documents `originAudience`, `categories`, `additionalPatterns` and `allowlist`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#314)
- **`persist-plan-approval` no longer mints a `-2.md` copy when the session saves the approved plan by hand.** An approve-and-clear session whose first tool call is a `Write` of the plan fires the injected-prompt arm on that Write. The hand copy carries its own frontmatter and often a ticked step, so it no longer hashed to the approval, and the ladder wrote a second copy and pointed the nudge at it. The hook now adopts the file that Write saved as the persisted plan, under any name, when all of these hold: the path (a relative one resolved against the payload `cwd`) is a regular `.md` file, not a symlink, directly in the plans dir; and its content is the approval once frontmatter is stripped, checkboxes unticked and blank runs collapsed, or it carries the approval's first heading line verbatim. It then writes nothing new, never rewrites the hand copy, records the ledger row against it, and names it in the nudge. A Write of unrelated content, a Write outside the plans dir, and a Write through a symlink are not adopted, and the plan is persisted as usual. The re-fire nudge for a plan this session already persisted now says whether that copy is still on disk, and on a same-session re-approval it keeps the driver-announcement reminder and the model check. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#861)
- **`enforce-worktree` sees a `cd` behind `builtin`, `command`, an assignment word, `time`, `!` or a redirection.** The guard counted a segment as a directory change only when its first word was `cd`, so from a linked worktree `builtin cd <primary> && git commit` was judged at the worktree and allowed while the commit landed in the primary. The `cd` word is now found behind any of those, and an escaped `\cd` or quoted `"cd"` counts too. `env cd`, `exec cd`, `nohup cd`, `\time cd` and `command -v cd` are still not a cd: each runs an external `cd` in a child process. A cd behind an assignment to `CDPATH`, `HOME`, `OLDPWD` or `PWD` is left unresolved, because the assignment changes where it lands. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1057)
- **`enforce-worktree` follows a `cd` only in a plain command, and judges every other command from every directory it names.** From a primary checkout, `(cd <worktree>); git commit`, `cd <worktree> && cd - && git commit`, `cd <glob> && git commit`, `cd <missing>; git commit` and `cd <worktree> | true; git commit` were all allowed while bash committed in the primary, and four rounds of modelling bash scoping each opened new holes. The guard now uses an allowlist, like the `$HOME` one from #1018. A command is plain when its segments are joined only by `&&`, `;`, newlines, or a pipe between two commands that are not `cd`; it has no subshell, group, function definition, command or process substitution, parameter expansion other than `$HOME`, compound keyword (`if`, `for`, `case`, …), `eval`, `source`, `.`, `enable`, `alias`, `trap`, `pushd`, `set` or variable-assigning builtin, including behind `command [-p] [--]` or `builtin [--]`; no `cd -P`; no escaped `\<`, and no escaped quote anywhere in a command with a heredoc (`<<"E\"F"` ends at `E"F`), which core's heredoc reading does not model (a command like that is also read line by line, and quote-blind, on the union path); its only redirections are to `/dev/null`, `2>&1`, a heredoc, or input from an existing file; and every `cd` has one literal target (or is `cd -` after an earlier `cd`). A multi-line `-m "$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF)"` message with a quoted delimiter still counts as plain, but only where bash would run that substitution (not in single quotes, a comment or another heredoc's body), only when no body line starts with the delimiter and has a `)` anywhere after it (bash 5.2 ends the heredoc there and runs the rest of the line, so `EOF cd <p> && git commit)` is code; such a command is read line by line on the union path, with the code after the delimiter split off), and only as the value of `-m`/`--message`/`-F`/`--file` on `git commit`, `tag` or `merge`, or of `--body`/`--title` on `gh`; used anywhere else (a `cd` target, a `-C` value, the command word) it makes the command non-plain, and the union path treats the commit as unreadable. A control character other than newline and tab, or Unicode whitespace other than space and tab (a `\r`, NBSP, VT, FF), makes a command non-plain, since bash does not split words on it. A plain command is walked in order: a `cd` into a missing directory, or one after `&&` behind another command, keeps the pre-cd directory as a candidate once the `&&` chain ends, so `git worktree add <p> && cd <p> && git commit` still passes. A `cd` whose lexical target does not exist while its raw path does (`cd link/../x` with `link` a symlink) is unreadable on both paths, since bash's `cd` then falls back to the physical path. The walk keeps each directory normalized and caps its fallbacks, so a long `cd sub && cd .. && …` chain no longer grows the path it stats (100 KB took over a second, 200 KB over four, against a 4 s fail-open deadline). Every other command is judged from the session cwd and every directory any `cd` or `pushd` in it names, with `-C`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree` resolved from each; it blocks if any is a primary checkout, and if a `cd` cannot be read (a variable, a glob, `cd -`, `popd`, `eval`, `source`), it also blocks from a linked worktree. When such a block follows a `cd` into a linked worktree, the message says the command's shape could not be followed and suggests `git -C <worktree> commit`, which passes in every shape. A commit inside `if`/`for`/`while` bodies, and one behind a redirection anywhere in the command (`>/dev/null git commit`, `git 2>&1 commit`, `{fd}>/dev/null git commit`) or a leading `!`/`time -p`, is now seen. A word whose `<`/`>` is quoted (`git -C 2'>'x commit`) is no longer dropped as a redirection, which had turned `commit` into `-C`'s value; the same fix applies to core's push-target parsing. `(cd sub && make) && git commit` from a worktree still passes; `(cd <worktree> && git commit)` or `cd <dir> || exit; git commit` from a primary now blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1058)
- **`enforce-worktree` consumes a git global's value together with its flag.** The walk to the `commit` subcommand decided whether a word was a value by looking back at the previous word, so in `git -c -C commit` it read `commit` as the value of `-C` and never reached the subcommand. git rejects that command, so it could not redirect a real commit; the walk now matches core's push parser. It also reads a flag and the subcommand after the shell's escape removal, which closes three live misses: `git -\C <primary> commit` and `git --git-d\ir=<primary>/.git commit` from a worktree, and `git \commit` in a primary, were all allowed. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#885)
- **`enforce-worktree`'s block message now tells you to dismiss the repo it judged.** `dismiss-enforce-worktree` snoozes the repo it runs in, so after a `cd <other-repo> && git commit` or `git -C <other-repo> commit` block, the suggested command run from the session cwd snoozed the session's repo, printed a success line naming it, and left the block standing. When the judged repo differs from the one the shell starts in, or the shell starts in no repo, the suggested dismiss now carries `--repo <judged repo>`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#758)

## [0.108.0] - 2026-09-28

### Changed

- **`doctor --quiet` reports only blockers at session start.** A blocker is a finding that means a wired hook is not running: a shell-expansion error; a wiring that names a subcommand or namespace the binary lacks, in a plugin `hooks.json`, in `settings.json`, or in a plugin removed upstream; a `group` entry the binary cannot parse or run; a hook whose CLI is not on `PATH`; a plugin that ships hooks with no `enabledPlugins` entry; or a pinned cache dir that is missing or empty. A plugin set to `false` never blocks, and neither does a project-scope install pinned for a different project. The quiet route prints one fixed envelope with counts and exits 2 only on errors. Hook latency, stale telemetry, orphaned cache dirs, and identity findings no longer print there; a full `cadence-hooks doctor` run reports them, and `cadence:outro` makes that run at session end. The old once-a-day nag hashed every warning's text, and the hook-latency warning embeds rolling counts, so it re-fired most sessions and asked for a triage of findings nobody could fix at session start. The daily gate now keys on each blocker's plugin and command, which carry no counts.
- **Errors and configuration errors now reach session start.** `doctor --quiet` wrote them to stderr, which the documented SessionStart wiring discards, so the one class that should interrupt a session never did. The envelope goes to stdout; the detail stays on stderr.
- **`doctor --quiet` discloses only switches that are really off.** It prints the `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` banner and the `Disabled via CADENCE_DISABLE:` line. Refused, moot, and unrecognized `CADENCE_DISABLE` entries switch nothing off, so they appear only in the full `doctor` report; `guardrails enforcement-status` reports refused protected-guard disables at session start.

## [0.107.0] - 2026-09-28

### Changed

- **Folder trust is the documented boundary.** `docs/configuration.md` and the `bypass.rs` module docs now say that a trusted repository's settings `env` block can switch off every guard, including through `CADENCE_HOOKS_BIN` and `PATH` in the plugin wrapper, and that `enforcement-status` makes `CADENCE_BYPASS` and refused `CADENCE_DISABLE` visible without defending against a hostile trusted repository (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1031, closed not planned). The environment-variable table gains every variable that changes a guard decision, with the ones that weaken a protected guard marked.

### Removed

- **`guardrails guard-rm` and `guardrails guard-rm-liveness` are gone.** The path-aware recursive-delete guard and its SessionStart liveness check are deleted from the binary, with their registry rows, clap subcommands, tests and docs (cameronsjo/cadence-ecosystem#582). The `cadence-guardrails` plugin stopped calling them in cameronsjo/cadence#1479. **Other installs lose `guard-rm`:** recursive deletes now rely on auto mode's classifier, `permissions.deny` rows, Claude Code's own cwd-scoped delete guard, and `trash-guard` inside an Obsidian vault. Sessions in `bypassPermissions` mode get no hook-level delete check. A plugin pin older than cameronsjo/cadence#1479 still calls the removed subcommands; the wrapper sees "unrecognized subcommand" and exits 0, printing its once-a-day "binary is stale … Guards are NOT running" notice until the plugin updates. That notice is wrong in this case (only the retired checks are inert) and it uses up the day's shared fail-open notice. `doctor` may list those calls as "unknown subcommand(s) invoked by hand" until they age out of the metrics window.
- **`guard-rm-liveness` leaves `PROTECTED_GUARDS` and `BYPASS_EXEMPT_HOOKS`.** `enforcement-status` is now the only bypass-exempt hook. A stale `CADENCE_DISABLE=guard-rm,guard-rm-liveness` names no hook, disables nothing, and prints `Named in CADENCE_DISABLE but not a hook` in `doctor`.

### Fixed

- **`guard-body-budget` no longer calls "this session" narration when it names a session mechanic.** A body line like "the `session declare` record for this session" drew a nudge to "say what changed instead", though the phrase named the session a hook runs in. `for this session` and `of this session` are now exempt on a line that names a session mechanic in a code span. "In this session I fixed X" still fires. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#994)

## [0.106.0] - 2026-09-28

### Added

- **`doctor` warns when the deployed cadence rules differ from the pinned plugin's copy.** `/cadence-groundwork:initializing-cadence` copies the `cadence` plugin's `rules/cadence-rules.md` to `<config dir>/rules/cadence/cadence-rules.md` once, and nothing refreshed it after a plugin update, so sessions kept loading rules the installed plugin no longer ships. `doctor` now compares the deployed file with each user-scope `cadence` install's copy and warns (exit 1) when none matches. The warning says the files differ, not which is newer, and its fix names both paths for a `diff` first, then says to copy the pinned file over or re-run the initializer, and to update a dotfiles manager's source too so its next back-sync does not revert the copy. A deployed file without its `managed by cadence` line is treated as the operator's own and never checked. `doctor` reports only; it does not rewrite the file. (cameronsjo/cadence#1339)

### Fixed

- **A busy session stays live in the session registry, so `doctor --prune --apply` no longer deletes plugin dirs under it.** Since the per-call `session heartbeat` hook was unwired, only `session guard` touched a session's registry record, and only on `git checkout`/`switch`/`add`/`commit`. A session doing anything else for 30 minutes read as stale, and the prune gate proceeded with six sessions open. `session persist-plan-approval`, which already runs on every `PostToolUse`, now refreshes the record when the machine-wide mirror copy is older than a third of the stale window: one `stat` on most calls, no git and no extra process. The prune gate still cannot see a session idle at the prompt for 30 minutes, one whose cwd is outside a git repo, or one with `persist-plan-approval` switched off by `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` or `CADENCE_DISABLE`; close sessions before `--prune --apply`. A registration-audit test now fails if `persist-plan-approval` stops running on every `PostToolUse`. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#902)
- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` checks the PR gh actually flips.** It took the repo from `origin` and read the PR number with a whitespace scan. So `gh pr merge 511 -R other/repo` checked PR 511 in origin's repo, `-R other/repo 511` read no number at all, and a fork's flip checked the fork instead of the upstream gh resolves. It now reads `-R`/`--repo` in any position, reads PR URLs (whose repo wins) and branch selectors, and lets gh fill in the default repo from the session cwd (a leading `cd x &&` is not tracked). An empty `--repo=` counts as no repo, as gh reads it. Because the hook runs before the command is approved, it sends no request to a host the command names unless that host is `github.com` or `origin`'s host: a PR URL, `-R HOST/OWNER/REPO`, or URL-shaped selector on any other host stays silent. gh is given the repo the hook parsed, never the `-R` value as typed, and a `-R` value in git-URL form (`git@…`, `https://…`) or with characters outside a plain owner/repo set also stays silent. Unnumbered flips (`gh pr ready` with no argument) are now actually checked: the old lookup passed `-R` with no selector, which gh refuses, so the hook stayed silent every time. This corrects the 0.104.1 entry's claim that a flip without a number "still resolves it first". A flip with no number, or with a branch name, now costs two `gh` calls (a `gh pr view`, then the review query); a numbered one still costs one. A repo flag with no selector, disagreeing repo values, or an unreadable selector stays silent. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1028, #920)
- **`warn-alias-parsing` reads the command inside `$(…)`, backticks, and subshells, and prints once per command.** It split a command at `|` before it saw any substitution, so `f=$(command ls | head -1)` read `f=$(command` as the producer and nudged about `ls` despite the `command` prefix, while `f=$(ls | head -1)` and `(ls | grep x)` stayed silent. It now judges the command after the innermost opener still open in each pipe stage: the first form is silent and the other two nudge. The plugin wires this check once per aliased tool, so a command naming several of them printed the same nudge once per match; it now prints once. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1029)
- **`doctor`'s hook-latency finding no longer drops hooks with too few runs to rate.** The summary listed at most four hooks, and hooks with at least 20 runs rank first, so four rated slow hooks pushed out a hook that was cancelled on every one of its five runs. Hooks with too few runs now get their own "Too few runs to rate" clause. The finding also says "N of M recorded runs slow", since Claude Code records the runs that produced output, not every invocation. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1027)

## [0.105.0] - 2026-09-28

### Added

- **`guardrails enforcement-status` reports at session start when the guards are switched off.** It prints one line of context when `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` is set (every guard is off, the protected ones by name) or when `CADENCE_DISABLE` names a protected guard (the disable was refused, and they still run). A normal session gets nothing, and so does a `CADENCE_DISABLE` naming only unprotected hooks or the status checks themselves, such as the estate's standing `guard-rm,guard-rm-liveness`. A repository's checked-in `.claude/settings.json` can set either variable, and until now only `guard-rm-liveness` reported the bypass at session start. This check takes over that job ahead of `guard-rm`'s retirement. Like `guard-rm-liveness`, it is protected from `CADENCE_DISABLE` and exempt from `CADENCE_BYPASS`, and it never blocks. The report names guards only from the built-in protected list, never the raw environment value. The plugin wiring ships in a following `cadence-guardrails` change. (cameronsjo/cadence-ecosystem#582)

## [0.104.1] - 2026-09-27

### Fixed

- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip` takes half as long and sees a PR's newest reviews.** It made two `gh` calls in sequence on every `gh pr ready` and `gh pr merge`, about 0.4s each, which made it the slowest hook in the suite (median 1.1s over a week of session logs). One GraphQL request now fetches the head, the author, and the reviews, and a flip that names its PR number takes about 0.5s instead of 1s on the same PRs with the same verdicts (one without a number still resolves it first). The old reviews request returned the first 30 reviews, oldest first, so on a PR with more than 30 reviews a clean marker on the current head was never read and the hook nudged anyway. The request now asks for the newest 100.
- **`doctor` names the hooks that are slow, not the one that runs most.** Its hook-latency finding ranked hooks by the number of slow runs, so `guard-rm`, which runs on every Bash call, always came first: 2% of its 39,000 runs were slow, the lowest rate of any hook, and those came from machine load shared by every hook. The finding now ranks by the share of runs that were slow or cancelled, among hooks with at least 20 runs, and prints each as "N of M runs slow".
- **`doctor` no longer tells operators to file an issue for a panic a shipped release already fixed.** It warned on any `reason:"panic"` row in the 7-day window, whatever binary version wrote it, so a panic fixed in one release kept warning "file an issue" until the old row aged out, and the same fixed bug was filed three times. `doctor` now checks whether any panic row in the window carries the current binary version. A panic only from another version reads as a Note saying no action is needed and points at the CHANGELOG between that version and the current one. A panic on the current version still warns, and now says to search open issues for the current version's last error before filing, even when an older binary wrote a newer row. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1007, #956, #917)
- **`guard-gh-write` names a repo-less endpoint instead of offering an impossible fix.** `gh api /markdown` (and `/rate_limit`, `/meta`, `/emojis`, `/zen`, `/octocat`) has no owner in its path, but a write to it was refused with "use `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/…`", which no form of that endpoint can satisfy. The write still blocks; the message now says the endpoint has no owner to check and to do the work locally or ask the user. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#971)
- **`guard-sops-decrypt` says where its override is read.** The block offered `CADENCE_ALLOW_SOPS_DECRYPT=1`, but an inline `CADENCE_ALLOW_SOPS_DECRYPT=1 sops …` prefix does nothing: the hook reads Claude Code's environment, not the command's. The message now points at a personal settings `env` block (`.claude/settings.local.json` or `~/.claude/settings.json`, never the shared `.claude/settings.json`, which would turn the bypass on for every teammate) and says it takes effect next session. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#975)
- **`lint-plan-shape` says only the `Panel:` line blocks.** Its block called every missing template stanza "mandatory", though only `Panel:` blocks and the rest draw a nudge. One session padded an advisory answer into a full implementation plan to satisfy it. The block now says `Panel:` is the one stanza that blocks, lists any others as advisory only, and notes a plan with nothing to implement needs only the `Panel:` line. The nudge marks its list as advisory too. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1019)

## [0.104.0] - 2026-09-25

### Fixed

- **`guard-rm` asks on a delete behind `sudo`, `doas`, `su`, `timeout`, and other exec runners.** `sudo rm -rf ~` and `sudo -u me rm -rf ~/Documents` were a silent allow. The segment led with the runner, which is not a transparent prefix, so no delete verb was ever examined. A segment led by a listed runner (`EXEC_RUNNERS` in `guard_rm.rs`: privilege tools such as `sudo`, `doas`, `su`, `runuser`, `pkexec`, and wrappers such as `timeout`, `watch`, `flock`, `firejail`, `busybox`) now asks when it mentions a deletion. That includes a script passed as one quoted word, as in `su -c 'rm -rf ~'` or `sudo -s '…'`. The runners' flag grammars are not parsed, so the guard never judges a target behind one: `sudo rm -rf /tmp/x` asks too. A runner in front of anything else stays silent, and `sudo sh -c 'rm -rf ~'` still blocks. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#887)
- **`verify-pr-autoclose` closes a PR's issues only after GitHub reports the PR merged.** It ran after every `gh pr merge`, including one that failed on a draft PR, a red required check, or a ruleset, and one that only queued auto-merge. It then closed the issues the PR claims to fix, though the work had not landed. The merge-path `gh pr view` now also reads `state` and `mergedAt`, and the hook does nothing unless the state is `MERGED` and `mergedAt` is set. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#1004)
- **`doctor` no longer reports a hand-typed unknown subcommand as a hooks.json skew.** A session that guessed `cadence-hooks session list` left a `version_mismatch` row, and `doctor` folded it into the skew warning with a grep that could never match. `doctor` now checks each named invocation against the installed plugins' hooks.json and the user and project `settings.json`. Invocations none of them wire become a note (`unknown subcommand(s) invoked by hand`), which prints in a full run but never nags in `--quiet` and never changes the exit code. Wired invocations keep the warning, and the warning's grep names only them. When the list holds four pairs (its cap), the warning stays, since an unnamed pair could be the wired one. Only the current project's settings are read, so a pair wired in another project's or in managed settings shows as a note. Without an installed-plugins manifest, the old claim stands. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#917)
- **`markdown-lint`'s printed commands paste as one argument.** The `Fix: markdownlint --fix <path>` line and the clamped-output recovery command named the path unquoted, so a space split it and shell syntax in it ran on paste. A path over 200 characters was also cut with an ellipsis, naming a file that does not exist. Both commands now single-quote the full, control-stripped path; the displayed filename keeps its 200-character bound. The clamp now runs on the findings alone and the `Fix:` line is appended whole, so a long path can no longer push it out of a clamped message. A path too long for the recovery hint's cap gets a hint that names the file just written instead; a path too long for the `Fix:` cap gets a path-less `Fix:` line. `shell_single_quote` moved from `doctor` to `core::display` so both share it. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#967)

## [0.103.0] - 2026-09-25

### Added

- **`cadence-hooks group <ns>/<hook>...` runs several checks against one payload in one process.** A Write used to start a dozen processes whose checks each decide in well under a millisecond. With one group entry per plugin it starts two: 14.6 → 6.0 ms wall and 45 → 11 ms CPU per Write, and 8.2 → 4.9 ms wall per Bash call (4-core Linux, release build). Members run in parallel, each with its own git budget, panic guard, `CADENCE_DISABLE` switch, and audit rows. Results merge the way separate processes would: any block exits 2 with every blocker's message. After the git budget plus one second the group emits what it has decided rather than being killed by the hooks.json timeout. `doctor` and the registration audit read group entries member by member. See `docs/hooks.md` § Grouped wiring. Adversarial security and code reviews found three ways a block could be lost in the first version (a shared git budget, a hung member, the first member fixing the event); all three are fixed and replayed as regression cases.
- **`plugin-hooks-skew.yml` takes an optional `runs-on` input.** It defaults to `ubuntu-latest`, so existing callers keep their current runner. A reusable workflow bills the calling repo, so a private caller can now run the gate on its own self-hosted label instead of paid hosted minutes.

### Changed

- **`warn-main-branch` no longer spawns `git` in the common case on macOS and Linux.** It read the branch and repo root through two `git -C` calls on every Edit and Write, which made it the slowest hook on the Write path (~24 ms per call, against a ~3 ms process floor). It now reads them from the on-disk `.git` through the shared `GitState` resolver, so it costs about the same as `--version`. Wherever that read could disagree with git, it asks git instead: a path inside `.git/`, a detached or non-branch HEAD, a reftable repo, a bare repo in the path, a repo owned by another user, or `GIT_DIR`-style discovery variables. A parity test pins the read to git's answer across those layouts, and the once-per-session marker keys are unchanged. Windows always asks git: there `canonicalize` spells the root `\\?\C:\…` while git prints `C:/…`, and the snooze marker is keyed on git's spelling.
- **The git probe deadline is per thread.** A standalone hook runs on one thread, so nothing changes there. It is what lets `group` give each member its own full budget.

## [0.102.1] - 2026-09-23

### Fixed

- **`persist-plan-approval` no longer writes a second copy of a plan when an approve-and-clear session resumes after a reboot.** The injected-prompt arm scans once per session behind a marker in the temp dir. A reboot clears that dir, while the resumed session keeps its id and its transcript still starts with the same implement-prompt. So the arm scanned again and persisted the plan again. The copy was keyed to the new day's date, so the same-stem hash check did not see the first copy, and it also missed any copy moved or renamed since. Before writing, the hook now checks `plan-links.jsonl` for a row with the same `body_sha256` and `child_session_id`. When one exists and no same-day copy is on disk, it writes nothing and nudges with the earlier copy's path. This covers both the approve-and-clear arm and a same-session `ExitPlanMode` re-approval. A plan deleted on purpose and approved again in the same session is not rewritten, and the nudge says to save it by hand. A same-day re-fire whose file is still on disk keeps its existing nudge, and a different session approving the same text still persists. Before 0.82.0 a ledger check keyed on body and machine did this job; the new one is keyed on body and session.
- **`nudge-polish-before-pr` looks up the polish marker in the checkout that owns the PR's branch, not the cwd's current branch.** A `gh pr create -R <owner>/<repo> --head <branch>` said "No polish recorded for this branch" even when `record-polish` had run for that branch, because the marker key, the branch diff, and the working-tree digest all came from the cwd. No parser read `--head`, and the operand scan stopped at the first flag value, so `gh pr create --title x -R o/r --head b` hid both flags. The check now reads, for **every** segment of the command that ships (so `gh pr create --head a && gh pr create --head b` checks both, and the worst result wins), the global and post-subcommand `-R`/`--repo` values, an inline `GH_REPO=`/`GH_HOST=`, and `create`'s `--head`/`-H`. The scan follows pflag's rules: `--flag=value` and `--flag value`, shorthand clusters such as `-fH <branch>` and `-fR<repo>`, and each subcommand's value-taking flags from `gh pr <sub> --help`, whose values are skipped, so `-t "-Hx"` is a title. It stops at `--`. Where it cannot tell (any head or repo after a flag it does not know, in the rest of that segment), the head or repo counts as unreadable and gets the advisory below, never a named branch. An exported `GH_REPO`/`GH_HOST` leaves nothing in the command and is not seen. Every repo value must name one of the cwd repo's remotes (any remote, not only `origin`), compared on `owner/repo`, with the host compared only when the value names one or an inline `GH_HOST=` sets it (a remote on an SSH alias host such as `github-work` matches on `owner/repo` alone, and `ssh.github.com` counts as `github.com`; the #881 `gh pr merge -R` own-repo check uses the same comparison). A `--head` resolves to the worktree that has the branch checked out, and the marker, the diff, and the digest all read that worktree, so the security-skipped, docs-scope, and stale-digest nudges still fire for a `--head` ship. When the command names a repo that is not a remote, a fork `owner:branch` head, a branch checked out nowhere, or two conflicting heads, the check gives a new advisory: "Can't check polish for this PR: <reason>. Confirm `/polish` (with its security arm) completed in the checkout that owns the branch." It never falls back to the cwd's branch and never gives the clean allow. A cwd outside any repo keeps the no-polish nudge. **Behavior change:** `gh -R other/r pr ready 12` used to be checked against the cwd's branch and now gets the advisory. `log-polish-nudge` resolves the same segments and targets and records `markerTarget` (`local`, `cannot_check`, `unknown`; the least-resolved segment's when they differ), with `markerPresent` true only when every segment's marker exists, so a ship the check could not look up is not counted as a nudged skip. Repo-value normalization also strips `.git` from a bare `owner/repo.git`, and a URL's trailing `/` is trimmed before its `.git`. The ledger's `branch` field and `gh pr ready <n>` resolution are unchanged (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#537, #631, #452 stay open). (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#995)
- **The no-polish nudge names the route for a polish that ran in another checkout or as reviewer subagents:** `cadence-hooks cadence record-polish --repo-root <checkout> --branch <branch> --arm security=ran|skipped ...`, run after the polish completes. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#973)
- **`tests/broken_pipe.rs` covers `doctor`**, the command behind the last recorded panic rows, with `HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` pointed at a temp dir. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#956)

## [0.102.0] - 2026-09-22

### Changed

- **CLAUDE.md's platform-baseline upkeep step replaces its never-built follow-up note with the CI backstop now in review, and says plainly that the backstop is not yet live.** The step had read "A CI automation for this bump is a named follow-up, not yet built" for months while `cadence_hooks.current_version` went six releases stale (0.90 through 0.95) — a stale baseline fails open (ADR-0001), so the drift nudge and `doctor` both went quiet instead of erroring and every session in that window was told it was current. The step now names where the bump is edited and how it lands (a `cameronsjo/cadence` checkout, through a PR, since a direct push to that repo's main is rejected), tells the releaser to read the current value before writing so two parallel releases cannot both bump it, and names the tracker the backstop files to. The backstop itself is written as pending rather than live: bumping at release time is the defense, and a job that notices a miss within a day never replaces it. The redundant restatement of the fail-open direction was cut — the paragraph three lines below already carries it. No code changed. (cameronsjo/cadence#1369)
- **`verify-pr-autoclose` checks referenced issues after 2 s and skips the rest of the wait when GitHub has already closed them all; the full wait and the set of issues it closes are unchanged.** The `gh pr merge` path used to sleep a flat `GH_AUTOCLOSE_WAIT_SECONDS` (default 10) before checking, while GitHub closed referenced issues 1-2 s after merge in every measured case. A ref still open at 2 s, or one whose state read fails, falls through to the remaining wait and the unchanged final check. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#882)

### Fixed

- **Piping any `cadence-hooks` command into a reader that stops early no longer reports an internal error.** `cadence-hooks doctor | head -1`, `cadence-hooks list | grep -q <name>`, or any consumer that closes the pipe printed `cadence-hooks: internal error (panic) … failed printing to stdout: Broken pipe (os error 32)` and recorded the run as a `"reason":"panic"` row in `failopen.jsonl`. `doctor` then reported those rows back as a defect worth filing an issue over, so the tool manufactured its own findings out of ordinary shell use. The cause is that Rust sets `SIGPIPE` to `SIG_IGN` before `main`, so the write returns `EPIPE` and `println!` turns that into a panic. `main` now restores the default disposition as its first statement, which covers every writer in the process — the several hundred `println!` sites across `doctor`, `list`, `manifest`, `try`, `configure`, `metrics`, and every check that emits hook JSON, plus any writer a dependency owns — rather than patching the sites found so far. A closed pipe is now a silent death by `SIGPIPE` (signal 13, which shells report as exit status 141), the same contract `cat`, `sort`, and `grep` follow; no stderr text and no ledger row. Write failures that are **not** a closed pipe are unaffected: the kernel raises `SIGPIPE` only for a pipe or socket whose reader is gone, so a full disk or an I/O error still returns an error from the write, still prints the panic notice, and still records a genuine failopen row. One narrow exception keeps `SIGPIPE` ignored: `cadence-hooks try` writes a payload to the hook's stdin, and a hook that exits before reading closes a pipe `try` owns both ends of — an outcome `try` has always treated as normal, and one that under a bare default disposition would kill `try` itself mid-diagnostic with no output. **Unix only.** Windows has no `SIGPIPE` and no process-wide disposition to restore, so the Windows binary still panics when its reader closes early; that half is tracked in cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#980 rather than guessed at here.
- **`warn-unreviewed-ready-flip`'s review-marker regex becomes a character-for-character translation of its other consumer, `poll-prs.sh`'s `CADENCE_MARKER_RE` in cameronsjo/cadence.** One contract, two parsers, and they accepted different sets of strings with nothing checking the gap. Three fields were looser here. `head=` took any hex length where the bash side and `cadence-forge:review-loop` § What "reviewed" means both say 40 ("the full 40-char SHA. Anything shorter never matches") — harmless on its own, because `is_reviewed` compares the captured SHA to the PR's full head for equality, so a truncated SHA never cleared the gate. The other two were not harmless: every separator was `\s+` or `\s*`, so a tab, a double space, a newline mid-marker, a missing space after `<!--`, or a missing space before `-->` parsed here and not there — meaning this guard read "reviewed" and went silent at `gh pr merge` on a marker the review loop's own poller never accepts. And `\d` is Unicode-aware in the `regex` crate, so `crit=`/`imp=` took non-ASCII digits the bash side's `[0-9]+` rejects. `\S+` for the reviewer field becomes `[^ ]+`, closing the one divergence that ran the other way (a tab inside the reviewer token, which `marker-shape.test.sh` conceded with "no fixture case" and which now has one). **The pattern alone was not enough**, and an adversarial re-review at the fixed head is what established it: `is_reviewed` matched the whole body after a `trim_start`, where the bash side matches `split("\n")[0]` untrimmed. So a marker behind a blank line or an indent cleared here and not there, and — because a negated character class in the `regex` crate matches `\n`, unlike `.` — `[^ ]+` on the reviewer field swallowed a newline and let a marker spanning two lines clear here while jq, handed one line, rejected it. Both ran the under-nudge direction, and identical regex text did not close either: what the caller slices bounds the accepted language as much as the pattern does. `is_reviewed` now matches the first line, untrimmed. The module doc's `head=<sha>` spelling is corrected to `head=<40-char SHA>`, the same omission cameronsjo/cadence#1270 fixed in `poll-prs.sh`'s header, and the nudge's short-head render moves off a byte slice so a non-hex `headRefOid` cannot panic it. Four tests pin the four properties, each verified red against its own staged break. Verified against eight live PRs: all seven carrying a real marker still read silent, and the one with no reviews still nudges. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#879)

### Security

- **`failopen.jsonl` rows no longer record payload-derived parse or panic text.** The ledger's `error` field was documented as carrying no payload, and it did. A parse row recorded serde's error message, and serde quotes the offending value: `{"cwd":424242}` on a check wrote `424242` to disk, and a string sent where `metrics log-subagent` expects the integer `duration_ms` wrote the whole string. A panic row recorded the panic message verbatim, and a formatted panic can embed runtime data. Parse rows are now built by `json_parse_failure` from the error's category, line, column, and the payload's byte length only. A `from_value` data error has no position, so it is recovered by re-parsing into the target type, on the error path only. Panic rows are built by `panic_row_error`: a `&str` message is a compile-time literal and is kept, a formatted `String` message is replaced with `panic message withheld (formatted, N chars)`, and the source location is kept. `.expect("…")` messages arrive from std as `String` even when the text is a literal, so they are withheld as well; the location still names the call site. The panic notice on stderr still prints the full message, since stderr goes back to the harness that sent the payload. New `tests/failopen_privacy.rs` drives all six paths end to end with a canary in the input and asserts one row with no canary. A debug-only `CADENCE_TEST_PANIC=formatted` mode produces the formatted panic. (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#959)

## [0.101.0] - 2026-09-19

### Added

- **Every hook message is now held under Claude Code's 10,000-character output cap by the renderer itself** (cameronsjo/cadence-ecosystem — hook output budget plan, Task 1). `display::clamp_hook_output` takes a message, a budget in **UTF-16 code units** (the unit the platform measures in, because it is JavaScript — an emoji costs 2 there and 1 to Rust's `chars()`), and an optional recovery hint. Under budget it returns the input untouched and borrowed. Over budget it keeps whole lines from the head and the tail with one `[hook-output-clamped]` marker line between them naming how many lines went; a line longer than the whole budget is cut at a char boundary rather than dropped. The marker is built and reserved first, and the head and tail budgets are computed from what it leaves, so `head + marker + tail` fits the budget by construction; the caller's recovery hint is capped at `MAX_RECOVERY_HINT_UTF16` (200) so a long one shrinks the kept text instead of truncating the tail off the end. The marker is a readability aid, not an attestation — it is plain text in a message that can already contain echoed tool output, so nothing about it is authoritative and nothing prevents a message from containing the same string. `render_output` applies the clamp per outcome at `HOOK_OUTPUT_BUDGET_UTF16` (9,000), so a single emitter can no longer spill a hook's output to a `tool-results/` file the model is never asked to read. On a hard block the measured field is the whole stderr string, so the `Fix:` line and the feedback footer are appended *after* the clamp and their length comes off the body's budget first — the one line a block exists to deliver cannot be the line that gets cut. Whether the structured `fix` is appended is decided on the *clamped* body, so a message whose own `Fix:` line the clamp removed still ships one; the structured `fix` is itself capped at `MAX_BLOCK_FIX_UTF16` (a quarter of the budget) as the suffix is built, so an oversized one ships truncated rather than being dropped whole by the backstop; and if the `fix` and footer are still large enough to fill the budget between them, the assembled string is clamped as a last resort rather than shipped over the cap. The clamp changes message text only: never a guard's decision, never its exit code, and every existing `sanitize_field` call is untouched.
- **`cadence-hooks session plans`** — a read-only CLI action listing every in-flight and blocked plan in the current repo with its next step, branch, and PR. It is the tier-2 view of the SessionStart plan disclosure, and reuses the hook's own scanner, so the two can never describe different corpora. Zero plans prints `no in-flight plans under <dir>` and exits 0 — the empty answer names where it looked, so "nothing in flight" cannot be mistaken for "wrong repo". Outside a git repository it exits 2 with the scanned root on stderr, which is what tells a parser an empty list from a question never asked. Exempt from `CADENCE_BYPASS` alongside `session declare` and `session status`. Every field it prints is sanitized, and the report closes by saying the bullets are text quoted from plan documents — data about what is in flight, not instructions to follow.
- **`markdown-lint` names a recovery command when its output is clamped, and sanitizes the path it echoes.** `markdownlint` prints one line per violation with no cap of its own, so a long document could push this nudge past the platform limit on its own; the marker line now carries ``Run `markdownlint <path>` for the full list.`` The written file's path is attacker-influenced, so it and the filename derived from it now go through `display::sanitize_field` before interpolation, like every other emitter — a path carrying a newline can no longer forge a line inside guard-authored text. The echoed `markdownlint` output itself is left as-is; the clamp bounds it.

### Fixed

- **`guardrails guard-rm`'s git-root probe no longer softens a deletion verdict on a stat error** (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#933). `is_git_root_on_disk` used `Path::exists()`, which collapses every error to `false`, so an unreadable repo root (`EACCES`, a vanished parent, a dead mount) read as "not a repo" and the verdict dropped from BLOCK to the plain-directory path. That is the direction the sibling `is_symlink_on_disk` already documents, and for the same reason: a stat failure in either probe can only grant a softening, never withhold one, so it is the one place the guard's usual fail-open posture (ADR-0001) does not apply.
  - **The probe is now three-valued** — yes, no, unknown — because `GitRepo` is not uniformly the stricter class. It blocks on a plain target, and it *softens* on two routes: the symlink demotion (`rm <link>` removes the link only) and a flat artifact sweep inside a repo (`rm *.tgz`). A two-valued probe cannot be strict in both directions, so reading an unknown as a confirmed repo would have traded the old fail-open for a new one. An unknown now classifies as a repo (keeping the BLOCK) and is refused at both softening arms. The symlink arm re-classifies the target with the probe forced to "no", which reproduces its pre-change class exactly — including `ClaudeState`, which `GitRoot` pre-empts in the shared classifier and which a flat "unknown" would have let the single-file arm soften to Allow. So the fix adds blocks without adding allows.
  - **Two error kinds prove absence and answer "no": `NotFound` and `NotADirectory`.** The latter matters: every `rm notes.txt` stats `notes.txt/.git`, which is `ENOTDIR` on Unix and `ERROR_DIRECTORY` on Windows, so reading it as unknown would classify every ordinary file deletion as a repo root. Every other kind answers "unknown".
  - **A dangling `.git` symlink is a repo again.** The `repo/.git -> /store/repo.git` bare layout with the store unmounted stats `NotFound` through `metadata`, so a real repo root read as an ordinary directory (true under `exists()` as well). The `NotFound` branch now re-asks with an `lstat`. A linked worktree's `.git` **file** still classifies as a repo; a directory is not required.
  - `guard-rm` is switched off estate-wide via `CADENCE_DISABLE`, so no live behavior changes until it is re-enabled.
- **`cadence-hooks configure --list` now reports the live session, not only `settings.json`.** It previously read `CADENCE_DISABLE` from the settings file alone and never consulted `CADENCE_BYPASS` or the environment's own `CADENCE_DISABLE`, so a hook disabled or bypassed from the session's own environment — the state the binary was actually running in — went unreported here while `cadence-hooks list` and `cadence-hooks doctor` described it correctly. **This supersedes the claim in `[0.99.0]` below** that `configure --list` "describes what is written in the settings file" rather than the live session, and it is the same false-reassurance shape as the two prior findings in that entry: `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` plus a settings-file `CADENCE_DISABLE=git-safety` printed `Refused (protected) — … these still run: git-safety` and `71 of 72 hooks active`, though the bypass had already switched the guard off. `configure --list` now renders through the same `cadence_hooks_core::bypass::resolve_from` resolver as `list` and `doctor`, reports the bypass banner when engaged, reports a bypassed entry as moot rather than refused, and attributes each entry to the source(s) that named it (`via settings.json`, `via environment`, or `via settings.json and environment`) — the two mean different things to fix, since an environment entry leaves no trace once the session exits. The interactive wizard is unchanged: it still reads and writes `settings.json` only, deliberately not the environment, because pre-selecting an environment-sourced name in the picker would let one confirmation persist a session variable into a committed file. Refs cadence-hooks#929 (item 4).

### Changed

- **The SessionStart in-flight-plan disclosure is now a pointer, not a list** (same plan, Task 1). It was a header plus up to 20 bullets, each carrying a plan's slug, `next:` step, branch, and PR — the largest single block the session-start hook emitted, and one that grew with the plan corpus. It is now one fixed-size line: `N in-flight plans in docs/plans/ (M blocked). Run `cadence-hooks session plans` before your first edit in this repo; …`. A session start states that something exists and where to look; the detail loads on demand. **Both counts are read from the scanned frontmatter's `status:`**, never by matching `[blocked]` in rendered text, so a plan whose `next:` prose merely mentions the word is not miscounted. No plan document's text reaches the line at all, which retires the `PLAN_SCAN_MAX_EMITTED_LINES` cap that existed to bound exactly that exposure — `render_block` is now the uncapped tier-2 renderer, reached only through the new subcommand. The uncommitted-plans line is unchanged and still rides the same disclosure.
- **Documented the project-scope settings `env` channel in the bypass resolver's module doc** (cameronsjo/cadence-ecosystem#567). A cloned repository's checked-in `.claude/settings.json` `env` block reaches hook processes, so it can set both switches for every session opened in it: `CADENCE_DISABLE` silences every unprotected hook, and `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` from the same channel silences even `PROTECTED_GUARDS`, since the bypass rule is reached before the protected-refusal rule and only `BYPASS_EXEMPT_HOOKS` survives it. The binary cannot tell a project-scope value from a user-scope one — it reads one merged process environment — so the module doc now records the exposure, corrects the old claim that `CADENCE_BYPASS` "cannot be left switched on in a settings file" (and its "per-session" and "one-session" siblings in `main.rs`, `docs/configuration.md`, and two notice strings: the `DisableRefused` stderr line and `guard-rm`'s block message), drops the "loud vs silent" contrast (both switches print one stderr line per affected invocation), and notes that `guard-rm` stays out of `PROTECTED_GUARDS` deliberately. The same channel writes every other `CADENCE_*` variable the binary reads; the doc names the ones that weaken a guard on their own, including `CADENCE_ALLOW_SENSITIVE_TERMS`, which downgrades a protected guard's block with no bypass banner, and `CADENCE_METRICS_DIR`, which relocates the `bypasses.jsonl` that downgrade is recorded in (cadence-hooks#963). Docs, comments, and two notice wordings; no verdict or exit-code change.

## [0.100.1] - 2026-09-15

### Fixed

- **Running the `guard-body-budget` test suite no longer appends rows to your real `failopen.jsonl`** (cameronsjo/cadence-hooks#946). Every `cargo test -p cadence-hooks-guardrails body_budget` wrote the same five `unmeasured` rows (`no-body-flag`, `unreadable-body-file`, `body-file-not-utf8`, …) into `~/.claude/metrics/failopen.jsonl`, because the unit tests reached `Check::run` without pinning `CADENCE_METRICS_DIR`. They now share a throwaway metrics root, the way the integration suite already did. That test-run noise is what #946 observed; **a heredoc's text was never scanned** — `detect_surfaces` already reaches `shell::command_segments`, which strips heredoc bodies. Four regression tests pin that: the reported heredoc shape detects no posting surface, a heredoc-written body file is still measured on the segment after the terminator, an unterminated heredoc keeps its text (ambiguity keeps), and a `--body` value quoting another `gh` command is measured once.
- **An orphaned grandchild can no longer hold a hook past its deadline.** `run_bounded_with` waited for the stdout pipe to reach EOF, which is not the same as waiting for the child: a backgrounded grandchild inherits the pipe's write end and holds it open after the child exits. Measured on both arms at a 1s deadline, a 20s orphan took **20.1s** (reported as a confident `Completed`) and 30.1s on the timeout arm. The drain is now bounded on every return path, and a drain that never sees EOF yields the new `GitSpawn::Truncated`, which carries the real exit status and the bytes read so far. A **success-status** truncation routes to `GitOutput::TimedOut`, so every fail-closed guard reads a partial answer as *no answer* — most degrade to a recorded fail-open rather than a block, and only the push-remote and worktree checks block there. A **non-zero-exit** truncation still routes to `GitOutput::Failed`, because the exit code is a complete answer, so "git said no" keeps blocking. Refs cadence-hooks#927.
- **`guard-rm-liveness` now survives `CADENCE_BYPASS=1` and refuses `CADENCE_DISABLE`** (cadence-hooks#927).
